"What?  From ol' Blevens?  No way!" said Misty.  "Just ask him.  You'll see."

Jane wasn't so sure.


~22~
A Little Time Off

Jane walked into the stock room of Blevens Department Store with no small amount of apprehension in her gait.  She took her anxiety pill but it didn't seem to help.

Dust in the air turned the morning rays of sunlight into nearly solid beams.

There, in the wide center aisle near the loading dock, stood Mister Blevens comparing a pallet of shoeboxes against a checklist on a clipboard.  He saw her approach but kept working without looking up.  He wiped his meaty thumb across a layer of dust covering the label of a shoebox that had, evidently, come over with the Mayflower.

"Yeah?" he asked with his typical non-existent politeness.

"Um, sir, I was thinking that, er, maybe..."

He kept working.

"Maybe I could have some time off?"

"Mmmm..." he said, as if it was an interesting tidbit of philosophy rather than a request.

Finishing his checklist, he stood and walked back towards his messy, overflowing desk near the rear entrance.

''Because, well, if I - I mean we - could only..." she fumbled on, following him.

He wedged his portly form behind the desk.  He thrust his filthy mitt into a jar of Peanut m&m's and palmed the whole handful into his mouth at once.  He didn't offer any to Jane, which was just as well, since she wouldn't have touched that filthy dirty candy for cash money.

Jane stood staring at the disgusting thing with a wrinkled up nose.  She shook herself out of the trance and forced her eyes to look back at Blevens.

Even though he seemed unmoved, Jane knew she had to keep trying.

"No, really.  I do, because this Bible Study I'm a part of is..."

He raised an eyebrow, looking up at her for the first time.  Possibly for the first time ever.

"Not the Moast Bible Study?" he asked, surprised.

She slowly nodded.

"How long?" he asked.

"A month or two." 

"Done." he said.

It made no sense.  Just the mention of the Bible Study seemed to change everything about everything.

She was about to thank him but had no chance to get a word in.

"But I want you here the INSTANT you get back!"  He stabbed at the desk with a stubby finger.  "I got things to sell, and you're gonna sell em!"

The old Mister Blevens was clearly not dead.  Only on hold temporarily.

 Jane made her way back out onto she sales floor.  Misty smiled knowingly at her.

"I don't get it." said Jane.

"Maybe you'll find this town has its share of secrets after all." she replied with a sly smile.

And that was the perplexing end of it.


~23~
Shuttle Bus

Jane's little car pulled into the familiar driveway of the Moast mansion.  It loomed an indistinct grey outline in the predawn twilight.  

Everyone else was already there, and so was an airport shuttle van.  The Bible Study crew were all busy piling their gear inside for their trip to the Santa Augusta International Airport on the far side of town, and then, onward to the four corners of the globe.

Jane had no idea where in the world she would wind up.  The Moasts had all that worked out, and weren't planning on divulging the information until they were all on their way to the airport.  The only hints she'd gotten were to dress for warm weather, and that she and Misty would be teamed up together.

Apart from that she could very well wind up anywhere on the map, which certainly magnified the excitement.  It was almost tangible.  It felt like electricity buzzing about her.

She pulled her car into the first empty parking spot at the end of the row.  

Next to her was Ms McCready climbing out of her bright red Ferrari.  She pulled a large and well-worn Navy sea bag out of the passenger seat.  As if she and the car didn't already seem like strange enough match, the bag was nearly as big as she was, but she slung it over her shoulder with a familiar sort of ease.

Jane suddenly felt ridiculously under-prepared with her one tiny duffel - barely larger than a gym bag.

She didn't bring a lot simply because she didn't own a lot.  After all, she'd been living off the few meager clothes she'd brought from Kansas.  Her entire wardrobe fit in a single cardboard box in her back seat.  For the trip, she upgraded the container to the duffel bag and added a swimsuit, sun hat, and a jumbo sized sunscreen bottle, but that was the end of what she could afford.

It wasn't much of a shopping spree, but she only had two paychecks under her belt, less the customary 'first and last month's' rent for her new apartment.  Add to that a costly muffler repair looming on the horizon and there wasn't much left.  As it was, she was becoming quite the ramen noodle connoisseur.

Ms McCready beat her to the back cargo doors of the van, so by the time Jane arrived it was full.  There wasn't even room for her tiny duffel among the other gear crammed in every nook and cranny.  The van was, as Dr Puttery eloquently pointed out, "Stuffed tighter than Wendell at an all-u-can-eat buffet".

Wendell, in rebuttal, asked if the shuttle van could stop at the drive-through so he could grab a McMuffin for the road.

Now that the van was tightly packed with cargo, the fun really began as everyone tried wedge themselves in as well. 

The passengers all began to climb aboard.  Even Dr Puttery came along, despite the fact that he didn't plan on leaving town.  Instead, he would return to his strange home and coordinate the effort from among the dust and obsolete machinery that he loved so much.  Right now he was tagging along to finally hear the secret orders just like everyone else.

Just as Jane placed one foot on the van's step about to enter, Michael Moast pulled her aside.  She stepped back down and turned to face him.

"You were planning on taking your camera, I suppose?"

She nodded.  She was a little surprised that he brought it up, but yes.  Of course she would bring a camera when taking an extended tour of the subtropical seas half a world away.  Why wouldn't she?

"Give it to me." he ordered.

Jane was downright aghast.  She wasn't going to be allowed to take pictures?  This was just unbearable!  Part of her even wanted to back out because of it, but the lure of adventure was too strong.  Still, her stomach sank at this miserable, unreasonable, rule that it seemed she must abide by.

It was like winning a lifetime supply of marshmallows but upon the one condition that you could never eat s'mores again.  It was unbearable! 

She reluctantly unslung the padded camera bag and handed it over to him.

Michael, in turn, handed it to a servant who Jane hadn't noticed a moment earlier.  "I'll hang onto it for you.  You can come back here and get it when you return."

Jane felt like she was leaving a part of herself behind.  Her shoulders sagged and she slowly turned to walk away.

"Wait!"  called Michael.  "Aren't you forgetting something?"

Was she?  No.  She had her small bag of clothes still in her hand.  All of the clothes she owned in the world, in fact.  What could she possibly be forgetting?

She turned around just in time to catch the cellophane-wrapped box that Michael thrust into her arms.  A brand new waterproof Nikon dive camera.  Good down to 150 meters. It even had adjustable buoyancy so it would stay floating at whatever depth you set it - almost acting like an underwater tripod.

Jane tried to read more off the package, but couldn't as he plopped on top of it a plastic shopping bag filled to the brim with new memory cards still in their blister packs.  It looked like he'd grabbed everything off the store rack and stuffed it in a bag.

"Try and document everything you can." he said.  "Many times the smallest clue can make or break a mission."

Jane's emotions were suffering from whiplash.  She just shifted gears without a clutch from 'sad resolve' to 'Christmas morning'.  She didn't know how to process it.  Finally, she threw her hands around Michael in a warm hug and issued a simple, yet heartfelt, "Thank you!"

"Who knows?" he said.  "Maybe God knew we needed a 'Photography Enthusiast' after all!"


As the van sped down the interstate on the way to the airport, Michael and Melinda laid out the plan of where each group was heading.

The concept was that voyagers were to split up and survey different regions of the world's oceans, concentrating on areas known for having plentiful coral reefs.  

Ms McCready was to take on a crew and work southward from the China Sea.  As she'd spent a lot of time in this area and knew it well, that would give her a solid advantage in that part of the world.  (Also, Michael had received strange rumors coming in of something else stirring.  Probably nothing, but either way, McCready was certainly the person to find out.)

The Moasts were to start at Australia's Great Barrier reef and work their way North.  Then they'd eventually rendezvous with McCready somewhere more or less in the lower Philippines or Indonesia. 

Wendell was to cover the Gulf of Mexico - a daunting task in its own right.  There were a huge number of reefs in the Caribbean, any one of which an ideal habitat for their mollusk friend.

Finally, the girls were to skirt the west coast of Africa for three weeks and then run up through the Suez Canal and down its East coast.  An impressive trek for a girl who, up until a month ago, had never ventured outside of Kansas.  

The last member of the party, Dr Puttery was, as usual, to coordinate global operations from the less-than-exotic abandoned fish plant in Santa Augusta California.  He liked that job.  

He was also responsible for pet-sitting the Moasts' dog Thor while they were out of town.  That job he could do without.

As the van began to veer off the interstate on the airport exit, there was one last item of business to be taken care of.  Michael Moast prayed over the mission as a whole and over each member individually that God defend them according to His Providence but more importantly, that they act according to His will in all that was to come.


~24~
Army of the Czar

Jack lay on his belly with a pair of binoculars on a hill overlooking the compound.  The dusty ground was cold and if felt like the ants were hosting a sock hop on his lower back where his shirt rode up.

He brushed them away as best he could and then re-focused the binoculars.

Tanks, trucks, helicopters... There was a lot of hardware moving around down there.  And then there were the fences and guard dogs and machine gun nests.  He guessed they didn't have to deal with a lot of annoying door-to-door salesmen.

It was a long, cold, painful ride across untold miles of Russian wilderness to get here.  It took so much time he'd fully suspected the place to be folded up and gone by the time he arrived.  They either should have run away or been reduced to a smoldering crater by now.  The coordinates he had given to Igor Rurick the police commander were legitimate.  That was quite a few days ago, so it was completely incomprehensible that a squadron of stealth Tupolevs hadn't bombed the rebel camp to pieces by this time.  Yet there it was, large as life.

Of course, the Russian jets could still just as easily show up any minute.  That cast a bit of a bad light on his scheme of sneaking into the compound.  

And why should he, anyway?  Sure, the mission was to find out who this group were, but once they were goners there wouldn't be anything left to worry about.

Something else drove him though -- something that drove all of the Bible Study in one way or another.  They were each ruled by a tragic and insatiable sense of curiosity.  If placed in a room with all the pleasures of life and one locked chest, they would never be satisfied until they saw what was in it.

Jack put down his binoculars and rubbed a crick in the back of his aching neck.  "Run away"?  Who was he kidding?  He'd go in there regardless of risk or reward.  As the saying goes, "Curiosity killed the cat."  

Jack somehow doubted that the cat was saturation-bombed to smithereens while sneaking into a secret insurgent compound.  But hey, either way...


~25~
Bermuda

Jane and Misty began unloading their gear and hauling it up the gangway to the luxurious yacht, the Sea Gypsy II.  The beginning leg of their mission would take them to the West coast of Africa.  But since the Michael had no boat in that area, the pair were forced to settle for the one berthed in Bermuda.

'Settle for' Bermuda?  Jane thought, amused.

To hear him describe it, their having to go to Bermuda was some sort of hardship - like a boring and lengthy layover on an airline flight.  

Now that she was here, Jane couldn't disagree more.  Pink sandy beaches, subtropical climate that always hovered between 60 and 70 degrees, adorable seaside shops in pleasant pastels...  She instantly loved the island and only regretted that they had to set sail so soon after arriving.

Once aboard the Gypsy, she convinced Misty to show her around before going back for the rest of their gear.

The boat was a hydrofoil, which meant that when it got up to cruising speed it had the unique ability to lift itself completely out of the water on legs attached to underwater airplane-like 'wings'.  Without having to push the ship's hull through the sea, the powerful engines could shoot the vessel through the water at a whopping 55 knots (or nearly a hundred miles an hour in landlubber terms)!

Even so, it could take the better part of a week to cross the Atlantic, so preparations to start out on the voyage were being made with all possible haste.

The boat was equipped with a set of headphones on the bridge that tied directly into a satellite com-link.  It could instantly connect the team with one another anywhere in the world and with their home base back in California.  

Like all of the Moast vehicles, it also had a tightly-encrypted moast_net wifi repeater.  This connection could perform a host of scientific and electronic stunts.  The one that was the girls' favorite was that it allowed their special pink phones to still text message eachother during their journey.  Even though there would be no cell towers available on the open ocean, they could still have their fun text-chatting.

(Misty'd had the foresight to install the moast_net decryption software on Jane's phone all those weeks ago at the California jewelry counter.  Now it seemed like a different world.)

"This is a nice boat." said Jane, impressed.

Misty replied, "That's for sure! You know she's even got her own sub?"

"A submarine!  You're kidding?"

"Nope.  Not a big one.  Basically a plastic bubble with an electric motor.  Still, I wish we could take it for a spin."

"Won't we?"

"I doubt it.  Most coral reefs are less than fifty meters deep.  A snorkel might cover some of the shallow stuff, but for the most part I suspect we'll be using SCUBA."

Misty thought about that a moment.

"You do know how to dive, right?" she continued.

Jane shook her head.  She was a good swimmer thanks to the community pool, but in middle of Kansas, SCUBA was a little hard to come by.

"It's okay.  I'll teach you." Misty smiled.

The two worked in silence for awhile longer, dashing back and forth bringing provisions and equipment aboard.

"So what's the deal with Atlantis, anyway?" asked Jane as suddenly as the thought came to her.  She was still curious over what had gotten such a big reaction back at the fish plant.  That curiosity finally got the better of her.

"Oh that." Misty replied with distaste.  "What a fiasco!

"About a year, maybe eighteen months ago, the group was sent out to investigate all these illegal historical artifacts suddenly showing up on the black market.  All kinds of stuff really: ship parts, sculptures, and whatnot.  What really got under Wendell's skin though was the huge amount of ancient Greek artifacts.  Weird ones though.  Seemed to be made of things the Greeks didn't even have access to.  Some of the pigments were even made from teoxihuitl turquoise, which he traced back to the Americas!

"It was insane, but all the pieces added up to one thing.  An extinct civilization derived from the Greeks, yet living in Atlantic waters.  Even Dr Puttery could see no other explanation.  So we spent months diving here and there, snooping around where we shouldn't, getting shot at by black market antiquities dealers.  All that stuff.  One dead end led to another until finally even those ran out.

"Mr Moast has never been a guy to give up on a hunt until he's got what he came for -- which is why he's almost always successful.  This time, even he reached his limit.  We kept going till were all too bone-weary to carry on.  And so, the lost aisle of Atlantis remains the stuff of myth and psychic hotlines."

She chose to end the story at that point because the Captain was approaching.  She called him aside and recommended a slight change in plans.

"Captain, my friend here has never been diving before.  I was wondering if we could postpone our departure 'till tomorrow morning?"

He sighed, but nodded.  "I hate to, but it makes the most sense.  Bermuda boasts some of the best SCUBA in the world.  There's no better place to learn than here.

There are some great reefs around Bermuda to practice on.  In fact, they say the original settlers of this island were shipwrecked sailors cast upon 'em." 

" 'Reefs', huh?  Any chance we'll get lucky and discover the mollusks here?" asked Jane.

Misty shook her head.  "Nah.  Finding a creature that poisonous so close to a major beach is pretty much out of the question.  Somebody would've run into one by now, and then it'd be all over the newspapers."

With that, the two bid the Captain goodbye and struck off in search of the nearest coral reef.

Upon arriving at the beach, Misty and Jane donned two sets of SCUBA gear borrowed from the Sea Gypsy's stock.

Misty taught Jane how all the equipment worked.  She taught her how to clear her mask properly and equalize the pressure in her ears.  

Misty, of course, also gave her all of the standard safety spiel about pressure decompression and air supply amounts and what one could and couldn't do while diving.  

Finally, there were also the hand signals you needed to learn because it is impossible to speak underwater without radio gear and a full face mask.  

It was a lot for one lesson, but Jane gobbled up all of it, anxiously wanting to get on to the fun part.

"You ready?" Misty finally asked.

That was a mighty dumb question.  Jane had spent the entire afternoon going out of her mind with readiness.

The two felt the sand squeeze between their toes as they waded out into the vibrant robin-egg blue surf.  Jane cleared her mask as she'd been taught.  Together, they slid beneath the surface and into another world.

It took a little getting used to and Jane was still quite the novice.  Even so, she immediately fell in love with the sport.

The two explored several miles of reef and even got to see a shipwreck before it was time to return to shore.

It was a beautiful day on the island and it went by all too quickly.

As soon as they set bare, dripping foot back on the deck of the Gypsy as the sun took its final plunge over the edge of the calm sea.


The next morning began early with the sound of the Captain of the Sea Gypsy barking out orders to his crew.  He was in a hurry to get underway to try to make up for lost time.  The boat was cast off from the dock.  The mighty engines rumbled to life.  Soon the powerful water jets began to suck the craft briskly through the sea with seemingly effortless ease.  

Butterflies filled Jane's stomach.  They were really doing it!  They were really going on an adventure to Africa!  It all seemed so fresh and magical.

With no small amount of wonder she surveyed the mystical Bermuda Triangle one last time as they picked up speed, heading for distant shores.  It was a wondrous place.  

The mystery of Atlantis may have escaped the group, but Jane was pretty sure that she'd solved the one about the Bermuda Triangle disappearances.  It was Bermuda for pete's sake!  She had a mind to disappear here as well someday.  (Though the only magic involved would be the call of a lounge chair on a cool beach.)


~26~
The Back Entrance

Both radar operators looked deeply into their glowing green display screens and listened intently to their headsets.  Jack was satisfied that they were sufficiently dedicated to their jobs to be functionally worthless at nearly everything else.  Including fighting.  (Hopefully.)

Having seen enough, he un-threaded the small fiberscope from the hole he'd been using it to peer through.  It was a hole that he, himself had silently punched in the thin underbelly of the enemy command trailer.  He'd gotten underneath the portable command post by pushing aside some sandbags just barely enough to squeeze through, and yet not enough to be easily noticed.  Again, hopefully.

The high-security trailer's door was, of course, tightly barred from the inside.  Since the two occupants were so busy, however, the idea of cutting a new door seemed a much quicker route to Jack.

Cautiously, he looked around his surroundings once more.  From the look of it, this thing was parked here fairly recently.  Fortunately there was not a spider in sight.  Jack wasn't afraid of spiders per sae, but he also didn't want to have his hands full with a cutting torch and have one decide to dance a jig on the tip of his nose.

Satisfied as he'd ever be, Jack struck up the torch.  He hadn't seen the need to bring one along with him, so this one was generously donated to the cause by the rebels' own automotive repair crew.  (Without their knowledge, of course.)

It was little more than a ten minute job to slice a ragged hole in the soft, un-armored belly of the trailer.  The cut out piece was still held in place by a few drips of slag.  Jack lay on his back underneath with both feet braced against it.

He pulled a pin on a gas grenade.  (Also donated.  This time by a guard who now lay face down in a ditch nearby.)  Shoving hard with both feet, the hole popped loose and he lobbed the grenade inside.  Then he rolled over several times and got his head close to his entrance hole for air.

As he waited for the knock-out gas to dissipate he had a lot of time to think.  The whole fiasco still didn't make any more sense than it did when Melinda Moast had given him the assignment back at Virginia Beach.  How could a bunch of rabble put together an operation of this magnitude?  And even if they could've somehow gotten the equipment and rallied together, how could they possibly have done so under mighty Russia's very nose?  After all, of all the things you could accuse Russia of, lack of surveillance was not one of them!

This brought him back to his earlier thoughts about the bombers.  Even if the KGB had somehow missed all of this going on originally, the police inspector would've given them a map straight to the rebels' front doorstep by now.

As uncanny as it seemed, there was only one conclusion that the clues all added up to.  These jokers must have one or more highly-placed moles within the Kremlin itself running smokescreen for them!

Jack stayed at the opening for a good forty five minutes longer, waiting for the air to clear.  When it finally did, he crawled back to the hole he'd cut and hoisted himself inside.

Both radar operators now lay slumped over their console.  Jack didn't stop to look.  He hoped to find something more interesting than radar inside this can.

The commanding officer's room was at the far front end of the trailer.  Jack tried the door.  Locked.

He may have had to borrow the torch, but a good set of lock picks was something he'd never leave home without.

Once inside he immediately saw the ranking reconnaissance officer flopped spread-eagle across his desk.  As insulted as Jack had felt by the patronizing of Igor Rurick, he had also learned a few things from him as well.  Instead of making for the file cabinets, he pushed the officer aside and examined what he'd been working on last.

There was one ledger with the times and designations of Russian aircraft movements and a second listing troop deployments.  The first few entries in each list were crossed off until they both reached approximately 1100 hours.  He must have been compiling them into a master list, but where was it?

Jack looked around for it, but the only other item on the desk was a note, "To My Darling Anastasia".  It was evidently the last thing he had been writing because the last word on it ended with a jagged line trailing off the page -- the last mark from a collapsing man's hand.

Jack heard something.  The nearly imperceptible scrape of metal against nylon webbing.  To most people the sound would have been meaningless.  To him it was as obvious a noise as the ringing of a telephone.  Somebody outside had just brought up a shoulder-slung weapon.  

They had the place completely surrounded!  He was trapped!

With no time to waste, Jack hastily crammed all of the papers into his pocket and made his move.  This whole 'getting trapped while snooping' thing was turning into a real bad habit.  Worse, these guys wouldn't be likely to have Igor Rurick's sense of sportsmanship if they caught him.


~27~
Pleasure Cruise

Jane lounged on a deck chair, staring up at the cloudless sky though dark, oversized sunglasses.  Except for a rusty, but cute fishing boat off on the far horizon, the sea was equally as empty, its ever-shifting surface shimmering in the noonday African sun.  

Earlier that day, like so many others, Misty had taken her below on a reef survey.  In the process, she was slowly teaching Jane the finer aspects of SCUBA diving.  The latter was becoming quite adept at the sport.

Meanwhile, they'd been performing their all-important scientific survey.  (It sounded a lot more impressive than it was.)  Their job was simply to swim around and enjoy the scenery of the reef.  All of the difficult work was done by a neutral buoyancy capsule filled with electronic chemical analysis gear.  Their role was merely to tow this around by a rope wherever their undersea adventures took them.  The sensors and computer would do the rest.

Results were continually delivered back to a specially-encased 7-inch display the size of a small notepad.  Misty had this strapped to her arm where she could check it periodically for safety's sake.  If there was one thing they didn't want to do it was to accidentally swim into a colony of the deadly mollusks!

After she got used to the breathing equipment, Jane fell more and more in love with diving on the reefs.  The world below seemed like just that - a different world.  The colors and the interplay of all the exotic marine life seemed like the inhabitants of some mystical distant planet.  It was so hard to believe that this figment of a science fiction writer's dreams was hidden right there, mere meters below the surface of the waves!

Jane filled memory card after memory card with pictures of colorful fish, coral formations, strange undersea life, and yes, more than a few of Misty clowning around for the camera.  Misty was normally such a painfully shy person that it was all the more amazing what a ham she tended to become whenever there was a camera involved.

The camera itself was truly state-of-the-art.  Jane's inner photography nerd still hadn't figured out all of the cool technical tricks it could do. 

Misty was genuinely uninterested in almost all of it.  When Jane showed her that the pink phones that Misty had bought for them could directly read the photos from the camera's memory cards, Misty found that pretty cool.  But then her friend went off on "f-stops" and "chromatic aberrations" and things, and Misty went off to sleep.

The high-tech toy was stashed below decks at the moment.  They had already satisfied themselves that the day's reef was nothing more than another undersea paradise.  Now it was all about relaxing for the time being while the Captain piloted his boat to the next survey site.  

From the far distant foredeck, Jane could hear the rattle of chain as the boat dropped anchor.  They arrived too late in the day for another dive, so there they sat, basking in the glow of the late afternoon sun.  No doubt another pleasant adventure awaited them tomorrow morning.  

It briefly crossed Jane's mind that she hadn't needed any of her anxiety pills since they left Bermuda.  But then, why would she?  Everything was awesome!

The two friends relaxed lazily, side by side, in their favorite deck chairs.  The sea gently rocked the boat like a cradle beneath them.

All things considered, a couple months' all-expense-paid cruise aboard a luxurious private yacht with your best friend in the world...  How could you beat that?  Jane couldn't come up with a way.

"I wonder if Heaven has boats in it?" the quirky thought drifted lazily in, and then back out of her mind.

Then a more concrete question popped into her thoughts, which she immediately asked the (possibly awake) figure of Misty in the next lounge chair over.

"Yo, Mist?" she asked.  (Jane spoke in the private vernacular the two had developed over the past week and a half aboard the Gypsy.)

"Yeah, J?"

"What's the deal with this Bible Study anyway?  I mean, we got: a couple of millionaires, a guy who wears pj's and lives in a warehouse,  a sweet little old lady who could out-shoot Wyatt Earp..."

"And a Wendell?"

"And a Wendell." Jane confirmed.

"Well, the Kingdom of God makes brothers and sisters out of all different kinds of  people.  Like remember the book of Philemon where..."

"Not that!"  Jane interrupted.  "Like if I was Michael... and I was putting together a team... I wouldn't choose us weirdos!"

"You'd choose the popular kids in school, right?  The strongest, the smartest, the fastest?"

"Well, yeah...  I guess."

Misty laughed and admitted.  "Me too, probably."

She continued,  "Michael, he always says,"  she changed her voice to imitate Michael Moast, " 'God made you exactly who you are, to do exactly what He needs you to do.' "

"Even my Aspergers?  How's that make anything better?"  asked Jane.

Misty shrugged.  "I dunno yet.  It must fit into the picture somehow.  Anyway, you'd be surprised what a bunch of weirdos can do."

"And a Wendell?"  Jane mocked Misty's earlier question.

"And even a Wendell."  replied Misty in turn.  Then she added, "He was trained the same time as me, Jack, and Kurt.  You wouldn't know it by looking at him, but he's actually got... skills."

"Yeah, right.  But you said 'trained'?  Nobody trained me."

Silence.

"Misty?"

She heard Misty shift nervously, but there was still no reply.

Jane adjusted the question, but didn't let up.  "Misty, why were we sent here to the middle of nowhere in the Atlantic, while McCready and the others were sent places like Indonesia?"

Misty sighed.  She knew there could be no escaping it.  "Sounds like you already know."

"Say it, then."

"Well, you nailed it.  You haven't been trained.  Some of the group just wants to see how you handle yourself before getting in over your head, that's all."

"What a waste of time!"  Irritation hung in the air.

Misty jumped in to calm her friend down.  "No way!  We're still looking for the poison, same as everybody else.  Hey, who knows, we may even find some."

"And if we did, then what?"

"Then our orders are to get a safe distance away and call it in.  Let the rest of the group handle the dangerous stuff."  She sighed.  Evidently, she disliked the situation even more than Jane did.  "That's the plan, anyway."

Jane turned to look at her friend.  There was something in the tone of her last statement...

"Plans sometimes change."  Jane offered.

"Sometimes."  agreed Misty.  A coy smile tugged at the corners of her mouth.

She rolled over and adjusted her floppy hat.  "Anyway, we'll have to see what happens first.  Then... whatever."  She yawned.

Jane let it all sink in while her friend dozed.  Maybe I'm getting all uptight for nothing. she thought,  We're probably just on some wild goose chase anyway.  Probably the whole group is.  

It made her feel better.  Her mind cleared enough for another nagging question to jump in.

"Yo Mist?  You awake?"

"No."

"K, cuz I was just wondering:  Of all the different kinds of 'mollusk' thingies, which do you think we'll find?"

"Probably an icky-looking one."

The statement hit Jane square in the funny bone and her body folded in half in laughter.  It was contagious.  Pretty soon both girls were giggling away, unable to stop.  The problem was only compounded when a finger appeared out of nowhere and landed in the side of Misty's ribcage.  

Then all out tickle-war was declared.

No doubt she and Jane would've been less relaxed if they had only known what sinister eyes watched them at that very moment!


Enzo hated boats.  Not enough people to kill.  But he was a mercenary, so he followed the money rather than the pleasure.

"Yes sir.  Ship's name: confirmed.  She's the Sea Gypsy II, all right."   Enzo's henchman looked into a monitor, shrouded against the glare of the sunlight with cardboard scraps.  

The monitor was connected to a video-feed from an automated telescope high atop the ship's foremast.  It was the concept of the old 'crow's nest', reborn for a digital age.

"They don't look like agents, sir." added the henchman.  It was the wrong thing to say.

"Agents never look like agents, you moron." snapped the leader.

And then, in a rare instance of explaining himself to his subordinates, he continued.  "The boat's registry checks out.  Definitely not worth the risk."

"Orders, sir?"

"She's dropped anchor.  We'll just board her at midnight and kill everybody." he said casually.

Then he took another swig of his drink and went on to perform other trivial duties.


~28~
Nightmare Cruise

Jane couldn't sleep.  

The combination of all the strange noises aboard the boat were still new enough to her senses that it kept her awake at times.  Even after so many days at sea she still wasn't yet used to them all.  The splashing, gurgling, and lapping of the water, the hum of lights and pumps, the groan of metal...  Now there was an all new noise bugging her - a faint, mechanical buzz that echoed though all the cabins and hallways.  Though subtle, it was enough to drive her half out of her mind.

The insomnia problem was only being inflamed by the fact that she'd spent so much time napping on deck that afternoon and now her body didn't want much sleep anyway.

Lying in her bunk looking up at the ceiling was starting to seem ridiculous.  Jane decided to wander up on deck and breathe come cool sea air instead.  She threw a criminally soft bathrobe on over her silk pajamas.  (Both of these things belonged to the ship and were provided for the comfort of the Moasts' guests.)  On the way out, she also grabbed her pink phone out of habit.

Jane ascended the metal spiral stairs and headed out of the cabin house door, making a beeline for the nearest rail.  

The moon cast a swirling silver beam across the rolling swells, and the salt air did not disappoint her expectations.

Jane thought that she heard something behind her, but as she turned to look, it was gone.  Curiously, she noticed light shining out from the cabin door, which was swinging loosely on its hinges.  Strange.  She was sure she'd shut that.

Jane turned back towards the rail.  Slowly resting her elbows on it, she leaned over for another lung full of the cool, pleasant breeze.  She had no way of knowing that as she did so, her relaxing vacation had come to an immediate, screeching end.

The festivities started with a bang as the fuse of a stick of TNT twirled a searing path through the night sky.  It bounced twice on the deck behind her, and then exploded in midair.

A first wave of a million slivers of splintered teak decking slammed into her back mere milliseconds before the full force of the explosion sent her sailing overboard into the black night sky.  

Two more explosions sounded, but she never heard those, first due to screaming through the air, and then because of crashing through the waves and struggling to regain the surface.

Treading water was hard in the open sea.  The worst part was the thick, fluffy bathrobe from the yacht.  Its terrycloth puffed up like a frightened blowfish when it hit the water.  She gurgled and spat as it tried to tug her under.  With shaking hands she struggled to loosen the tie as the first sounds of machine gun fire erupted on board.  

Screams!  

She freed the knot, slipped off the robe, and let it sink away into Davy Jones' boudoir.

She couldn't see all that well what was happening with all the waterlogged hair streaming in her eyes so she brushed it back and squinted.

Through the flames on deck she saw the silhouette of a gunman, rifle trained on a crewmember with arms upraised in surrender.  The assailant pulled the trigger and the other shape collapsed.

Jane gritted her teeth and committed herself to trying to swim back to the ship.  Her mind was set on it, even in spite of the terrible amount of pain from the salt water attacking her fresh wounds.  

It never even occurred to her that she didn't know what she'd do when she got there.  Taking on one or more armed baddies didn't seem like the correct answer.  She never got the chance to work out a plan, nor needed to.

Aboard bridge of the assaulted ship, Enzo noticed the gunfire of his men slackening and determined that the job was nearly done.

"Explosives are set, Sir." said a henchman.  "Shall I detonate?"

Enzo hesitated.  This was a fairly nice ship indeed!  He ran his calloused, dirty hand over the sleek contour of the helm.  Under most circumstances, he wouldn't mind stealing her.  Wouldn't mind it a bit.  

But, no.  Sadly, they were too far out to sea.  He'd assuredly be spotted before running it to some hiding spot ashore.  Anyway, association with Draco had taught him to dream bigger of late.

Oh well. he consoled himself.  He didn't much care for boats anyway.

"90 seconds." he told the henchmen.  That man, in turn, blew three ear-splitting blasts on a handheld air horn, which signalled the countdown time to the other killers.

The rest of the murder team knew better than to stick around once they heard that noise!  They all dropped what they were doing and made quickly for the large rubber boat that drifted off the Gypsy's stern.

The line was cast off and the vessel cruised back into the night from whence it came, all the while emitting a small, mechanical buzz designed to be silent.

Silent, that is, to all but the most sensitive ears.


~29~
Mr Coffee

Dr Puttery awoke on a dusty old duct taped couch within the sanctuary of a certain abandoned fish plant in central California.

He stretched.
His bones made some unfortunate noises.
He stopped.

He smacked his dry, pasty lips and rose groggily.

Somewhere nearby, wedged in between some unidentifiable machines was a Mister Coffee pot.  The anthropomorphic appliance, despite his being spliced into the electrical system in ways that would make a building inspector weep, faithfully delivered up his morning fuel on time.

The sight of it only proved that Dr Puttery's handiwork was a dangerous thing in its own right.  He had a quirky habit of hacking amazing things together out of mismatched junk parts and duct tape.  Nobody knew whether his choice of building materials was driven by cheapness, eccentricity, or pure scientific cunning.

It drove the Moast couple nuts that he wouldn't hear of simply buying what he needed with their vast resources.  It drove them even more nuts that Dr Puttery's cobbled-together version somehow always worked out better than anything money could buy.  And so they eventually shrugged and accommodated his strange ways.  

The phrase, 'the ends justify the means', is generally taken as a negative one, but in Dr Puttery's case, it somehow seemed to legitimize his work.

Having filled his mug, slippers shuffled on concrete as he made his way towards the other end of the plant.  On the way, he passed a mechanical display surplussed from the war room of World War II battleship.  Across a vertical etched glass representation of the globe moved four mechanical arms representing the four vessels associated with the mollusk project.

He looked down at his mug.  The coffee had a unique flavor of arabica, blended with a hint of fish and stale dust - an unfortunate bouquet contributed by the environment.  He shrugged.  It'd probably never become a hit flavor with Taster's Choice, but one got used to it.

Lost in the groggy half-thought of morning he nearly walked past the strange old display, when he noticed something out of place.  One of the markers was back at the top left corner of the glass panel.  Unless one of the team was paying a visit to Santa Claus at the North Pole, something was definitely wrong.

He took a step backwards to get a better look at the display.  He tapped the metal arms experimentally with the corner of his mug.  It wasn't stuck.  In fact, it wouldn't be there if it was stuck anyway.  It would just be a little off course, not this!

Curiosity now piqued, he picked up a clipboard off the console.  He flipped back and forth between two pages.  Having consulted the list of ships, he looked back up at the display.  It was the Sea Gypsy all right.  With a rising apprehension, he realized that the arms had gone back to their park position because they had lost signal.  

Dr Puttery walked over to an old green and black VT-200 computer terminal.  He brushed a pile junk and papers off its keyboard and started typing.  He busily threw commands back and forth to ask the positioning system what on earth it thought it was doing.  

All of the tests he tried told the same simple tale.  There was just plain no more data coming in from the Sea Gypsy.

That was impossible!  The Moast boats were all satellite linked back to home base in three different ways: K-Band to the Moasts' leased communication satellite network, their contracted LEO (Low Earth Orbit) internet provider, and worst case, by modem uplink over the public telephone satellite grid.

For all three to fail there were only two possibilities:  Total failure of the entire international telecommunication satellite infrastructure, or, he realized with dread, no boat!

Immediately he reached for a nearby office phone and hit speed dial number 1.

"Moast here." said Michael Moast into his small wireless earpiece.  (It was an experimental next step in their personal communication system.)

Dr Puttery explained the situation briefly, concluding, "I've run all the diagnostics and everything checks out.  The boat: she's just not there!"

"Run a naughty query of the Navy's positioning system and see what vessels they've got in the area.  I'll call in a favor and maybe we can get a patrol nearby to..."  

"Hold on."  Moast was cut off by Puttery.  On the other end of the line, he spotted something else on his strange mechanical display.

"Sir, it's the Cheapskate!"  Dr Puttery was referring to the Sea Gypsy's sister ship presently commanded by Wendell.  He consulted the terminal again.  "She's only 200 miles south of the Gypsy's last known coordinates and headed for open sea."

"WHY THAT STUBBORN LITTLE...." said a livid Michael Moast.  He knew exactly why Wendell was off course.  Atlantis again.  The only thing that tempered Micheal's anger was the knowledge that the kid's insubordination might very well spell rescue for the ladies aboard the Sea Gypsy.

"Get him!" ordered Moast.

Dr Puttery was way ahead of him.  As soon as he'd recognized where the second boat was he'd already conferenced-in speed dial number 4 and the system was presently in the process of ringing.


When he heard the phone ringing, the captain of the Cheapskate paged Wendell over the ship's P.A. system.  Not many rings elapsed before lanky kid came bounding up the stairwell to the bridge.

"Wendell's Bowling Lanes and Hang-Gliding School?" answered the upstart into his oversize headset.

"Wendell, I'm seriously going to skin you alive." threatened Moast from another part of the globe.  "But first I have an important errand for you."

"Yes sir?" said Wendell.  His tone turned suddenly respectful, realizing that he was on severely thin ice with Michael.  He must know.  According to how it played out in Wendell's imagination, Michael wouldn't find out until the Cheapskate sailed back into Key West... with him sitting atop a mound of priceless gilded artifacts and a smiling mermaid under each arm.  

Well... one could dream... 

That dream, however, just met with a rude awakening.

"We've lost all communication with Sea Gypsy.  We'll upload her last known coordinates to your GPS.  Get there as fast as you can!  The girls may be in danger!"

"Understood.  Cheapskate out."

The Cheapskate was so named because she was the smallest of the Moast hydroplane fleet.  Even so, anyone who knew the Moasts would immediately doubt that there was anything cheap about one of their yachts.  And 'anyone' would be right.  Though the runt of the litter, the Cheapskate also packed the most powerful engine of the group.  There wasn't a faster boat in all the Moasts' holdings - commercial or personal.

Wendell marched to the helm, grabbed the wheel, and pushed the throttle lever to its stops.  The staff Captain looked on in horror as he struggled to keep his feet.  Generally he drove while the Moasts' guests did other things.  Wendell was living proof why.

With a lion's roar, the large boat's hull quickly climbed out of the water and tore across the sea on four spindly legs, the hydroplane lifting it like an airplane wing as it sliced through the water below the surface.

"Boat!" yelled the blanched white Captain as his finger shot up.  He covered his eyes.

Wendell swerved, passing directly over the top of a small native fishing boat.  The hull of the Cheapskate soaring overhead snapped off its single mast, while the hydroplanes passing beneath the craft neatly sliced through the full fishing nets the men were reeling in.

"Sorry!" he shouted back over his shoulder.  It was an apology that didn't help, in a language they didn't understand, and couldn't hear over the engines anyway.

The men lost their fish, their net, and now looked forward to a long row back home.  They stood and shook their fists back in reply.  It was a form of communication more universally understood.


~30~
Adrift

The events of the previous night repeated themselves over and over in Jane's mind.

She saw and heard the bad guys leave and swam a stroke and a half more towards the Sea Gypsy when it exploded in a fireball!

She was too far away to feel the air blast, but the sea was a different story.  Every square inch of her body was smashed like a grape under a cinder block as the heavy concussion surged through the incompressible water.  It seemed like the shockwave would be felt on shores a thousand miles away.

Moments later debris began raining down around her.  A few flaming boards falling from the sky were the only reminder of a once great boat.

At some point she'd felt a piece of floating scrap bump against her and had enough sense to hoist herself up onto it.  Even this was a the borderline of what her mind, dazed with pain and shock, could process.

There she bobbed.  In the dark.  What else could she do?  There was nothing else.  She simply bobbed.

All through the night.  She bobbed.

The sun came up (eventually).  She bobbed.

There was nothing worth looking at in the dawn.  Just some driftwood.  And her?  She bobbed.

What else could she do?

Jane saw her waterproof camera float past and grabbed it.  Not much point now, but there wasn't any sense in watching it drift off into the sunset.

Even today she was still mostly numb from the concussion, the water, and the shock.  She didn't even feel any emotion when she turned around saw the rusty fishing boat less than fifty yards away.  Apparently she had drifted near it during the night.  It didn't matter.  She rememered the direction that the rubber boat had gone.  She wasn't about to try to hitch a ride with that bunch.  A boat full of terrorists is about as good as no boat at all when it comes to hope of rescue.

Then she had a brief flicker of a thought.  She took a picture of it.  Maybe if somebody found it on her dead body they'd be able to track down the criminals responsible.  Revenge from the grave.  what a morbid thing to think.  

Perhaps that was another reason why her brain refused to work.  Maybe she was preventing it from thinking because she didn't like what it might tell her.

Somewhere far beneath the surface, Jane thought she saw a white light streak towards the sham fishing vessel.  A moment later she saw a green light pass beneath.  The lights made no sense so she'd have been inclined to write off the first one as some kind of mirage, but the second event made it too unmistakable to be denied.

A moment later the horrid sound of rending steel sent a chill to her very bones.

She spun around to see the hated vessel sitting lopsided in the water with a huge jagged hole gaping back at her.  It looked like it was torn apart by some insane giant with a can opener.  Now that was photo-worthy!

The torn iron chunks couldn't stay afloat long before physics took over and everything too heavy to float quit pretending to do so.  The sea gobbled up the killers' boat with a growl of froth and bubbles.  

A violent, baffling, though not unwelcome end to the matter.


~31~
Rescue?

Yet again, Jane bobbled along with the plankton of the vast sea.  "Everything is plankton!" she remembered Dr Puttery's rant.  Jane didn't know at the time that in a few weeks his "everything" would include her as well!

The good news is that her membership in club plankton was about to expire.

"There's one!" shouted an unseen English voice.

A moment later and without further communication, she was being hoisted firmly aboard a small lifeboat.  Unfortunately, several of the killers from the night before were already aboard and huddled under wool blankets.  Fortunately, however, they were clearly not in control of the situation as the boat was commanded by a third party of sailors. 

That was "fortunate", right?  She sure hoped so!  Especially since those in charge also bore wicked-looking harpoon guns and an array of long, jagged knives strapped to their person. 

They didn't look like typical sailors either.  All had pale skin with no trace of tan.  How they could've been that white and ever have set foot on deck was a mystery.

They also wore iridescent silver-grey uniforms with no markings, save one.  On the shoulder where a national flag would normally have been, each wore a shoulder patch bearing an insignia of a trident.

The trident was the three-pronged spear carried by King Neptune from Roman mythology.  Jane's mind proudly delivered up this information as if it was more meaningful than some random bit of trivia.  It probably wasn't.

But then again, she recalled what Michael had said about how any detail could be important.  She slowly reached for the camera around her neck and discreetly pressed the shutter release.  She'd have been more certain of getting the shot if she could've aimed and focused.  But that certainly wouldn't have gone unnoticed.  Instead, this stunt was more a matter of pushing the button and hoping for the best.

Doubts nagged at her once more whether anyone would ever see the pictures of her adventure anyway.  She was beginning to be annoyed with all this second-guessing herself.  Therefore, she committed herself to keep taking pictures regardless.  Even at worst it would give her something to do while she awaited her fate.

Actually, the patch she photographed was indeed significant, but she was only half right in her assessment of it.  This particular trident was technically meant to represent Poseidon, the earlier Greek rendition of the same pagan deity.

After a time of paddling the small boat amongst the floating debris a voice was heard calling out in the distance.  "Ahoy!  Return to ship!"

A pair of sweat-stained sailors heaved themselves against the oars.  Jane tried to strike up conversation with them multiple times and in various ways.  If it wasn't for the orders she'd overheard, she'd have sworn the men did not speak English for all the response she'd gotten.  

Taking their communication down to the nonverbal level though, it was clear by the lack of eye contact and upturned gaze that she was regarded as no friend of theirs.  

And, again, there were those lethal harpoon guns to consider.  They never lowered.  Never for an instant.  Not exactly a gesture of hospitality to say the least.

The boat soon arrived at a rendezvous with two similar ones.  One held two other refugees (or "captives" may be a better word?) while the other was empty.  This 'ship' the men kept speaking of was nowhere to be seen.

The group of boats drifted for only a minute or two when the water quickly appeared to come to a raging boil all around them.  Jane peered over the side and noticed the same green light as before, only steadily growing brighter.  The sea rocked and foamed for a few hearththrobbing seconds more before a steel column finally broke the surface.

It was a 'conning tower'.  It was a submarine!

When the entire deck of the sub was above the surface it stopped its ascent.  The miniature waterfalls flowing off its hull were nearly drained by the time the main hatch slowly creaked open.  Several more armed sailors emerged and took up positions on deck.  Following them was a stately, white-haired woman who stood surveying the scene with hands clasped behind her back.  She was definitely the one in charge here.

The smaller boats were tied off with the help of those aboard the sub.  

Jane tried to inspect the faces of the other refugees as they were being hustled off the row boats.  She couldn't see many of them, but from the body shapes, tiny Misty was certainly not among them.

The sailors began methodically marching prisoners below under the stern leader's watchful gaze.

She was pushing 60.  Her eyes were wrinkled, but it wasn't of excessive age, or the laugh lines of Ms McCready, but wrinkled instead by years of staring out against the horizon of a thousand endless seas.  They were experienced eyes that must have seen one of everything in life.  Seeing those eyes up close made her role as the sub's Captain unmistakable.

She also had the same pale skin as the sailors, yet it seemed all the paler on her due to the bright snow-white hair pulled back tight from the temples.  It suddenly dawned on Jane that the reason for the pale skin was that they lived and worked, not on the ocean, but beneath it!

A younger man stood behind the Captain's left shoulder, also surveying the scene.  A First Mate, no doubt.  It was strange that he'd have such a high rank though.  He was younger than Jane was.  He couldn't be more than about sixteen years old or so.

As Jane was prodded closer to the hatch, she tried to gather whatever clues she could about the strangers.  The officers also wore the same silver/grey jumpsuit that betrayed no nationality or rank.  In fact, the only thing different that the Captain wore from her men was a strange silver ring with a large black gemstone of some kind.

As Jane passed the enigmatic leader she stumbled slightly on her left leg.

The woman turned and noticed that the back of Jane's silk pajamas were a shambles of splinters and blood-soaked fabric shreds, leaving her leg bare from the buttocks on down.  It looked ghastly!

The Captain held up a hand which effectively stopped Jane's escort.

"Fetch the surgeon." she said to the First Mate behind her.  It was the first polite, or even positive gesture any of the newcomers had yet made.

Between the armed guard and the First Mate, Jane was handled slightly, yet noticeably, more gently as she was lowered through the sub's main hatch. 

The last member of the crew entered.  The hatch clanged shut.  The sub sank beneath the waves.  Jane was trapped.


~32~
Nemo's Legacy

Jane was escorted down the ship's corridor, more gently now, but still very much like a prisoner.

She was led to a small one-bed cabin somewhere in the recesses of the vessel.  Her guard stopped outside the door at attention, but the First Mate and Captain followed her inside.  Immediately afterward, another man arrived.

The newcomer was evidently the ship's surgeon.  This was made obvious by the quaint, nearly forgotten, clich é of a reflective mirror strapped to his head.  The picture was rounded out buy the quintessential 'little black bag' he carried.  He had an intelligent, wrinkled face to go along with all that, but the grey jumpsuit and setting seemed to break with the Norman Rockwell portrait.

He escorted Jane to the narrow metal bunk bolted to the ship's framework and directed her to lie on her stomach.  He began to carefully peel back the fabric of her pajamas to get a look at the wound.

The Captain noticed her First Mate leering at Jane's bare thigh as the doctor examined it.  She waved the fellow outside, a harsh reprimand looming in the kid's future.

The doctor first spritzed the afflicted area with an antique perfume bottle.  It was full of something that stung bad and smelled worse.

Only a second or two passed before the medicine took effect and the pain magically disappeared.  The doctor began carefully picking out the splinters with a pair of pincers.

As he worked, the Captain walked up to the bedside to begin her interrogation.

"Now then," said the woman, "I'm going to ask some questions.  You are either going to answer those questions, or you are going to die.  Do you understand?"

"Y-yes." stammered Jane.  She was not used to having her life threatened. 

The doctor pulled out a particularly large splinter.  More of a 'board', than a 'splinter', actually.

Jane groaned.

"Why are you doing all this if you're planning on killing me?" she asked.

The Captain eyed the gigantic splinter.

"Can't let my sharks get one of these in its gut.  They don't chew their food, you know."

That little dash of trivia was not at all comforting.

She continued.  "But you forget how this works.  I ask the questions, remember?

"Now then.  You're going to tell me all about the Sea Dragon ship back there.  What were you doing on it?  What did they do to that reef?  Where did that sub go?"

"Dragon?  What?" asked Jane.  It was probably the wrong response, but it was the only one she had.

The Captain knelt and looked her in the eye, trying to determine whether she was being flippant or honestly didn't know.

"No." she concluded, "You're not one of them.  I can see it in your eyes.  Just some clueless little girl in the wrong place at the wrong time."

Jane knew it was an insult, but she was too busy feeling relieved to feel insulted.

The Captain stood back up, straightened her uniform, and continued her speech. 

"But you'd better tell me something interesting right now if you don't want to wind up fish food.  Go on.  What were you doing out there?"

Jane was still quite jumpy, which was reasonable under the circumstances, but gave her words a scattered, clipped aspect.  "My ship.  The Sea Gypsy.  They blew it up.  I don't know who they were.  But they killed... everybody!"  Her eyes began to fill.  The shock, the cold seawater, and the pain of her injuries cleared away, leaving the spotlight wide open for despair to take center stage.

The Captain was annoyed.  Beating a few helpful truths out of a hardened mercenary was productive... and even enjoyable at times.  Questioning a weepy little girl was neither.

The Captain turned to leave.

"Wait!" called Jane.

She stopped, but didn't bother to turn.

"Who are you?"

"I am Nemo.  Queen of all oceans."

Captain Nemo!??  Jane thought.  I'm hallucinating.  That's it.  I'm really still out in the water and the sun is playing tricks with my brain.

"You probably haven't heard of me..." declared the captain, and went on with some self-flattering speech Jane didn't listen to.

Jane was still lost in thought.  Wake up!  Snap out of it!  You're on the ocean.  But no force of will could bring her out of it.  Come to think of it, why would she be thinking reasonably if she was so addled that she thought Captain Nemo was real?  

She shook her head to try to clear the many levels of guesses and second-guesses from her brain.

The Captain stopped and narrowed her eyes, skepticism written all over her face.  "You shook your head.  You mean you have heard of me?"

Of course she had!  Silly hallucination  (or whatever).  Jane's long-term memory rifled back through its dusty old file cabinets for the correct folder.  There it is.  Mrs Greenwell's 7th grade English class:  20,000 Leagues Under the Sea.

The first memory to come back was a disastrous group project to build a model of the submarine -- which the other members quickly dumped on her lap under threat of not doing the work either way.  An unpleasant and irrelevant memory.  Keep digging, brain.

More relevant details began popping up now and continued to arrive as the conversation progressed.

"If you're Nemo, then this must be the Nautilus!" said Jane.

Then remembering a later tale, she corrected herself.

"No, wait.  That's impossible.  The Nautilus sank in the grotto beneath Lincoln Island."

"Very good." said the Captain with a pleased nod, "I'm surprised the schools on land bother to teach the glorious histories of the deep.  My great-grandfather had two sons.  Twins.  I am the descendant of the eldest, and therefore, rightful heir as Queen of all Oceans."

Jane felt reluctant to tell her that the only mention of any of this was from a book regarded as science fiction.

The Captain continued speaking.  This was her favorite subject.  "The Nautilus of old was a brilliant vessel for her time, but our might has increased exponentially since those days.  

"No, this is not my great grandfather's Nautilus, but my own flagship, the Starfish.

"She may have a gentle name, but make no mistake land dweller, she packs a powerful sting." added the Captain.   It was a boastful threat that did nothing to Jane's original lack of desire to pick a fight with the woman's precious submarine.

With that, the proud Captain Nemo, Queen of all Oceans, walked away.

If this really is a hallucination, thought Jane, I must be more creative than I thought.


~33~
McCready's Vice

Sharon McCready.  That was her given name.  The first name dropped off at some point, lost along the wayside a long time ago.  Few even remembered it.   Many days it sounded strange even to her.  She was Ms McCready,  "Nae more.  Nae less."

Tonight she sat in a dive Hong Kong bar sucking at a chipped tumbler full of lukewarm corn whiskey.  To call it a bar was giving it too much credit.  In reality it was little more than a greasy little wharf shack.  The boards were ill-fitting and rat-chewed.  

The furniture consisted of some landfill-worthy chairs, shipping crates, empty cable spools, and other unidentifiable junk.  If an item was close to the correct size for furniture, it was shanghaied for duty as such.

Ms McCready wore a tight, black, knit skullcap and matching wool sea coat.  She sat under the pool of light cast by a lone, bare lightbulb.

If local critics ever held a contest for the establishment with the least stars, this place would be a shoe-in.  If the other patrons didn't kill you, the tetanus probably would.

To Ms McCready though, the whiskey was even more dangerous than the location.  She knew she had to watch it.  It was a vice carried over from a bygone chapter of her life.  One that proved on many an occasion how fast it could sneak up on her.

"Easy McCready," she cautioned herself aloud, her accent deepened by the booze,  "don't wanna take it t' far.  Farst thing ye know ye start drinkin'.  Then ye start gettin' rowdy.  Then people start t' fightin' and a gettin' hurt... 

"What d' you say Li?"

There was no response from Li, who was beaten unconscious and who she was presently sitting on like a large, fish-scented sofa.

It was getting on about morning and due to liquor, or fighting, or both, Ms McCready discovered that she was the last one in the place still conscious.

She patted the man beneath her and continued the conversation as if he had responded.  "Ya know Li, when you're right, you're right."  She pounded back the rest of the whiskey in one gulp and slammed the glass back down to the table.  She sucked air in through her clenched teeth as the sauce warmed her tired bones.  

Melinda Moast was her accountability partner back home, and there'd be you-know-what to pay when she found out that Ms McCready was back to drinking again.

Ms McCready decided to stop there.  The reason was partly because it was the responsible thing to do.  It was partly because she didn't want to have to confess it to Melinda.  But it was mostly because all the bar help was presently passed out.

She took Michael's instructions to heart and was presently tracking down rumors that something big was going down in the underworld of the Orient.  She decided that the quickest route to getting to the bottom of this was to pump the local wharf rats for information.

She'd heard that this Li character was regarded as their leader.  Therefore, the best way to put the scare into rest was to put the pain to him.  It was an old standby tactic that almost always worked.

She sincerely hoped that this was the right guy though.  He'd gone down surprisingly easy.  No rib-crushing blows to the gut.  No cheap shots.  No head butts.  No Kung Fu.  No nothing.  Come to think of it... he did have a knife though...

Ms McCready looked around the room to see where it had gone.  Then she lifted her right arm.  It still dangled from the flesh by its point.  She pulled it out with a jerk and eyed it.  

"Kids now days..." she sighed.

This new crop of 'toughs' were anything but.  She remembered what they were like back in her Navy days.  Back then the thugs could really fight.  These guys were nothing more than pretty boys in comparison.  She sighed again.  Times were changing.  No getting around it.  Civilization was creeping in even here.  Soon there'd be no wild places left.

"Oh well!  Can't fight destiny!"  She threw some money down on the stained table.  

Tomorrow she'd shake down the other dock workers and find out some more about this 'Fong' character she'd heard about.  Maybe, with luck, they'd all try to jump her at once.  That might at least be interesting.

After that, it was back to the boat and the ever-enthralling search for mollusks.  

It was a good thing there was no more whiskey.


~34~
A Welcome Message

The pain shocked Jane back to her full senses for the first time since the Sea Gypsy explosion.

Jane sat rigidly upright on the edge of her bunk in her comfortable, but locked and guarded, cabin.  The foul-smelling and fast-acting pain killer that the doctor had used on her leg had just worn off.  In its place a stinging/burning sensation quickly spread across the limb.

She did a fair bit of wide-eyed hyperventialling.

Jane now wore the same grey jumpsuit that served as the uniform for the ship's crew.  Whatever the strange fabric was, it was not exactly gentle on her wounds either.

The pain drove her back to reality.  And with that reality came the gravity of all that had happened in the past 24 hours.  Her emotions suddenly tore loose all at once.

The Sea Gypsy...  All the crew...  All those wonderful people...  Dead!

Then an even worse thought struck her like a sledge hammer.  Misty!  Is she... 

"No!"  She shouted aloud and slammed her fist against the wall.  Jane's heart refused to let her even think it.

Could Misty have somehow made it to a lifeboat?  Or perhaps somehow have been taken aboard with another group of prisoners?

It was desperate, illogical thinking.  Anybody below decks when the Sea Gypsy exploded was thoroughly blown to bits along with it.  

Blown to bits!  Jane immediately broke down under the crushing weight of the thought.

In between sobs, she looked down at her hand.  She still held the pink phone in a death grip.  It was probably busted, but she'd treasure it forever.

Jane stared and stared at its blackened, lifeless screen for hours.  Perhaps all night.  Or was it day?  She didn't know anymore.  The passage of time became meaningless.  

The searing physical pain, Captain Nemo, her best friend dead:  It was all some kind of sick dream and she couldn't seem to make it come to an end.

She would cry until she couldn't cry anymore, dry her eyes, and then cry again.

While gazing at the phone for the thousandth time, it suddenly beeped!  She was so startled that she dropped it.  Then, horrified by what she'd done, she picked it up again and hit the power button.  Amazingly, it still worked!

More than that, there was an unread text message:
Dear Jane,

I'm so sorry for everything I put you through.  I wish I had never brought you into that Bible Study.  Now it has cost me the dearest friend I've ever known.  I'll always miss you, my friend.

Till next we meet on Heaven's shores,
Misty

Jane's body, so racked with despair and pain, suddenly pulled a 180 as it dawned on her that Misty was still somehow alive!

"That's very touching.  Thank you so much!" tapped Jane into the phone, and hit [SEND].

"WHAT!  I THOUGHT YOU WERE DEAD!" came the quick reply in all caps.

Overcome with joy, Jane teased back.  "I am.  I'm in Heaven.  You wouldn't believe how fast the wifi is here!"

Misty didn't respond to the teasing.  Apparently the comment made her wonder how they did suddenly have a connection again.

"My phone says I'm connected to one of the moast_net routers.  How's that possible?" she wrote back.

Jane glanced at her own network settings.
"Mine too.  I knew the Moasts had a lot of reach, but all the way to the sea floor is a new one on me."  She thought for a moment.  "Maybe the router from the Sea Gypsy sank around here and is somehow still broadcasting."

"Sounds majorly far-fetched to me, but I've got nothing better." replied Misty.

The screens were silent for awhile.

"I don't get it.  How come you're alive?" texted Misty.  "I saw you blown up with dynamite!"

Jane's mind put the pieces together.  So that explained how Misty had survived.  She must have followed her on deck that night.

"Just blessed, I guess."

Another stab of pain prompted her to add, "My leg's in pretty bad shape though.  Doc says no more tickle wars for a month."

Misty "LOL'd" the joke.

"Did you meet that character calling herself Nemo?", Jane asked.

"Yeah.  Don't know whether she's legit or some kinda crackpot."

"Same here."  

Recalling the chilling madness of character from the book, Jane added, "Does it matter either way?"

"Maybe not.  Crazy's crazy, you know."

Jane continued after a moment.  "You know what else though?"

"We've got to get outta here!" replied Misty, reading the other's thoughts.


~35~
Skeksis Dinner

Time dragged on as the two girls sat alone in their separate cabins.  Another full day passed (according to the time on their phones).  Frequent text conversations now took the edge off of what would otherwise have been an unbearable loneliness.

No sooner had Jane and Misty ended their conversation for the night but a guard entered the cabin of each.  Both were escorted to the Captain's private dining room.  Well, that was progress at least.  Maybe.

They were taken to a large, elaborately-furnished room with an ornately-carved wooden table set up in its center.  The table seated four and had a place set for each.

"About time we had an official welcome dinner." said he white-haired Captain Nemo.

The same First Mate Jane remembered from days earlier brought out an antique china tea service.  He presented it to the Captain.  She poured a cup and offered it up in Jane's direction with the look of a question on her face.

"No thanks." said Jane.

The Captain shrugged.  "Seaweed tea is an acquired taste, I suppose."

She flavored the cup to her own liking and waved the young man away.

"Sandy.  My son." she explained as he faded back into the shadows.

Jane thought it odd that he made no move to approach what was, by deduction, his seat at the table.

Turning to her side, Jane saw a strange rectangular object sitting on a shelf.  It was made of glass but was filled with some kind of transparent, bubbly goo.  What was the most amazing part about it was that the perfectly detailed head of an old man floated inside.  It was so realistic that at first glance Jane was startled.  It seemed like a head-in-a-jar gag from a spook alley.  After her brain caught up, she realized that it was a type of three-dimensional photograph.

"And that is a picture the original Captain Nemo," explained the present Nemo,  "my great grandfather.  Place your hand on top and see what happens."

Jane did.

The head began to speak.  The voice was a little wet and garbled but still understandable.  It sounded almost like someone trying to talk with their mouth half underwater.  "Captain Nemo, King of All Oceans", it said, paused, and repeated.  She touched the top again and it lapsed back into its frozen state.  Very interesting.

Is this for real?  She thought, for not the first time.

The medium, more than its message, tipped the scales in Jane's mind from mostly skeptical to mostly believing.  Of course it could all still be some elaborate hoax on a Mission Impossible level... but to what end?

She wished she had her camera to take a picture of the thing, but Jane had prudently left it back in her cabin.  She was rightly worried that the Captain might decide to confiscate it for security reasons.

"Not as good as a modern aquagraph." the Captain explained.  "But for a recording over a hundred years old, what can you expect?"

The Captain continued speaking as a server brought out several silver platters of food and laid them out on the table.  

As soon as the steward finished, she gestured to the spread and said,  "Please, be seated.  As you can see, the bounty of the great oceans of this Blue Earth has been provided for your enjoyment."

Jane and Misty took a seat next to one another and went for the food.  Both were fairly starved.

There, on the table, was indeed an array of sea creatures, cooked to perfection, complete with whatever antennae, tentacles, and eyeballs they were born with.  Misty had a little trouble meeting their beady gaze.  Even so, she handled the situation far, far better than the inland-raised Jane who was immediately sick to her stomach.

The Captain saw her pale expression.  The woman's perpetually hard lips morphed into a subtle smugness.

"Very well," she said with all airs, "It seems that the 'Landies' would rather pass up the great delicacies of the sea for a..." she paused and added with disdain, "hamburger."

She rang a small bell.  The steward immediately reappeared, this time with plates of hamburgers. He placed one in front of each of the girls, and a third at the empty place setting.

Jane surmised that choosing the burger over the strange seafood was some kind of admission of guilt.  One that would only serve to reinforce the Captain's prejudices of all land-dwellers as cultureless brutes.  Her stomach didn't have so much pride though.

Just as Jane was about to dig in despite the political ramifications, the Captain froze.  Slowly, she stood and slid back behind a support girder next to the room's entrance.  

"So...?" Jane started to ask something.

The Captain continued to sip her tea but held up two fingers in a command for silence.

Jane and Misty couldn't explain the reason behind the gestured order, but obeyed it anyway.

A moment later the door's handle slowly, slowly, began to turn.  It took quite awhile but eventually the latch disengaged and the door swung open with equal slowness.  An eye appeared at the crack.  It widened as it espied the girls.

Swift and smooth as a breeze, the door swung open.  The figure stepped inside and pulled it back closed again without a sound.  The mystery person turned and removed its black mask to reveal the face of... 

"Wendell!??" both girls exclaimed in surprised unison.

Wendell darted over to where they sat.  He wore a rubberized wetsuit, with the tanks, mask, and the rest of his SCUBA gear in a bundle under his arm.

"Hey girls, we gotta get out of here, quick!" he whispered with urgency.

"Is that so?" said the Captain behind his back.

He did a double-take.

"Okay, you can come too." he whispered to her.

"Nice try, kid.  But there won't be any escaping tonight.  Now sit down and eat your hamburger."

Wendell realized that the jig was up, but was not opposed to any change in plans that involved stuffing his face.  As he looked over the table his eyes suddenly lit up.

"A'right!  Toppings!" he said excitedly.  He leaped forcefully into the lap of the fourth chair and dove for the seafood.

He grabbed an octopus here, a crustacean there... and all of it went on the burger!

The Captain's formerly smug look began to take on a subtle revulsion of its own.

"Wow!  Is that fried sea urchin?" he shouted with glee.  

Wendell grabbed a couple of skewers of something and commenced eating them off the stick.  He dipped one of the kabobs in ketchup.  The Captain visibly winced this time.  

Jane realized with astonishment that he was getting to her!  She was playing the iron lady, but he was actually getting to her!

Furthermore, Jane couldn't determine whether Wendell was starving, remembering some Japanese cuisine of his heritage, trying to egg Nemo on, or just being a weird, gross boy.  Probably some combination of all that, she ultimately decided. 

The Captain tried to ignore the scene by pressing on to her agenda for the evening.

"You two, I fished out of the drink in your pajamas."  The captain stabbed a pair of fingers in the air in the direction of at Misty and Jane.

"And you,"  her gaze turned to Wendell,  "you came at me in a glass peanut pretending to be a submarine.

"Therefore, you are clearly not part of the Sea Dragons." she concluded.  "I don't know who you are, but you should know right now that I don't do rescues.  If Landies want to sink in the sea and feed my fishes, I let them.  Give me one good reason why I don't set you adrift right here and now?"

She was definitely tired of fooling around.

"Because I can take you to the Sea Dragons." shot back Wendell immediately.  

It needed to have been immediately.  Any hesitation at all in a gambit like that would reveal the whole bluff and buy them all a one way ticket to a watery grave.

"Oh really?" she said skeptically.  But at least she was interested.

Wendell bit into his burger which, being covered in octopus, made a sickeningly juicy squish noise.  Even Nemo was affected.

"Easily." he said with his mouth full.

"Fine.  Convince me and I'll put you back on land then.  Fail to do so, and you take one last bath."

"The Sea Dragons have developed a horrible new neurotoxin derived from a species of sea mollusk.  Presently, they're only using it to wipe out high-ranking politicians.  But once the fun of that wears off, they plan to scale up and unleash deadly worldwide war.  That gets them to their ultimate goal of overthrowing all the governments of the globe."

The woman laughed and simply waved off the threat.  "I'm the Queen of the seas!"  she said amused, "My kingdom is 70% of the Earth.  Let the Landies kill eachother in war after war for the other 30%."

She took another sip of tea, trying to look at ease with herself.
