"Nope.  Sorry." said Wendell.  He picked something out of his burger, flicked it aside, and continued with the universally disgusting meal.

"I said 'worldwide', and I meant 'worldwide'.  The Sea Dragon leader; He's got the plan, he's got the poison, and he's got..." Wendell paused for effect before playing his last wildcard, "Atlantis."

The Captain nearly choked on her tea, Jane froze, and Misty clenched her fists as if wanting to throttle him.  Wendell kept munching away nonchalantly.

There was only one thing that could tear Captain Nemo away from a conversation of such personal importance as this one.  Fortunately for the bluffers, that one thing happened.

A torpedo blast rocked the Starfish from stem to stern.


~36~
Dock

Ms McCready walked along the worn wooden dock.  In spite of its disrepair, it ranked much higher than the dock she'd visited last night.  This one was broad and in a state of steady use.  Many small-time shipping vessels lined it on both sides, while their cargo crates were staked intermittently in large mounds, reaching up into the early morning fog like wooden ziggurats.  

The sum total of the fog, the mournful cry of gulls, and the creaky planks all combined to produce an overall sense of eeriness.

Ms McCready walked past a weather-worn Chinese sailor nailing a lid on a wooden crate.  She gave him a friendly pat on the back as she passed.  He looked up, wondering who the unfamiliar person was who had materialized from the fog in one direction and then disappeared back into it in the opposite.

Even if you didn't believe in ghosts, you could still feel them watching you from within the mysterious haze.

Ms McCready kept walking until she arrived at a three-masted junk which was presently being loaded with cargo.  Near its forward gangplank were two men.  One was giving a progress report to the other, who stood leaning against a skewed pylon smoking a cigarette.  Its red ember seemed like a lighthouse in the gloom.

"Hello everybody." said Ms McCready in the same sloppy Mandarin dialect as the dock workers.  It was perfect it its imperfection.

Mister cigarette looked up and eyed her suspiciously.  From the look of it, he was the guy in charge.  Second in command, of course, to...

"So you're the one who beat up Li?" he said in a husky voice, "I wondered when you were going to show up."  The tone he used when referring to the incident made it sound like something that he secretly approved of.  

And why not?  
It gained him a temporary promotion, after all.

Even so, the man couldn't allow an outsider to make monkeys of them, and so, put on his best 'tough guy' face.

"I suppose you think we're impressed by that.  Well, we're not!  Go home, mom, before you get yourself hurt."

The other sailors stopped what they were doing and began to close in around her on all sides.

Tension hung in the still air.

Somewhere in the distance, the bell of an unseen buoy donged.

Finally, Ms McCready answered the cigarette man.  "Sure!  I'd be glad to go home.  Just as soon as you tell me who this 'Fong' character is I been hearin' so much about."

The men looked from her back to their de-facto leader.

"The last guy who came around asking about Fong... was found floating in the harbor shot full of holes.  So run along now,"  his eyes narrowed in menace, "...while you still can."

It was a lie.  They'd jump her before she got ten steps and McCredy knew it.  That's why she had a plan B.

She let out an exasperated sigh and withdrew from her pocket a black box the size of a deck of cards.  There were two large, red switches in the center.  She slowly extended a metal antenna.  "I was hoping you'd be willing to be sociable..."

The leader scoffed.  He wasn't buying the bluff.  "You want us to believe there's a bomb on the boat?  I'm sorry, but no chance!  With the kind of stuff we're hauling, I have highly-trained guards swarming all over these docks.

"Now you're going to wish you'd have left when you had the chance."  He quit leaning against the pole and chucked his cigarette hard at the ground, ready (and eager) for trouble.

The men all took a step forward.

McCready's first switch flicked over with a snap.

For a moment nothing seemed to happen, but then a large crate by the nearest deck rail of the boat exploded.  The charge went up in a brilliant orange fireball which mushroomed skyward.  It caught some of the rigging on fire but was otherwise harmless.

McCready didn't duck like the other men when it went off, but instead used the diversion to her own full advantage.  She leapt forward, grabbed the leader's own knife out of his belt, and held it to his throat.  That kept him immobile just long enough for her to take another fast step and get behind him so that none of his men could jump her from the rear.

"Now," she said, pulling the knife tighter, "I think I' been more than polite in the askin' up t' now."

He knew what she wanted.  He also knew he was out of options.  As some of the men broke off to extinguish the small fires he began to spill his story.

"Fong's got some powerful backers.  The Chinese Dragon Syndicate.  Nasty stuff.  He's positioning himself to take over the whole works."

"You mean all the smuggling?" she waved her free hand at the smouldering junk.

"Nah.  He consolidated all illegal smuggling under his belt ages ago.  I mean everything, everything.  He'll have total control of the Chinese mainland in three... four weeks at most.  After that, who knows?  No stopping the ambitions of a greedy character like that!"

Ms McCready still didn't understand.  Or maybe she did understand, but it was too big to fathom.

"Sure'n you're not tellin' me that he's taking over the Chinese government!"

"Yes ma'am!  Unexplained accidents, bribery, threats, blackmail, poisoning, and good old mysterious disappearances...  Pretty much anybody who'd stand in his way is already long gone.

"China's his already - only waiting for the chance to make it official.  And then, country by country, they'll all fall like dominoes until the new Emperor owns the world!"

"That's impossible!" her skepticism spoke for itself, "The fist time he dared set foot on foreign shores, he'd be nuked back to..."

A shake of the cigarette-loving smuggler's head cut off the thought.  "No," he said,  "that kind of thinking only works for a diplomatic leader who has patriotic pride and wants what's best for his people.  Fong doesn't care about any of that.  What Fong wants is the whole cake for himself and doesn't care who gets nuked along the way."

The leader shrugged.  He was already a dead man if any of Fong's spies were listening, so he decided that he might as well spill the whole story.  "Nukes are old news anyway.  Fong's developed an all new kind of weapon.  Some kind of poison that can wipe out an entire city and harm neither buildings nor loot."

The man smiled a heinous, lopsided smile.  "Isn't that great?  My ship here will have more plunder than it can carry!"

Ms McCready didn't share his excitement.  "What else?"

"That's all I know.  You now know everything I can tell you about Fong.  You have all the information you wanted."  Then his face contorted into a jagged grin that showed every one of his few remaining teeth.  "Now let's see if you can leave the dock alive with it."

He was right, of course.  The other thugs were now packed across it in a tight picket line.  It was a solid wall of muscle and menace that effectively barred the way between her and the shore.

Ms McCready dropped their leader like a cat that tired of playing with a mouse and walked boldly towards them with a confident pace.  He didn't follow.  He wanted to watch.

As she strode down the wharf, she flung the man's favorite knife over the side where it landed in the water with small sploink noise.  Then, just as a large stack of crates came between her and the boat, she casually flicked the second switch on the transmitter.  

The first charge had been for show.  This one was anything but.

The large multi-ton ship was blown momentarily clear of the water before bursting apart in innumerable flaming fragments.  The concussion was equally as tremendous and sent the row of thugs hurtling into the sea.

When the blast went off, Ms McCready was well enough sheltered behind the crates that she didn't even break step.  She just kept on walking, but hesitated when she got to where the gang had recently stood.  She turned back around to face the leader once more.

He lay sprawled on his back on the dock looking at her in astonishment.

"Oh yeah," she called, "Here's a little piece of advice:

"Next time, hire professional mercenaries for your guards.  Those boys put up nae fight at all."

With that, she continued walking down the dock and slowly faded away into the Hong Kong fog.


~37~
Peanut

Still staggering from the torpedo blast, Captain Nemo darted out of the dining room to take command of the situation.  As she met the exit, she barely grabbed the bulkhead in time just as another, smaller explosion rocked the boat.  With a shove she was out the door and running drunkenly toward the bridge.

Sandy followed her but stopped just long enough to eye Jane.  Then he was also, thankfully, on his way.

The steward quickly began to clear the table so that he could secure the area for battlestations.  (Although red-alert wasn't yet sounded, it was pretty obvious to all concerned.)

Wendell saw the meal disappearing and quickly did some securing of his own.  He began stuffing as much in his pockets as he could grab before the quick steward whisked the serving dishes away.

To Wendell, it was a losing proposition, and the dishes were ultimately cleared out from under his crestfallen nose.  But once that diversion was gone, it gave his attention a chance to settle on the more important situation.

"I don't know who our new pals outside the sub are, but I'm afraid we might be dead meat." he said.

" 'Sea Dragons,' that's what she called 'em." said Jane.

Wendell shrugged.  "Means nothing to me.  But if they're surface-dwellers (like I suspect) then we could be in bad trouble all the same."

"Why's that?" asked Misty.

"You forget, I got the self-guided tour of this whole soup can.  Whatever technology they're using, it's nothing like we've got on land.  Some pretty off-the-wall stuff.  Almost like it branched off some time in the past and they developed their own way of doing things."

He scoffed.  "Crazy contraption doesn't even have propellers!  How it even goes is beyond me!"

"Maybe Nemo can hold her own then?" asked Misty.

It seemed overly optimistic, but still plausible enough.

"Maybe so," Wendell conceded, "but I sure didn't see any smaller subs or torpedoes anywhere."

As if on cue a diver with a heavy, brass face mask swam past the room's port-hole window.  The spear gun he carried would turn a human or other creature into a sea urchin shishkabob, but against submarines they might as well be marshmallow guns.

Wendell waved at the window.  "Just look at 'em.  These guys don't know modern warfare.  They've got no idea what they're up against!"

Another blast hit them.

"We've got to do something!" he said, finally.  With that he began to search the room for some means of escape.

The girls saw how futile that was, and didn't see any way that the three could "do something" even if they did get free.  They decided that the only thing they could do was to let Nemo fight her own battles and pray that they were underestimating her.  

Meanwhile, they needed to keep their wits about them and try to push back the urge to panic.  Through many years of tight scrapes, the one thing Misty knew was that panic was always the greatest danger to survival.  She feared for her friend who was new at this game and decided to take her mind off it with some light conversation.

"I was so happy when I got your message earlier." said Misty as the battle raged without.  "Like you said, it must've been a miracle that the moast_net router of the Gypsy survived the sinking."

"No.  Not possible." snapped Wendell.  He was more irritated at not finding anything than with the ignorant comment.

"Sorry." he said when he heard how sharp his tone was.  "The thing is, girls, this sub has been underway for many hours.  Even if the Gypsy's electronics could've survived, we're miles away from where it went down.  The moast_net you're connected to must be..."

A wave washed over his face with a sudden epiphany.  "The Peanut!"

(Wendell unconsciously adopted Captain Nemo's term for the minisub.  Little did Nemo know at the time, but her offhanded remark had officially christened the little vessel.)

Both girls looked confused, not recognizing the reference.

"...the minisub from the Cheapskate!" he explained, walking briskly to where they stood.

"Excuse me." he said, snatching up Jane's phone without waiting for permission.

She didn't have time to object.

Wendell tapped the phone for a few seconds making little boops and beeps as he went.  The girls, now silent, strained to see what he was doing.  They probably wouldn't have understood most the geek magic that was going on, but they were curious nonetheless.

"Got it!" he announced proudly.

"Got what?" asked Misty.

He turned the phone so they could see.  On the tiny screen they saw an underwater scene with the familiar hull of the Starfish stretching off into the distance.  It was the camera from the minisub!  A body of the strangely-clad divers armed with harpoons swam past.  A moment later, an armored minisub shot past in pursuit.

"They're getting slaughtered out there!" exclaimed Misty.

"That's what I was afraid of." said Wendell. "Those are Mitsubishi TX-80's.  Expensive little guppies, but more than a match for crazy-fool divers with harpoons.

"Plus, those are short-range craft, so it also means that there's got to be a support ship nearby.  That's probably what's flicking torpedoes at us."

But the time for speculation was over.  Now was the time for action.

Wendell started slinging orders.  "Misty, get over to that porthole and help guide me.  Jane, you grab Misty's phone and keep a running update on that wifi status.  We're in a tin can underwater so range won't be great.  If I let the sub stray too far away we could lose it completely!"

Wendell steered the tiny submarine from the phone as if he was playing a video game.

"Five bars," said Jane, "Looking good."

A few seconds passed.

"I have visual." stated Misty.

"Four bars."

"Two o' clock.  Injured diver.  Get his spear gun."

"I'm on it." said Wendell.

He maneuvered the Peanut in close and grabbed the gun with its short, stubby pincer.  He didn't even try for the handle.  There was no way the clumsy little sub could've pulled the trigger anyway.

"Bogie at 4 o' clock, 60 degrees elevation."

"I see it."

"Four bars.  Holding steady."

The little Peanut charged at one of the enemy minisubs.  

Surprisingly, the pilot decided to flee.  It was a little like a chihuahua driving off a doberman, but hey, whatever worked.

"Three bars." chimed Jane.

Wendell streaked towards the other sub at full speed.  If driven normally, the Peanut's tiny motors wouldn't have been a match for it.  Now it was empty of passengers though, and slowly gained.

"Two bars."

Wendell lowered the harpoon gun like a knight readying his lance.  No sense going for the hull.  The sub had one central propeller, and that was its vulnerable spot.

"One bar left!" cried Jane.

The little Peanut jammed its harpoon gun into the other sub's propeller shroud and looped over backwards toward the starfish.  The spear gun was wrenched from its gripper by the maneuver as the Sea Dragon sub's brass propeller sheered off its shaft and the craft drifted to a stop.

The pilot, realizing his sub was dead in the water, did an extremely stupid thing.  He pulled on a scuba mask and abandoned ship.  Before he was even fully out of the hatch, the Starfish's divers had run him through with a pair of harpoons. 

"One down.  Where's the other?" asked Wendell to his spotter.

"I lost it."  A pause.  "No!  There it is retreating!" Misty corrected herself.

"That can't be good." observed Jane.

"Two torpedoes!  Brace yourselves!" shouted Misty suddenly.

A bone-shattering explosion smacked the Starfish so hard that it sent it into a side-over-side roll.  Two and a half times it rolled over like a dog doing a trick, leaving those inside battered and the sub itself upside down.  On the ceiling, Jane staggered to her feet and looked around.  Wendell lay crumpled against a bulkhead in a pool of blood.  Misty was momentarily not to be found.

"Those were concussion rounds." the other girl reported.

Jane looked up to see her still at the port hole only dangling from a support pylon.

Slowly she passed overhead like a comet as the sub's ballast began causing it to right itself.

Meanwhile Jane walked and skidded in the opposite direction below and Wendell's poor, limp body slid along the ceiling and down the wall, leaving a trail of blood as it went.

The girls both ran to him as soon as the sub returned to a normal attitude - albeit rocking like a giant, demon-possessed baby cradle.

"He's okay." said Misty, checking his pulse.

And added, "He's just unconscious.  Bleeding has nearly stopped already."

Then she thought of something and ran to the porthole.

"Jane!  Quick!  The Peanut!  She's nearly out of range!"

Jane did a quick scan of the room.  Somehow in the confusion she remembered last seeing her phone sliding...  that way!  She righted a chair and found it underneath, thankfully, not broken.  The wifi signal was on the last bar though and the video was already a garbled mess of pink and blue confetti.

She spun the direction dial as fast as she could, just as the last bar disappeared.
"Connection Lost" popped up on the screen.

"I lost her." said Jane with a sinking feeling.

Misty watched the far away object shrinking off into the distance. 

"Wait!" she shouted excitedly.

It began to make a slow circle back toward the Starfish.

"That was too close!" Jane shook herself as the warning message disappeared and the video stream slowly began to recover itself.

Then she noticed from her readouts that something else was wrong now.

"We're moving.  Picking up speed fast." Misty confirmed the observation with her own.

It was all the little Peanut could do to keep up with the larger sub.

Jane tried the manipulator but it was difficult to control (requiring substantial practice that she simply didn't have).  As the large Starfish began to slide past her, she finally got enough hang of the arm to clamp onto a protruding fin and hold on tight.

The Starfish accelerated like a crazed beast.  The Captain seemed intent on running down the enemy's mother-sub, which was indeed been the source of the big explosives.  The other sub tried to get away, propellers churning the sea to bubbles but the Starfish was far too fast for them.

To Misty, looking out the porthole, it seemed like they were about to ram the other vessel.  Instead, the front end of the Starfish peeled back into five petals like a blossoming flower.  Or, one might observe, like the arms of an actual starfish about to feed.  As it touched the back of the other boat, they quickly snapped shut around it, clamping the two subs together tight.

An unshakable embrace -- but to what end?

The answer was not long in coming, as somewhere below their feet they heard the metallic protests of huge machinery coming to life.  A giant fourteen foot diameter auger began to spin and slowly extend itself from what would be the mouth of the Starfish's namesake.

Metal screeched, and snapped, and tore with huge, titanic noises that made themselves felt by every bone in a person's body.

Their aggressor's sub was a modern SSN (Submersible Ship, Nuclear-powered), with the latest double-hull design to prevent all kinds of punctures and damage from getting through.  Its designers could never have accounted for this, though.  The Starfish easily bored through both hulls like a wet paper towel, and then through as many inner compartments as it could reach as well.

(When Nemo's ship bit, it bit hard!)

The enemy's lights flickered and went out.  It was mortally wounded.  The moment the Starfish disengaged it began to plummet towards the sea floor.  There, it landed with a hollow, metallic, thunk and a billowing cloud of silt.  It was done for.

The Starfish reversed engines to maneuver back to where it had left its divers fighting a losing battle against the minisubs.  Two enemy subs still remained.  One seemed to be dead in the water.  It looked like somebody must have taken notice of Wendell's stunt with the harpoon gun and had taken out its drive system.  The second was soon subdued as well, thanks to the help of Jane and the humble Peanut.

Once the subs were incapacitated, Nemo's divers relieved them of their occupants and rounded up all of the retreating enemy SCUBA divers.

Jane didn't know what she was expecting to happen next.  Perhaps she expected the captured men to be taken aboard.  Perhaps she expected to see them respectfully treated as prisoners of war under the Geneva Convention.

But this was the wild ocean.  There was no Geneva Convention here.

Each Starfish soldier drove his or her captives before him past the bow of the Starfish as if parading them before Nemo herself as a trophy.  Soon, all of them were together in a group.  Then, as if hearing some unknown order from the Captain, each of her men quickly and with cold efficiency, ran their captives through with their razor sharp harpoons!  They didn't need to fire at this distance.  Just ram the spear, gun and all, through the victim.

One or two tried to escape, but didn't get far.

Nemo's soldiers returned to the sub, leaving a scene of carnage floating behind them.

Jane felt like she was going to throw up.  What she saw outside was horrible!  Just horrible!  True, the battle had been brutal, but this.... this was nothing but the wholesale slaughter of unarmed men!  She didn't care that they were part of the same outfit that blew up the Sea Gypsy, nor did it matter that they were terrorists plotting to poison untold thousands of innocent people.  Nobody deserved to die like that!

Then the sharks came.

Jane did throw up.


~38~
A Bumpy Ride

The Russian rebels battered in the door and burst into the command trailer, SWAT team style!

Apart from a pair of radar operators slumped over their console there was not much to see inside.  Slowly they made their way forward, the muzzles of their ready firearms leading the way and sweeping the room as they went.

At a silent gesture from the point man, two men broke off and made their way forward to search the office of the commander.  Meanwhile, two stayed behind to do the same with this room.

One of the latter nearly stepped through a jagged hole cut in the floor, gaping like an open manhole.  He gestured for the other to check it out.  His companion lowered his head and weapon arm through.  He focused intently into the twilight void beneath.  He saw motion at the far end and munched down hard on his trigger.  The fully automatic weapon riddled the person's body like swiss cheese.

"Did you get him?" asked the first man in excited Russian.

"I don't know.  Give me your light."

He beamed the flashlight around under the trailer until he located the corpse.  It was clearly, gruesomely, dead.

Angry footsteps could be heard stomping up the metal stairs outside.  Then the door flew open in a rage.

"Idiot!  You just shot Yurgi!"

The mistake wasn't too surprising or uncommon.  These people were street thugs and smugglers, not trained soldiers.

"Well, if the spy wasn't down there... then where could he have gone?"

The detachment from the front office returned empty as well.

"Search the place again!" shouted the late Yurgi's partner.  "He must be in here somewhere!"

Jack lay on his belly listening to all this.  He was on the roof, presently timing the patrol patterns of the guards circling the trailer.  He'd have liked to have gotten a better feel for the rhythm of it, but he knew he must make his break the next time the darker-haired man passed.  He could hear the guys inside ransacking the place looking for him.  It would only be a matter of minutes before one of the buffoons noticed the ladder and radar dome maintenance hatch in the center of the trailer.

As soon as the dark-haired guard passed, Jack quickly swung his legs over the side.  He lowered himself slowly to the extent of his reach.  There he let his hands slip and dropped to a crouch on impact.  He'd hoped that by doing so he could absorb some of the noise of the fall.  That worked fine, but there was far more dust kicked up off the dry ground than he was comfortable with.

He took a nice, deep breath and then sprinted for the nearest cover - a stack of bright red barrels thirty yards away.

A few seconds later he sat in the dust behind them, panting hard.  His breath was stifled by the sour smell of gasoline.  The hiding spot had its element of danger, but at least provided a minute or two to rest and think.  As he did so, he drew the gun he'd borrowed from one of the radar operators on the way out.  (Such generous people, always lending him these things...  He must remember to write a thank-you note!)

Now that he got a closer look at the pistol, it wasn't much of a gun either.  Just a simple 10 round sidearm made mostly from cheap stamped tin.  Not that it mattered much.  He was behind a wall of gasoline after all, so a gunfight was probably not the best option available to him.

"Lord, show me a way out of this one!" he muttered aloud.

Just then a semi truck rumbled into view.  It towed a flatbed trailer with two U.S. made Humvees parked end-to-end.  They had a different paint job, but there was no mistaking that design.

It was certainly an interesting point of speculation how they might have come to be here.  What was more immediately interesting, however, was that from the angle it was moving, the truck was about to pass directly in front of him!

Jack couldn't decide whether that was considered an 'answer to prayer', or what.  Let the seminarians argue about it.  He wasn't about to be picky.  Either way, he'd take it!

He stepped out casually just as the back corner of the trailer was passing by, and hopped aboard.

Jack immediately crawled underneath the rearmost Humvee to try and minimize his chances of being seen.  Making his way to each tire, he silently removed the wheel restraint straps.  

He took a deep breath.  Now for the moment of truth.  He rolled out from underneath, slid the driver's side door open, and hoisted himself inside.

Thankfully, the keys were still in the ignition.  If the truck driver had any imagination at all, he'd have thought to take them with him.

Jack started the engine.  Again, thankfully, it was unable to be heard over the roaring semi.

He took a deep breath.  If Wendell was here he'd say something like "that first step" being "a doosey".  Of course if Wendell was here, he could be manning the 50 caliber machine gun turret on the roof. 

As the plan stood, however, Jack knew that he was about to kick over a hornets' nest.  His only option:  run away, FAST!

He tried to force the rising panic back down into his guts.  Oh well.  Here goes.  And he popped the clutch and mashed the accelerator to the floor before he could change his mind.

The Humvee soared off the back of the moving truck and four feet to the hard ground.  Its shocks took the blow but Jack struggled to maintain control as it bounced and skidded wildly.  If it got away from him and rolled, he'd be done for!

A pair of sunglasses fell off the sun visor, bounced off his head, and landed in his lap.  "Why thank you.  Don't mind if I do."  He put them on with one hand while trying to hang onto the bucking bronco with the other.

He punched the radio knob as well.  Might as well have a little driving music.  The first preset was for a Russian heavy metal station.  Guitars screamed like banshees trying to wake the dead.  The words were equally unintelligible in any language.  Perfect!

As jack zoomed past his former hiding spot, he rolled down the window and popped off some shots at the wall of gasoline barrels.  Soon they were nothing more than a roaring tower of fire in his rearview window.

"That oughtta give them something else to think about." he said to himself.

Jack passed the squad that had been searching the command trailer, now running towards the inferno.  He honked and waved.  Rifle shots pinged off the vehicle's armor plating, but they might as well have been shooting bb guns.

Jack chuckled as he watched the increasingly futile shots from the men shrinking away in the rearview mirror.  The smile slowly drained from his face as a pair of Chinese-made half-tracks pulled in behind him from alleys on both sides.  Unlike the soldiers, they had big guns, and unlike Jack's team, they actually had people to man them too!

Jack began swerving evasively.  Those were no pea shooters.  Armor piercing rounds too, no doubt.  If he took one of those in the wrong place, this joy ride would be over real fast!

As if to punctuate his thought a shell tore through the rear (otherwise) bulletproof window and showered him with glass.  He felt a trickle of blood running down his cheek but counted himself lucky that he still had a cheek for it to run down!

Jack yanked a tight left around a stack of assorted containers and made down a side road.  The East entrance he'd been heading toward was closer, but he'd never make it with these guys so close on his tail.  It'd have to be downhill to the North entrance instead.

The half-tracks tried to follow his high speed turn but only succeeded in knocking the containers all over, including onto themselves.  They reversed, righted their course, and began to follow, but Jack had a nice lead by now.

It was impossible to say what was stored in the containers, but it was apparently flammable.  The first gunshot from one of the trucks caused both to ignite.

Jack tried to convince himself that this was a good thing because it made it harder for them to aim, but the wall of flaming truck looming in his mirror seemed more like The Headless Horseman chasing Ichabod Crane.

Still, he had a hope that he could make the North gate before they caught up.  At least he thought so until he was finally able to make out the details of gate through his dusty, bouncing windshield.

There were thirty, maybe forty men with guns crouched in front of it and ready to fire.  In addition, there were several large tank traps across the passage looking for all the world like a giant decided to play jacks with iron girders.

Jack noticed for the first time that the encampment was built around a small village, etched into the rolling hills of Kazanka.  Furthermore, he saw that each building was terraced neatly into the next like a set of stair steps.  He really hoped this idea worked.  

But if it didn't at least he'd be dead so they wouldn't be able to capture and torture him.  That was a plus.  Sort of.

He jerked the wheel and swerved past the corner of one house before slamming square into the patio of the next.

A soldier sat in a worn easy chair inside the stolen abode watching an old TV on a cart.  He laughed hilariously as a badly-dubbed Ricky told Lucy she'd have some 'splainin' to do.  The laugh froze on his face as the Humvee smashed through the back door, erased the television from the room, and made its own exit out through the bedroom.

The stunned man turned his head slowly in the direction it had gone.

To Jack it was all a blur of plaster and snapping wood.  He was pleased to realize that his assumption was correct about the way the houses were stair-stepped, and, after punching through the downhill wall of one house, he found himself on the roof of the next.

A hail of gunfire peppered the wall below him from the half-tracks keeping pace on the street.  They were still dripping fire and doing a good job of lighting up half the town.

The edge of the roof loomed near, but Jack knew that he needed to keep his momentum up, lest the heavy vehicle do a nose dive once airborne.  The roof's brick ledge collapsed under the Humvee as it sailed into the air.

It came down hard on the next roof and busted through.  It landed on a queen-sized bed as the rugged offroad tires tore at the quilts and slowly brute-forced its way through the wall.  The long radio aerial was torn loose from its traveling straps and twanged its way out the ragged hole behind him.

Half the house came down this time, but Jack didn't care.  He was too busy worrying about the next jump.  He hit it even faster this time with fervent hopes that he wouldn't break through again.   If that happened he'd be too low and run smack into the guarded fence!

He went aloft again.  This time when he hit, the roof didn't collapse, but the antenna whipped forward and smacked the hood hard.  "Man!  Chipped the paint!" he complained as he put the hammer down for one last all-or-nothing jump to freedom.

Jack felt like a fist the size of a hubcap grabbed all his guts tight and gave them a sharp twist as he sailed thirty feet over the heads of the dumbstruck soldiers and their preciously-guarded gate.

Jack's body wanted to tense up but he consciously forced himself to remain loose.  Breaking bones was far less likely if you could stay loose.  At least, that's what people not currently plummeting towards the ground would tell you.

The hillside was still sloping down enough that Jack wasn't flattened like a pancake.  Although the impact was nothing to write home about the effect of the sudden stop was.

The friction of the ground was so much greater than the air that it was like reaching out and grabbing a parked car.  The Humvee hit, knifed sideways, and started rolling.  The vehicle did four complete cartwheels, but Jack's somersaulting brain did seven.  At least his ride stopped upright.  

He steadied himself against the steering wheel and took a few slow breaths to try and get a handle on himself.  It would take several minutes for the enemy to clear away their road block and come after him.  Enough of a head start to get away, but only if he acted fast.

The engine must have died sometime in the acrobatics and he tried to give it a crank.  Nothing.  He'd give it one last try and then have to make for the woods on foot.  Not something his jarred bones looked forward to.  Nor did he relish having to survive a subzero Siberian night.  "Come on...  Come on..." he said, trying again.  

Nada.

He reached for his buckle while the other hand kept cranking.

It started!

He quickly returned the buckle and shifted back into drive.  There was a logging road a few hundred yards down the hillside from him.

He navigated onto it with one hand and with the other found himself a good, calm, Classical station.


~39~
Reward?

Wendell groaned and started to stir.

"Are you okay?" asked Jane.

He began to feel himself over and then stopped abruptly.

"No!" he whimpered.

Both girls looked concerned.

"I broke my urchin!" he said pitifully, pulling a snapped kabob from his pocket.

Both girls burst out laughing.

"Interesting work out there." they were interrupted by the First Mate who appeared at the doorway behind them.  He must have been sent to deal with the group as the Captain now had more important matters on her mind.

Wendell and Misty both looked at Jane, clearly ascribing her all the credit for the victory.

The Mate looked at Jane as well.  She shivered unconsciously at his gaze.

Worse still, Jane wasn't quite sure about being the center of attention here.  She didn't know whether to feel credit or blame for being a part of the situation.  "Thanks." she said, for lack of any better way to respond.

She had to say something about the murder though.  She knew that.  There was no tactful way to put it so she just blurted it out.

"You killed those men out there!"

"Yes." replied the boy.

He gave the impression he was practicing the Captain's trademark absence of emotion -- trying it on for himself, it seemed.  The result was that Jane's complaint might just as well have been a weather report for all the reaction it got.

"But why?" she persisted.

This question was ignored entirely.  Instead he recited the message that he was tasked with delivering.

"The Queen of All Oceans is not one to overlook a reward when a reward is due." he said officiously.  "Because you helped to rescue her flagship, the venerable Starfish, she hereby returns to you your freedom and when next we surface near land commands that you be safely put ashore."

His speech was ended.  Strangely, Jane thought she'd picked up a tinge of regret in it.  Perhaps it was in deference to the dead men that the sharks were presently tearing to pieces outside?  She hoped so, but somehow doubted it.

"No." came Jane's answer.  She wouldn't accept it.

Nemo's eyes became big and round as saucers.  "No" wasn't a word The Queen usually tolerated, but the fact that someone was rejecting the rare gift of freedom made him all the more amazed.

Wendell and Misty were just as curious as he was, but at least had some inkling about where their friend was going with this, and so kept quiet.

"Listen.  We're in this thing as deep as you are." explained Jane to the Fist Mate.  "We've got our minisub, which is not only a good friend to have (as you've just seen) but more importantly, its sensors can trace the source of this poison.  We know the Landie technologies that the Sea Dragons use, and can help you come up with strategies for how to defeat them."  She hated to offer that last incentive, but it was a selling point not to be overlooked.  "Much as you and your Captain would like to deny it, you need us as much as we need you."

All the advantages that these primitive Landies could bring to the table were hard to deny.  Jane had put together such an irresistable sales pitch that it would've guaranteed a credit card swipe back at faraway Blevens' department store.  Still, the younger Nemo now found himself in a sticky situation.  To the elder Nemo's way of thinking, the sooner the deck was swabbed clean of these uncouth Landies' footprints the happier she'd be.

It's sad to say, but the real tipping point wasn't the welfare of the ship or the Captain, but Sandy Nemo's own desires.  He simply wasn't ready to see them go -- to see one of them go.

The gears in his head churned.  He needed to make the executive decision that he wanted, by exerting more authority than he had, yet also do so in such a way that the Captain would stand by it.  Further still, the Captain's pride meant that she certainly could never accept this "as much as" language that Jane was using.

"Very well." he replied at last, "On behalf of the Queen of All Oceans, and in deference to services rendered, you may remain aboard and participate in the ship's current mission to the best of your ability."

He turned on his heels in crisp, cadet style.  It was a masterful, if short, speech.  As he walked out, he congratulated himself silently on what a fine leader he'd make someday.


"Hey Wendell,"  Jane pulled the strange fellow aside,  "I know Nemo's supposedly our good pal now, and all that stuff, but you know... she's never going to allow any more 'landies' to join in the fight, and we are obviously in over our heads here."

"Yeah, about 20,000 leagues over our heads." he quipped.

She ignored the comment and continued.  "But my point is, we can control the Peanut over wifi, right?"

"Right."

"So that means that there's some kind of computer in between the network router and the subs' control panel, right?"

"Microcontroller, actually.  Raspberry Pi with a custom high amp h-bridge breakout board.  Originally intended to be used for tethered ROV operation."

"Right." Jane answered her own question, having no idea what he was saying.

"So then... you can program it to do things, right?"

"R-right." he hesitated.

In theory it was entirely possible, though he'd never programmed a submarine before.

"In that case, we need you to program it to go straight up, figure out its GPS coordinates, beam those along with a brief message to Dr Puttery, and then come straight back down."

"Right!" he said, smiling, now that he comprehended the plan.

"Also, I have a couple photos he might be interested in if we can spare the bandwidth to send them along too.

"Oh, and better do it quick!  If that sub's not back before the Starfish gets underway, we risk losing it forever!"

"No pressure, though." Wendell lamented.


~40~
Voyage of the Starfish

Much to her chagrin, Captain Nemo held to the unfortunate promise of her foolish son.  The three Landies were given free reign of the Starfish except for secure areas like the command bridge, the engine rooms, and of course the divers' portals.  (She obviously couldn't have certain individuals swimming away without her consent.)

In other words, they were only somewhat less prisoners than before, and yet, with the option of parole.  Probably.  If they survived.

That was a couple of days ago and the three friends had taken to gathering in a forward-facing mess hall with an impressive windowed view of the sea streaking past them.  It turned out to be the best place to discuss their plans in between meals when there were no other crewmen about.  Privacy was a rare commodity on any submarine.  The nearest person here was the cook in the galley at the far end of the hall.  He was too far away to overhear even if he wanted to.  By the clatter of pots and whistling going on they judged that he was far more interested in preparing the next meal than in eavesdropping.

Wendell listened attentively to the goings-ons in the kitchen.  His expression was that of a dog eagerly watching a can of food being opened.

"What do you suppose they've got for dinner?" he asked.

Jane hated to think about it.  So far it was always soup for the crew.  The stuff was weird and fishy, but she was infinitely glad that she didn't have to look at whatever sea creature was in it - or see the creature looking back!

"Soup." said Jane flatly.  Enough of its smell hung in the air to give you a full meal of it without ever eating a spoonful.

"That stuff we had with the Captain,  that was pretty good.  Remember?"

Jane's palm rushed to her forehead.  She rubbed her eyes.  Of course she remembered it.  She wished all up and down to forget it!

Misty remembered that evening as well, but unlike the other two, her memory of the event was centered around the conversation rather than the meal.

"I ought to slap you - hard - for throwing out your insane Atlantis theory in front of the Captain!" Misty threatened Wendell.

"Yeah, but did you see her reaction?" Jane came to his defense.

"Yeah, that was weird." said Wendell absently, not looking away from the kitchen.  (His full attention was too severely needed to be able to take his eyes off it for even an instant.)

"Of course!  Captain Nemo!" realized Jane with a snap of her fingers.

"What?  The Captain is Captain Nemo?  As in, the Captain Nemo?"  Wendell hadn't fully digested that critical fact up until now.  The revelation was strange enough to finally tear his gaze away from the kitchen.  

"Yeah, I thought you knew that." said Jane.

"You mentioned it, but I thought you were just jokin' around."

After it sank in for a few more seconds he added, "Either way, what does that have to do with Atlantis?"

"Well, The Captain Nemo from the book knew where Atlantis was!" exclaimed Jane.

"Okay.  Where is it then?" asked Wendell.

"I don't know.  But depending on how true to life the story is, I'm pretty sure that Nemo does."  

And then, "Wait.  Wendell, you did all the research on this stuff.  How come you don't know where Atlantis is?"

"Hard to say for sure." said Wendell, noticeable weariness in his voice.  "Plato and other ancient guys wrote about Atlantis like it was a real, actual place, so I always figured there's a good chance it once existed.  That's about where the details end though.  Some experts say it was located outside of the Strait of Gibraltar, some say inside.  Some people think it's another name for Crete or Sicily or even the Canary Islands.  A few even think it could be located near India (though that one seems a stretch to me)."

"Well, given all the choices, what do you think?" she persisted.

"Me?  I don't think it's any of those places.  Because, frankly, we checked!  But that only makes sense if you think about it."

"How come?"

"Well, it is called the 'lost island of Atlantis', right?  So if it was right where somebody - anybody - expected it to be..."

Jane saw where he was going with this.  "... then it wouldn't be 'lost' anymore."  she completed the sentence.

He nodded in agreement.

They all watched as a school of colorful fish tried to keep up with the speeding Starfish for a few moments.  The fish eventually lost the race, but it was a fun show to watch.

As they continued looking out the viewport at the ocean zooming by, they all shared the same, silent thought.  

Misty put it into words.

"I sure hope the others find us."


~41~
Rescue Team

"Michael!  I have word from the Peanut!" came the excited voice of Dr Puttery over the uplink.

His wife was, as usual, by Michael's side listening in on the same satellite feed with her matching wireless headset.

"What Peanut?"

"Ah, yes, sorry.  I neglected to explain.  That term is what Wendell and the girls have taken to calling the submersible from the Cheapskate."

He continued,  "Anyway.  Word in from the three is that the Gypsy's been sunk by a nasty group of characters calling themselves the Sea Dragons.  The three are in agreement that these are probably the same scum we're after.  They're currently in pursuit."

"In the mini-sub!" exclaimed Michael.

"N-No." stammered Puttery.  "That's the point where the message turns bizarre.  They claim they're aboard the battle-sub of Captain Nemo and en route to Atlantis."

"Oxygen deprivation?" asked Mrs Moast.  Concern was evident in her voice.

"Possibly." admitted Puttery.  The same thought had crossed his mind more than once.

He continued, "Regardless, you'd better get there as quickly as possible!  They uploaded their last GPS coordinates as an attachment to the message.  I'm sending them to your ship's Captain now."

"No sense searching for mollusks anymore.  Those kids might have slid of their dinghy, but they're certainly onto something." said Michel.

"One more thing," said Dr Puttery, "they also uploaded a picture of the ship who sank the Gypsy.  I've got a job currently running to try and find every occurrence of her registry and markings within the CIA's database at Langley.  It'll take some time though, because those guys never throw anything out."

"And the CIA knows about this search?" Michael asked skeptically.

Puttery's hesitation served as the answer.

Michael decided that he should have known better than to ask.

"Well, let me know.  Moast out."  Michael tapped a button on the side of his headset which dropped the com link.

The Moasts knew that Dr Puttery would come through in time.  He always did.  In the mean time Michael's foremost task was to rally the rest of the team for the rescue attempt.

He tapped the side of his headset again.  Doing so initiated a voice-command dial.  "McCready!"

There was one electronically-simulated ring and then someone answered.

"Tis McCready." said her voice.

"We've got good news!" said Michael.  But before he could elaborate she spoke.

"I wish mine was as good."

Interrupted, Michael changed gears to follow the new conversation.  "Why?  What happened?"

"Y' remember our pal Fong?"

"The thug that nearly killed Kurt and Wendell?  How could we forget!" said Melinda, recalling the name from one of their past adventures.

"Yeah, well, turns out he's gone legit."

"Oh?  Well that's good news." replied Melinda.

"No.  Not really.

"It looks like he's shaved an' taken a bath all right,  but only enough t' clean up for the cameras.  He's brachin' into national politics on the Chinese mainland.  Behind the scenes though, he's only redoubled his dirty dealin's.  

"By kissin' babies by day and bumpin' off the competition by night, he's positioned himself to become some kinda super-politician.  Gonna crown himself Emperor of China before the weekend, 'less I miss m' guess."

"What!??" exclaimed both of the Moasts.

"It gets worse.  Soon as he's got the backing..."  Ms McCready hesitated.  She didn't know how to deliver this next part.

"He plans to invade Japan, Michael!"

"That's crazy!  I hope he gets himself killed!" Melinda interjected.  Her harsh tone was more than called for.

"Normally I'd agree, t' be sure, but all my... um... 'sources', confirm he's got a good shot at actually makin' it all happen."

"I'll pass the info from your sources along to some of my sources and we'll see what sticks." said Michael.  "But as for right now lets concentrate on my news.  We've found Wendell and the girls!  We're chartering a leer jet now.  You want a lift?"

He glanced over at his wife.  She was, indeed, already on the phone to their preferred airstrip in Sydney.  He hadn't asked her to.  She just knew.  The couple had an amazing bond that way.

"No thank ye." Ms McCready replied.  "I can find m' own ride."

"Shall I wait for you?" she continued.

"No.  Get on their trail and we'll hook up when we can."

The two teams were about equally as far away from the coordinates uploaded from the Peanut and the Moasts had access to the fastest transport that money could buy.  Still, nobody in the conversation doubted that McCready's "ride" would get her there first.  She somehow had her ways...


~42~
Refugee of Atlantis

The Starfish slowed as it presumably neared the lost city of Atlantis.  At least, that is, the three friends were still working under the assumption that Atlantis was where they were headed.  It was a bit of a leap of logic, but the reaction of the Captain when they'd first mentioned it, made it a reasonably likely guess.

They were in their usual spot in the mess hall, watching the sea whip by.

Without warning the ship flung everybody forward to collide with whatever was in front of them.  Or, from a physics standpoint, it would be more appropriate to say that everybody continued to fling themselves forward as the sub's propulsion system suddenly reversed direction, stopping the monstrous machine with unbelievable speed.

The best sub in all the land-based navies of the world couldn't have matched that maneuver without their engines tearing their own guts out.  These undersea people, it seemed, had no end of tricks.

Wendell dragged himself to his feet.  The old injury from the undersea battle was nearly healed but the scab was now torn open again.  Blood poured down his head and he looked like a mess.

"Yikes! Who put on the brakes?" he said, touching the wound experimentally.

Jane came off battered but unscathed, but Misty was trapped under an overturned table and needed the others' help to get free.  She too was unharmed.

They heard footsteps running on the metal decking outside and followed in the direction they were headed to see what the problem might be.  On his way out of the mess hall, Wendell grabbed a stack of napkins and pressed them to his forehead to try to staunch the bleeding.

As they ran down the corridor, they were frozen in their tracks by the commands of the ships' surgeon.

"Make way, make way!" he shouted.

They pressed themselves tight against the walls to clear a path for him.  He rushed past with two other men carrying a limp Starfish diver between them.  At least his gear looked like the Starfish's equipment.  The man's clothes were different though.  He was shirtless and wore what could've passed for loose exercise pants in world above.

The doctor's sick bay was only a few paces past where they stood, so they followed him inside.

Two assistants laid the man on the operating table while the doctor walked briskly to the sink and washed his hands.  He scrubbed for a duration somewhere balanced between what was regulation and what was necessary to get back to his patient the quickest.

The doc reached into a drawer and pulled out a pair of heavy duty metal shears which he used to attack the copper band of the man's dive mask.  Jane shuddered to think what other uses the hefty tool would have in an operating room.

Captain Nemo roughly shoved aside the three friends and gawking crew members as she forced her way into the room.  Nobody complained.  It was a Captain's prerogative.

The doctor was just slipping the helmet off the man on the table when the Captain began questioning him.

"What happened?" she demanded.

The man couldn't speak.  He wheezed instead, clutching desperately at her sleeve.  The doctor pressed a stethoscope to his bare chest.

"No water in the lungs." he said.

He turned.

"Muscle relaxer!" he demanded of a nearby nurse, snapping his fingers and holding out his hand.

Into it, she slapped an enormous syringe made of glass and gleaming stainless steel.  It looked more like something for a horse than a human.  The doc rammed it unceremoniously into the man's arm and swiftly but smoothly depressed the plunger.

The wheezing of the injured man abated enough for him to speak, although his words were only partially intelligible and only came with great effort.

"Dead!  Dead!  All dead!" he babbled.

"Are you from Atlantis?" the Captain tried to coax some sense out of him.

"Atlantis." he agreed.  "The people -- everyone -- all dead..."

He returned to muttering "dead" over and over again.  His time was running out. Information was going to be slim.

"The Queen too?  What about the Queen?" the Captain fairly shook the man.

Everyone expected a half-sane "dead" to be the answer.  They were surprised that he actually paused and considered this for a moment.

"Captured." he replied instead.

The man grabbed his chest in an agonized howl.

The doctor turned to his nurse.  "Give me... give me a... a..." he snapped his fingers, racking the encyclopedia of his mind for anything to try.

It was too late.  The patient's screams cut off abruptly.  His hand released the Captain's sleeve and fell limply to the table.  Dead.

"More poison?" the Captain asked the doctor quietly.

He nodded.

"Helmsman, all ahead full!" she bellowed, pushing her way out and toward the bridge.

Jane followed.  The Captain stayed aloof from the Landies thus far, but now that she had her in her sights, Jane had a bone to pick.  She stuck on Nemo's heels all the way down the corridor to the command bridge.

"Captain?  About the Sea Dragon soldiers..." she began.

Nemo either didn't hear or pretended not to hear.  The off-limits bridge doorway came and went.  Jane was so close behind that the guards mistook her for being with the Captain and let her pass.

The bridge was an amazing new scene but there was no time to take stock of it.

"Captain!  You can't just murder people like that!" she fairly shouted the accusation.

Every eye turned.  Every eye except the Captain's.  Instead her hand shot out, grabbed Jane by the neck, and lifted her off the deck.  Her strength was amazing!

"You!"  She pointed at an operator with her free hand.  "The Territorial Treaty with Atlantis is canceled as of this moment.  Set a course for it as straight as she goes.  Best speed and then some!"

"And you..."  She turned to Jane at last.

Jane's feet dangled inches above the deck plate.  Her hands instinctively clutched at the Captain's as she tried to fight for air.

"How dare you question the Captain of this ship?"

Black spots were beginning to appear at the edges of Jane's vision from lack of oxygen.  She felt almost grateful when the Captain flung her down against a bulkhead.

"Get her out of here!" she ordered a guard near the door.

Both guards responded and dragged Jane roughly to her feet and towards the exit.

"Wait!"  Her mind searched desperately for something that would keep her there.  Something that would change the rules of the game to her favor.  

"Treaty"; the word flashed back into her mind.  Treaty... Treaty...  "You can't just charge into the middle of the city in your war sub!"   

"Can't" was probably a bad word choice to use with the Queen.  Jane softened her tone to, "Please." and added, "Let us go in instead."

"Halt." the Captain sighed to the guard.

"You really think you three can take out the Sea Dragons alone?"  The question was partially rhetorical and completely un-answerable.

The Captain chewed on the concept though.  If it worked, this could solve her political dilemma.  Even in the worst case, the Landies would be poisoned to death, and therefore, out of her hair.  Not an unwelcome scenario either way.  The biggest problem with the plan was that she didn't have the time to mess around for long.

"Very well." said the Captain.  "Tomorrow morning I'll put you in your... that thing of yours... on the outskirts of Atlantis.  From there you'll have twenty-four hours to get in, rout the Sea Dragons and get out.  I can't afford any more delay than that.

"Dismissed."

Jane was allowed to walk out under her own power.  She rubbed her neck as she went.  She really hoped this impromptu plan was a good move.


~43~
The Real Nemo

Jane approached the viewing ports of the mess hall where the three usually gathered.  This time she arrived alone.

After having heard the plan, Misty and Wendell knew that they had a big day ahead of them tomorrow and decided to turn in early.  Jane knew it too, of course, but had a hard time sleeping.  She was all in knots with the anxiety that her hasty words might prove to be the end of them.

Maybe the instant they gained the freedom of the Peanut they should get out of Dodge and make straight for the surface.  Nobody would blame them if they did.  

But still, they had a job to do.  The goal of their seemingly hopeless quest was now nearly within their grasp.  The all-important mollusks they had set out to find could lie right on the other side of that ridge she now saw before her eyes.  Being so close to their objective, how could they possibly turn back now?

She tried to put all that out of her mind in an attempt to quiet the anxiety bubbling away within her gut.  She watched the dark shadows of creatures swim by in the silent night.  There was less to see, but even at night this underwater world had its own kind of beauty.  "Deep calleth unto deep at the noise of thy waterspouts.  All thy waves and thy billows are gone over me."  The memorized words themselves poured out like waterspouts.

Jane was so lost in thought that she didn't seen the figure standing in the shadows near the viewport.  The unknown person brought a cup of tea to a pair of lips and then set it back on its saucer with an almost inaudible tink.  

Jane heard it and whirled to face the person.  It was Captain Nemo. 

"Yet the LORD will command his lovingkindness in the daytime, and in the night his song shall be with me, and my prayer unto the God of my life."

Jane must have given her a quizzical look.

"You're surprised? 

"Well don't be.  Down here we strive to make a new world, free from all the encumbrances of the one above.  The first Nemo set us on a new course.  The brand of 'civilization' that your kind practices is a cruel and selfish imitation of what humanity was created for.  But down here it's a clean slate.  We started over from scratch and forged an all new world for ourselves:  Free from wars.  Free from trouble.  A purer form of art and science that doesn't rage against nature, but cooperates with it.  Even Christianity itself has been cleansed of all vain traditions and man-made imperfections.  It's a perfect world down here, Jane.  And that's why the Landies and their poisons - both physical and moral - are unwelcome here."

" 'A perfect world'?  'A perfect faith'?  Did you forget that I stood there and watched those unarmed men being stabbed to death!??"  exclaimed Jane.  

Yesterday she was dying for an opportunity to bring up the matter with the Captain.  Since this morning's incident though she wasn't sure whether what she was saying was wise or not.  But there it was.  Said.

She rubbed the bruises on her throat.  Nemo saw it.

Thankfully the Captain didn't react in the same way as she did back on the bridge.  Instead, she changed the subject.  (Or so it seemed.)

"Nervous?"  she asked.

Jane nodded slowly, not sure what that had to do with anything.

"Me too.  If it wasn't for this stuff..."  She held up the tea cup.  "My ulcers would have long ago burned through my stomach like a boiler in meltdown."

The Captain was talking like a real human being for a change and Jane didn't know how to take it.  

"You know how many subs I command?" she asked nobody in particular.  
And then she answered herself.  "Eighty-three.  

And you know how many villagers depend on me for survival?  
Over three hundred thousand at last count.

"And you wonder why I drink this stuff."  She took another sip.

She switched thoughts again.  "Jane, did you explore the area around where your ship sank?"

Jane shook her head.  "We never got a chance to."

"Well, your friend was right when he said that the Sea Dragons wanted to own both land and sea.  The reef in that area fed a small colony of my people.

"They poisoned it.

"All of it.

"People, plants, everything.  Not even the crustaceans could survive.  Nothing but pain and corpses left for miles.  Nothing even fit for fish food.  And it won't be for decades!

"You might think me a monster for what I've done, but imagine if that kind of devastation ever happened on the streets your home town.  Now, you tell me how willing you'd be to let those responsible live on!"

Jane almost sympathized now.  She still didn't agree, necessarily, but sympathized.

The Captain wasn't finished.  Her voice grew dark and seething.  "I'm the only one who can stop them.  And stop them, I shall!  This is my vow:  I will not stop until every last one of those Sea Dragons is fed to the sharks!"

A cold shudder ran down Jane's spine.  Nemo left no room for uncertainty in her deadly oath.

After the Captain calmed herself with many more sips of tea she continued.

"Jane, the entire ocean is depending on me, and me alone.

"Now you know why I drink this stuff."  Another sip served to prove out her claim.

"But your son Sandy?  He's..."  Jane tried to be optimistic.

"... not fit to command a soap bubble.  I know.

"Of course he'll grow into it in time.  But for now I have to keep him on a mighty short leash."  With that she drained the last drop.

"Well.  Time for me to get to bed.  You too, actually.  Be careful out there tomorrow."  

She turned to walk away but only got three paces before making a show as if something else occurred to her.  (A classic Columbo move, Jane thought.)  "Oh, and one more thing.  You overheard what that Atlantican we rescued said, right?"

Jane nodded.

"If you can't drive out the Sea Dragons, that I understand.  But you must keep a sharp lookout for their Queen.  It's of highest importance!  Bringing her back with you means more than the city itself.  Got it?"

Jane nodded again.  It wasn't good enough.

"You got it?" the Captain asked again.

"Rescuing the Queen is the most important thing.  Yes.  I got it."

"Good."  she said and finished her departure.

Jane was surprised at the civility she'd just experienced.  And the Captain... executing people one minute, quoting scripture the next.  What a mixed bag she was!

Then there was also this underwater utopia thing that they'd shunned the rest of the human race to build.  Nemo made it sound great and all, but was it really everything that she claimed?  From what Jane had seen of this new world it was every bit as full of violence and strife as her own.  And she had the bruises to prove it!

Wasn't that always the way though?  Whenever somebody started dreaming of building paradise on Earth, watch out!

Countless lives were lost when Nero burned his own capitol city to the ground for the sake of his dream of rebuilding a perfect Rome.  Josef Stalin had inaugurated plans of achieving Socialism's "Radiant Future" by the bloodshed of untold millions.  (A single man whose death toll rivaled every other megalomaniac before him, combined!)  And yes, there were people living in the United States today with such beautiful dreams that God forbid they'd ever gain enough power to make a try for them!  The bloodbath would be unbelievable!

It was like the Tower of Babel was a grand idea that man's ego refused to let go of.  Every time he tried to rebuild it taller and more perfect.  And as a result, each time exponentially more innocent life was lost in its foregone collapse.

Well, whether the Captain was right or wrong about these things, she definitely had one thing pegged.  Jane really did need her sleep.  Tomorrow was going to be a long day!


~44~
Secrets of Atlantis

The three friends crammed themselves into the Peanut like sardines.  No.  Scratch that.  The sardines were outside and had plenty of room.

The problem was only compounded by the bulky diving gear that they had to take along in order to enter and leave the Peanut.  Wendell's gear was SCUBA and, though unwieldy, was elegant in comparison to the suits from the Starfish that the girls were wearing.

Both the suits that Nemo's divers used and those of the Landies were derived from the same "Rouquayrol Apparatus" popular in the 1860's.  From there they had developed down completely different lines.

The Landies' SCUBA technology was refined to near perfection by the brilliant mind of the famous undersea explorer Jacques Cousteau.  It was light and efficient and had built-in buoyancy compensation.

Nemo's suits weren't lightened much from the original design but had one important refinement that was an absolute necessity for prolonged undersea life:  They could produce their own oxygen for nearly a month before needing to be recharged.

The even bigger reason that the Peanut was so cramped was that it was originally designed for only one person, a fold down jump-seat behind the driver for a second.  Both girls crammed onto this and only got two thirds of a bum apiece on it.  Wendell alone occupied the pilot's seat in front of them, but he was so tall that he was no less cramped.  The steering wheel was between his long legs and he almost looked like a frog driving.

He consoled himself of the discomfort by picturing the local wildlife on a dinner plate.

"Oh, yum!  Look at that Herring!" said Wendell as a fish darted by.

"And some shrimp over there!" he added.

The sub crested the ridge overlooking the lost city.  It was still too far away to make out much detail, but the discovery was quite unmistakable.

"Knock it off with the seafood buffet and come up with a way to get us in there without being spotted." demanded Misty.

Wendell was way ahead of her.  "Easy.  We'll sneak in under cover of that seaweed."  He pointed to a slowly-waving field of fronds on the outskirts of the town.

They descended into it and inched towards the besieged city.  The going was painfully slow, but the risk of blowing their cover by jostling the plants was too great to move any faster.

Jane used the tedious progress as an opportunity to photograph everything they were seeing.  As they drifted along, she suddenly uttered an exclamation and pointed out the window so the others could see what she was so excited about.

"That." she indicated.  "It looks like some kind of farming tool."

Indeed, it did.  It was almost a hoe, only with a ridiculously distorted head that would have rendered it unusable for anything on land.  But presumably the alien undersea physics made it just the tool for the job down here.  (Whatever that job might be, nobody aboard could hope to guess.)

A few more man-made items littered the sea floor nearby, but soon passed out of sight as they putted along.

Brushing a large seaweed frond caused a cone shaped snail with a gorgeously-patterned shell to drop onto the glass.  Then all of a sudden there was another.  And another...

Soon, the outside of the sub was covered with dozens of the things.

One of them slimed its way down across the sub's window.

"There, you see?" said Misty, as if concluding some important, longstanding debate.

" 'Icky'." she tapped the glass briskly with the back of her hand.

Jane smiled at the inside joke.  

Wendell completely missed the point of this and carried on with his all-you-can-eat-seafood monologue.  "Actually, they're not bad with some garlic butter. Maybe a pinch of salt."

"Better steer clear of this escargot." cautioned Jane.  She got as close to the glass as she could and snapped several perfect close-up shots.  No doubt Dr Puttery would thank her for getting this specimen on film.

Then for Wendell's benefit, she pointed at the side display that Misty must have already seen.  On the screen was a chemical concentration bar graph, overlaid with a pop-up warning, "Mollusk Poison detected!", flashing in bright red letters.

Misty was right.  Even if they weren't covered in the most deadly neurotoxin known to man, Jane still wouldn't want to touch the slimy things.  She did, however, admire the glistening tapestry of colors on their shells.  Despite their lethality they had a brand of beauty all their own.  Their shells appeared to be made of shimmering copper flagstones set in golden mortar. 

Her photos of it would even make wonderful postcards, so long as you didn't know that you were looking at one the deadliest creatures on the planet.  

The sub trundled on, leaving Jane's mind to wander.  The struggling Atlantican from the Starfish still haunted her.  Who knows?  Maybe he was poisoned in this very...

Then the realization hit her all at once.

"This used to be a farm!" exclaimed Jane.

"Yeah, we noticed that earlier." said Misty, though she somehow knew that her friend had more going on inside her head.

"So, these Atlantis farmers were out growing seaweed..."

"Mmm.  Sushi rolls!" interjected the walking stomach.

Jane ignored him and continued, "... when along comes a poisonous mollusk..."

" 'They're all dead!' " Misty followed the thought to its conclusion.  In doing so she sounded almost like the poor Atlantican himself!

"The Sea Dragons didn't conquer Atlantis by battle." continued Jane.  "They poisoned it!

"They wanted Atlantis' fertile fields to feed their snails on, and decided the easiest thing to do was to just plop the mollusks right where they wanted them, and let their natural poison fight the battle for them."

It was a heavy thought - one which they pondered in silence for a moment.

"Slight correction." added Wendell in his rare, serious tone.

"What's that?"

"You said 'natural poison'.  It couldn't be.  You heard what the Captain said their testing did to the reefs near the Sea Gypsy."  (The Sea Gypsy had ceased to be something mobile in their minds and was now a fixed landmark instead.)  "Anything that devastating to sea life would've been heard of before."

Jane realized where he was going with this.  "They're breeding them!"

"That, or somebody got a 'Junior Gene Splice Kit' for Christmas." added Wendell.
 
With that happy thought they drifted into the open.

It happened so fast, that they were fully exposed before Wendell could slam the motors into reverse and duck them back inside the seaweed.  

"That was close!" he breathed, along with nervous chuckle and a wishful, "I hope nobody saw that!"

The mistake provided a brief glimpse of a magnificent Grecian-style city of which they were on the outskirts.  Unlike what they had envisioned in their imaginations, it was not in ruins like so many tourist attractions topside.  The buildings were all intact and even 'modern' - for whatever the word meant down here.

The emptiness that prevailed in the ghost town was not like an ancient city whose inhabitants died off centuries earlier, but more like a holocaust whose people were killed only weeks or months ago.  The former would've made the place feel like a museum.  This was more like a scene from a horror movie.  Only it was real!  And more than that, the real, live psychopath was still in there somewhere!!


"Should we go out and have a look around?" asked Wendell, reaching for his dive mask.

"No!" said both girls decisively.

"What?"

"The outside of the sub..." began Misty.

"...is covered with poisonous mollusks." completed Jane.

"Dr Puttery said the poison can soak in though your skin," added Misty, "so even our breathing masks would be useless in keeping it out."

"What else can we do?" asked a frustrated Wendell, "Cruise the Peanut through the streets of town?  Sounds like a good way to get noticed and clobbered."

Jane shrugged and looked at Misty.  Misty shrugged and looked back.

"Wait." said Jane.

She had noticed something.  It was there, somewhere in the periphery of her mind.  The glimpse of the city had been fleeting , but even so, her keen senses had seen... seen... what?

"I've got it!" she announced, finally.

"There's a large building a few blocks in on the left side with a Sea Dragon sub floating outside.  If we can shake off the mollusks on the way there, we can slide in behind the building, sneak through to the other side, and steal it.  They'll let us go wherever we want once we look like one of their own!"

"Wow!  Good plan!" complimented Wendell.  "Of course that means whoever drove it here will need to take a little nap."  He glanced at Misty as he said it.

She stretched her tiny neck and shoulder muscles.  "Sounds fun."

Jane still couldn't picture it.

Moments later the tiny Peanut tore along towards the back of the building at the best speed it could manage.  The speed was necessary to minimize the chance of being seen, but more importantly, to shake off all the poisonous hitchhikers.  With a low thunk it bumped into the rear of the structure near what appeared to be the building's back entrance.

All eyes were on the poison display.  If the didn't start falling it meant they still had a little hitchhiker hanging on somewhere.  The only remedy at that point would be more cruising around and creating a significant risk of detection.  All three held their breath.

None exhaled until the bars on the graph began to slowly fall.

The several minutes that it took for the poison to dissipate gave them all plenty of time to don their dive gear and thoroughly check that each piece of equipment was functioning properly.

Once the toxin reached a save level, Wendell hit the "pressure cycle" button.  As the tiny pumps did their work, water began to fill the sub.  The Peanut was far too small to have an airlock, so instead it let the sea directly into the cabin itself.  The controls and circuits were all made extremely waterproof with this functionality in mind.

As soon as the pressure equalized, the plastic dome was released and swung forward on its hinges.

The three all peered out cautiously, looking for any of the deadly snails who might have somehow remained.  None were seen so they drifted cautiously out.  

The three began swimming towards the door, but one or two kicks later Wendell turned and went back.  

Upon returning to the Peanut, he opened a medium-sized compartment marked "Emergency", releasing a flurry of trapped bubbles.  He hefted out a breadbox-sized cube with a red ribbon dangling from one end.  As the girls watched, he sharply yanked the ribbon loose and let the object float away.  It slowly formed itself into the shape of a rubber boat as it rose up and away in the direction of the surface.

Both of the girls were curious what that stunt was all about and why he ditched an important piece of safety equipment.  They had no reasonable means of asking though, so let it slide for the time being.

As they made their way toward the building's rear entrance, they passed a rubbish pile.  It seemed to consist mainly of construction junk, both old and new, from the time of the city's renovation.

They really didn't have time to stop, but as they swam past the heap Jane's keen eye spotted a small cache of artifacts amid the debris.  She deviated slightly from the others' path and quickly pawed through the pile.  If this was going to be their one and only visit to the fabled city, she needed something to prove it to Michael and the others.  She grabbed a few unidentifiable brass things and then spotted a broken orange vause identical to the one Wendell had been waving around back in California.  She shoved it all into a mesh dive bag and then kicked fast to catch up with the others.

They all arrived at the door together.  Wendell turned the handle and pushed.  It swung open easily.  Honestly they didn't know what to expect inside.  They assumed that there must be some kind of airlock to enable people to enter the building.  Since it would be made by the undersea people rather than their own Landie technology, they were afraid they wouldn't know how to operate it.  Even that pessimism was a gross underestimation of what they found. 

As they finned inside they swam through an invisible magnetic field.  This detected their presence and a Ruhmkorff light in the ceiling slowly warmed to life.

Across one entire side of the otherwise empty room was a pink blobby substance.  That was all there was.  No controls, no drains, no nothing.  Just a blob.  Maybe this entrance was sealed off by some sort of foam plug?  Maybe this door led nowhere and they'd have to figure out how to get around to the front unseen?

As they made one last pass looking for clues, Jane swam too close to the blob and triggered the edge of another detection field.  Another light turned itself on, this time from the other side of it.  Now that the pink stuff was lit from behind they could see that it was somewhat translucent.  Through the irregular distortion, they could even make out a hallway beyond.

All of a sudden the pink stuff took on a lot more importance in everyone's mind.  Jane touched it experimentally.  Her finger indented deeply.  Then she tried to push her arm inside.  It was like shoving her hand through thick bread dough, but it seemed to work.  The others watched wordlessly as she worked up the nerve and forced her entire body into it.

It was difficult, but entirely possible to walk through.  The pressure was intense, pushing in hard and tight over every square inch of her body.  

For a moment Jane was worried that she'd made a mistake and this was some sort of trap or some sea creature's stomach.  But as she pushed through the four feet or so of the stuff, she suddenly found herself laying sprawled on the floor of the hallway beyond.  The resistance unexpectedly gave up its grasp all at once, teaching her the same physics lesson as a tug-of-war where the other party lets go.

Soon Wendell and Misty were by her side.  They each removed their SCUBA masks.  Several sets of the sea peoples' dive gear already lined the walls on pegs, but they couldn't leave theirs.  They'd need them on the other side.

The team walked as quietly as possible down the corridor, expecting the mercenaries to appear at any moment.  Strangely, none did.  They arrived at a similar, though more ornately framed goo portal on the other end.  A couple sets of modern SCUBA gear on the floor next to it testified that they were not alone in this building.

"Well that was easy." said Jane.

"Not really.  Now have to go back and find the bad guys." said Wendell.

"What?  Why?  Let's just go." she urged.
