"They must have some kind of walkie-talkies or something." explained Misty.  "If they find their sub missing and radio ahead, we're fish bait!" 

That made sense.  Jane had thought they were lucky in not encountering any enemies.  Now she was convinced it wasn't luck but the opposite.  Now they had to search the entire place, and what with doorways,  blind corridors, and the works, there were a million places they could get jumped along the way.

But what else could they do? 

Slowly, from the end where they stood, they began a thorough search of the strange building.

As they rounded the corner to the umpteenth side passage, Wendell quickly put out an arm to stop their progress.  He held a finger to his lips for silence.  Then he looked directly at Misty, held up two fingers, and pointed.

She nodded.  Then she slunk noiselessly around the corner like a shadow.  A moment later the other two followed at a distance.

Misty silently crept up on the guard like the mouse she was, and then, with a spring, she held him in a headlock, slowly cutting off the flow of blood to his brain.

"Is that what they call a 'combat multiplier'?" whispered Jane to Wendell.

"Uh huh.  You see, Misty's just a small girl.  Therefore, to take out somebody bigger and stronger, she uses a 'force multiplier', or as some say, a 'combat multiplier'."

"There are lots of different varieties of combat multipliers:  from weapons, to martial arts, to simply the element of surprise.  Basically anything that gives you an advantage greater than or equal to your natural disadvantage."

"Shouldn't we help her?" asked Jane a little concerned.

"Correct.  Greater numbers is another type of 'force multiplier'."

"No.  I mean, shouldn't we help her right now?"

He looked in Misty's direction.  She was already on to the second guard.

"Would you ask The Incredible Hulk if he needed any help carrying his groceries?"

Finally the last guard dropped to the floor.

Misty knelt on one knee, panting.  Sweat pored from her dainty face and patted against the floor like raindrops.

"Not very well trained." observed Wendell.

"Yeah." she agreed.  "Still, it's nice to see some action again."

Having neutralized the enemy, they turned and began walking back towards the main entrance again.

"I still don't believe it." said Jane under her breath at the thought of her small friend taking out two mercenaries, singlehandedly.

"You should see Ms McCready!" said Wendell.

"She's scary!" added Misty.

"Wait a minute!" a thought hit Jane like a brick wall.

"Those guys were guarding something.  We'd better go back and check those rooms."

"We're kindof in a hurry here..." objected Misty.

Wendell nodded in agreement with that.  "Yeah, I thought you were the one who wanted to get out of here."

"Come on, guys." begged Jane.  "One quick look and then we'll go get the sub and cruise mainstreet."

They checked one door, which was empty.

The second they came to was locked, but after a little bit of violence it opened just as easily as the first.

There, in the back of the cold cell, was the prone figure of an adult woman.  They moved closer to see if she was sleeping but stopped short.  Every square inch of her skin was torn, or swollen, or both.  Though her face was a mass of one bruise atop another, and the light was quite bad, the features were unmistakable.

"Nemo!" blurted Jane.

It was logistically impossible that she could've arrived before them let alone with enough time to get such a beating.

"Do I... know..." the woman forced the words out despite her broken ribs, "...you?"

"I'm Jane, and these are my friends.  We came here aboard the Starfish."  She hoped that dropping the name of the ship would shake something loose about who exactly this person was.

"The... sweet Starfish..." she said.  She sounded far away and a little delirious.

Then suddenly the information hit home.  "Where is... is my cousin?" she asked, trying to look around.

"She sent us." said Wendell.  "What happened here?"

"Sea Dragons... kill us all... Queen of Oceans... Queen of Atlantis..." her eyes were glazing over more and more by the minute.  It was clear that her time on this world was short and every question needed to count.

"Where are the Sea Dragons?" asked Jane.  As she did, she tried to make eye contact and keep the woman around as long as possible.

"Dome...  Landie dome... Near Cleito Palace..."

Her eyes fluttered and closed for what was, for all intents, to be the last time.

The trio of explorers stood, fueled by a newfound anger for what the Sea Dragons had done to this poor lady.  They turned to walk out, but spun back around when they heard the voice of a ghost.

"Wait!" commanded the woman.

They were astonished by the force of the command and even more astonished when  they turned to see her sitting up.

She was fishing inside her clothing for something.  She withdrew it and held it out to Jane.  The ornate ring she held made her hand shake as if it weighed seventy pounds.

"Take this... neyrrugh!" the woman groaned, grabbing her chest.  Something bad happened to her insides with all that moving around.

The strange, choppy, message came filtered through a combination of adrenaline and pain.

"... secret! ... hide it ... Don't let her get it...

"Atlantis must be free!" the last decree of a mighty leader rumbled from her throat.

At last her race was run.


The three retraced their steps to the front entrance and made their way to the enemy sub.  

Its controls were not labeled in English so it took some amount of fiddling until Misty and Wendell figured out how to cycle the pumps and re-pressurize the cabin with oxygen.

When she was able to finally remove her mask, Jane said distantly,  "That poor woman."

"Yeah.  Did you see what they did to her?" added Misty.

Wendell simply didn't want to talk about it and decided to occupy his mind by learning the controls.

"What did she give you?" asked Misty.

"A ring."

"What are you going to do with it?"

"I don't know."  said Jane, realizing it was a good question that hadn't occurred to her.  "Give it to whoever's in charge of Atlantis.  ...I guess.  ...If there's anybody left."

"If there are, I'm sure they're too busy to care much."  Wendell finally spoke.

"It would at least let them know what happened to their Queen."  Misty corrected.

Wendell conceded the point with a shrug.  He was pretty sure he knew what the controls did by now and was ready to try his hand.

The group in their pilfered sub made their way toward the palace of Cleito.  Not that they knew what that was exactly, but they were presumably looking for a prominent castle-like structure with a large man-made (or, 'Landie-made' would be a better term) dome sticking out of it.  Something like that shouldn't be hard to find.

The assumption was quickly proved true.  The palace was obvious and they made their way along a main thoroughfare leading directly to it.

As they neared, they saw that there were several moats ringing the outside of the palace walls.  The walls, obviously, were made in a day and age when people couldn't simply swim over the top them.  Furthermore, the moats seemed particularly insane down here.  Though they were certainly full of water, so was everything else!

Beyond this, they found the Landie pressure dome and set a course for it.  As they puttered through the strange new sites they passed a cage with an enormous jellyfish the size of a greyhound bus!

"I don't suppose that's another 'poisonous mollusk'?" asked Wendell.

"I think jellyfish are considered a mollusk, and yes, some jellies are poisonous." lectured Misty, "But, call it a hunch, I suspect they were trying for size, not venom with this big boy."

"Glad to know they have other surprises in the works." said Jane, laying the sarcasm on thick.

The small submersible made for the underwater dome at full speed.  Jane's plan seemed to be working perfectly.  

That is, until the cabin was, suddenly and without warning, torn in half with the wrenching sound of tortured steel.  The three friends found themselves in bare open water with no mask on...  

Suffocating on the bottom of the sea.


~45~
Tuna Plant

Deep within the mainframe's whirring harddrive, the last bit of information was delivered up, processed, and translated into a form that the strange, illogical humans could read.  Having finished the meat of its job the computer's process had one last item of business to attend to before the instructions ran out and it was removed and deleted forever.  

It accomplished this with ease.  The results were all packaged together and shoved out a port on a network card.

From there the data was routed through several switches.  It squeezed out a creatively-constructed pinhole in Langley's most advanced firewall and flew out into the internet at large.

The data shot across the electrical miles before being deposited on another computer on the other side of the country.  There, another patiently watching process happily accepted the response, saved it to disk, and sent out a command of its own over the moast_net network.  It was mighty peculiar command indeed, and one that only a strange, hand-kludged circuit board could understand.  

The circuit caused a relay to trip and set off an archaic metal school bell on one end of the tuna plant.

The Moasts' dog Thor was presently resting by Dr Puttery's slipper-clad feet but snapped awake at the sound of the bell.

The Dr was seated in a decrepit old office chair on the highest balcony at one end of the plant.  He used the balconies as a makeshift library for the few reference books he liked to keep on hand.  (Only his favorite seven or eight thousand or so.)  The rest were stored in a sub-basement annex at the local college.
  
Today's reading material had him nearly out of his mind with annoyance.  He looked up from the book.  He turned it over so that he could see the cover and frowned at it.  20,000 Leagues Under the Sea, by Jules Verne.

"Stuff and nonsense!" he shouted at the book and threw it down.  "Any fool knows you can't run a magnetohydrodynamic propulsion drive like that!  Why... the inductors alone would take..."  He was too vexed to do the math.  "Well, certainly too much current to achieve resonance!  That's for sure!"

He felt very much like ripping the pages out of the book and throwing it straight the garbage.  That's where it belonged!  But instead, like a moth to a flame, he picked it up again.  

"Then again, there is Brandenberg's Theorem to consider.  Nobody's ever applied it to wave mechanics before, but if you substitute X for..."  He shook his head.  He couldn't seem to work out the proper integrals with all that crazy racket going on!

'Racket'?  What was that, anyway?

Ah, yes!  The bell.  His program was complete!

The computer core was on the far end of the building and there was only one way to get there quickly.

Dr Puttery tapped a few digits into the keypad hanging by his side.  All at once the bell stopped and a huge gantry crane overhead began to move.  Its modified gearing allowed it to shoot over to his location in a blur.  

Puttery pushed the chair back slightly to line up with some tape marks on the floor just as the gantry came to a stop.  Its winch disengaged and the heavy electromagnet at the end of its cable plummeted toward the earth mere inches from his head.

A relay clicked and the powerful magnet turned on.  It grabbed the chair by the iron flywheel of a 1991 Buick LeSabre which was duct-taped to its backrest for just that purpose.

The winch then reversed and lifted the chair, Puttery and all, up off the ground.  It was a little like the claw game at an arcade.

Once he was up to the proper height to clear the balcony's hand rail the gantry's motors engaged and he shot off towards the other end of the building.  The contraption moved alarmingly fast.  The weight distribution was also off-center and caused the un-seatbelted rider to hang at a sharp angle looking down at the floor zooming by 30 feet below.

Thor shot off in the opposite direction towards the stairs.  His favorite game in the whole warehouse was barking at, and racing, the crane.

It made one jarring zig-zag around a piece of old machinery that was too big to travel over the top of.

The white-knuckle ride was so iffy that even Ms McCready (who had survived helicopter crashes on no less than three different occasions) wouldn't even try it.  Only the eccentric Dr Puttery had that much faith in his cobbled-together contraptions.

When it reached its destination, the crane lurched to a stop.  The winch dropped fast, but still somehow deposited Dr Puttery to the ground, gentle as a feather, next to his mainframe computer.  The gadget released its magnetic pull, and then returned to its home position and powered itself down until the next time it was needed.

Puttery wheeled over to the main console of the 1970's era Cray supercomputer.  Though ever one for nostalgia, Puttery realized that the original number-crunching marvel of its age posessed less computing power than even a low-end cell phone nowdays.  Still, he couldn't seem to part with it and instead paired its powerful cooling systems with modern hardware.  The combination produced a Frankenstein's monster that could run circles around anything else on the market.

He inspected the data that the CIA's database had so kindly provided.  His eyebrows knotted.  He didn't immediately recognize the format of some of it.

Thor arrived a moment later, panting and out of breath.

"Ah!" said Puttery to nobody in particular.

He reached over to a round mesh platform behind him and flipped a switch.  It immediately began filling the air above it with a fine white mist.  He sent the questionable data file over to it and was immediately met with a three-dimensional representation of the same boat from Jane's photo, now hovering in midair.

He rolled his eyes.  It seemed hokey.  3D didn't provide any extra information here.  Must've been some government contract.  "Your tax dollars at work", he muttered.

Of much more interest to him were the plain old black and white text letters scrolling across his console.  They detailed the ship's every movement for the past thirty-six months.  It described who owned her, who the owner was a front for, and the crew roster at any given moment.

Enzo Fontana's name as captain stuck out loud and clear.  As did the fact that the boat just-so-happened to be docked in lower New York harbor the same night that the Ambassador was poisoned.  The clue was too circumstantial to stand up in court, but plenty of evidence to be more than mere coincidence.  This guy and his ship were now directly tied to the poison, tied back to Fong, and was also known to be responsible for the sinking of the Sea Gypsy.

There was no change of plans needed though.  Michael's gut instinct served him well and the team was already on the right track.  Now Dr Puttery could only pray that they would arrive in time to stop this dirty Enzo character before it was too late!


~46~
Tuna Plant Again

Dr Puttery settled back down from his last task, barely reclining when the phone rang.

"Can't a guy get any peace and quiet around here?" he complained.

He grabbed the ringing irritant off its hook and snapped, "What do you want?"

"Hi doc.  It's Jack."

"Jack!  How's it been, kid?"  Dr Puttery's impatience melted away in spite of himself.  He fancied himself a reclusive old ogre, but could never seem to keep up the act for long.

Jack didn't know how to answer.  He touched his forehead and looked at his fingers.  Blood was still weeping from the wound that he'd received in the rollover during his narrow escape from the rebel compound.  He decided to ignore the question and move on to the purpose of his call.

"I've got some documents I'm uploading for you.  First, there are the files from Rurick but I'm sad to say they don't have much in them.  Besides that, I also snuck into the compound and found some surveillance logs they'd been keeping."

"I just received the first file.  You're right.  There really isn't much there.

"Once the second gets here I'll convert it to text and run a pattern match to see if we can take a guess at what their game plan is... or at least where they're planning to strike first."

"Doc, I've also got one more thing.  The commander seemed like he was in the middle of writing a love note."

"You're a sentimental fool, Jack."

"Yeah, maybe.  But I think it's actually secret message of some kind."

Dr Puttery smiled and licked his lips in anticipation.  His fellow man was a set of smelly, illogical, emotional brutes.  But they did manage to provide him with interesting puzzles from time to time.  For that, he maintained at least a cursory relationship with his own species.

When the letter finished downloading he scanned it briefly.  "An excellent find indeed, Jack."

"Really?  What makes you say that?"

"The letter is addressed to an 'Anastasia'.  If you remember the file from the police commandant, the leader of this mob calls himself, a 'Czar', right?"

"Yeah?"

"Well, Anastasia (Grand Duchess Anastasia Nikolaevna, to be specific) was the mysterious lost heir of Nicholas the second, last Czar of Russia.  Ingrid Bergman played her once.  Great film."

Jack didn't let the Dr's attention wander too far.  "So you think this new guy is claiming to be a descendant of her then?"

"Possibly.  More likely though, it's a part of the code.  The name is derived from the Greek Anstasius meaning 'resurrection', or, 'she will rise again'.  No doubt that would be alluding to the end goal of their plans of seeing the old empire reborn."

Dr Puttery decided in favor a second opinion.  "Has Kurt read it?  What does he think?"

Now it came out.  Jack hummed and hawed defensively.

Dr Puttery sounded worried.  "Where's Kurt?  Is he injured?"

"Oh!  No.

"Well, I mean, yes.

"But he's not here."

"What are you saying, man!??"

Jack took a breath to try and keep from babbling.  "Kurt hurt himself back at the airport.  Busted an ankle.  The hospital says he'll be fine though."  Jack hoped that the keen Dr Puttery wouldn't realize something important about that.  

Yeah, fat chance!

"You mean you've been doing all this alone?"

Silence.

"Melinda Moast is going to have a fit, you know?"

"I know, I know.  I'll take it easy.  I promise."  Jack reflected back on the events of the day, and added, "From here on out, anyway."

Dr Puttery grunted at the flimsy promise.  He'd known Jack far too long to put much stock in it.  "All right.  I'll take a look at the riddle while the computer does its thing on the surveillance logs.  But you be careful out there."

"I will.  Have fun Doc."

Jack hung up the phone.  His stomach growled angrily.

He searched through the vehicle for something to eat but it was a fruitless effort.  Its survival gear was woefully lacking.  He did find a blanket and a plastic bag to tape over the missing rear window, but spending the night hungry was certainly not going to be fun.

In his attempt to escape detection, he had traveled farther and farther away from civilization.  All broadcast radio had long ago dwindled out of range.  Jack decided to turn on the rebels' 2-way radio and give it a listen.  There was a slim chance that he could glean further clues to aid Dr Puttery's research.  But the radio served a far more important role though.  A psychological one.  It was going to be a long, cold, hungry night and it'd help keep his mind off things.

A lone wolf howled off in the distance.

He looked up and noticed a reindeer grazing in a moonlit clearing.  He smiled.

Well, at least he knew what he was going to have for dinner.  He thought only one word as he clambered up to the gigantic .50 caliber M2 machine gun turret:   Hamburger.


~47~
Out of the Frying Pan

Jane had seen the sub split around her like an egg shell and remembered the suffocating feeling of oxygen running out -- her lungs... burning!  Then black.

Then, light, as she felt her hands and knees slam hard against rough metal grating.  They began to bleed.  She hacked and vomited seawater until rough hands grabbed her hair and yanked her head back.

She was on a metal catwalk.  She could see through the open metal rectangles to the staging floor far below.  There were a series of pools across the floor, situated to allow Sea Dragon minisubs quickly come and go from beneath without the need for an airlock.  

The room swam in her oxygen-starved mind but she noticed that she was level with a pair of black combat boots.  Her wavering gaze lead up to equally black pants.  Finally, she made it all the way up the black uniform of the mercenary and saw the steel eyes of a ruthless killer looking back at her.

"Get her up." he barked.

Whoever held her hair decided to pull her up by that, and she wound up with one arm wrenched firmly behind her for good measure.

The man in black grabbed her chin roughly and brought it up so that he could see her face more clearly.  "Interesting." he said.  "I blew you up a few days ago."

He took a step to where other guards firmly held Misty and Wendell from behind.

"You, I don't know."

Misty began to say something, but he cut her off.  "Nor do I want to."

If that wasn't enough to shut her up, the guard yanking her arms painfully tighter behind her certainly did the trick.

"But you, Mister St Frank...  Tell me how come we're meeting like this again?"

Wendell smiled as if meeting an old school friend on the street.  "Hello, Enzo.  How's the nose?"

Jane noticed the brute's misshapen nose must indeed have been broken sometime in the past.  He rubbed it as if remembering something.

"Yes." he hissed.  "You kicked that cannon into my face instead of letting me shoot you.  That wasn't very smart.  By the time I'm done with you, you're going to wish you'd gone for that quick and painless death."

Having relieved his wounded pride over the embarrassing memory, Enzo returned to the skillfully avoided question.

"But first, I asked, how did you get here?"

Wendell fumbled for a believable lie.

Enzo knew what he was doing.

"Oh, that's okay.  Take your time." said the thug.  "I'll just amuse myself with your friend here while I'm waiting."

He walked briskly back toward Jane, drawing a shiny knife from his belt as he went.  Very shiny.  Very long.  Very jagged.  He wasn't kidding in the least!

Her eyes were wide and she tried to scream, but a thick and calloused hand clamped itself over her mouth from behind.

"Okay!" shouted Wendell.

He was ignored.

Enzo brought the knife slowly toward her head.  He had what he wanted, but he wasn't stopping!  He wasn't stopping because he didn't... feel... like it!

The room swirled around Jane, her senses in hyperdrive now.

No, God!  Oh my Lord, God, please!  No!  Her head reeled she prayed.

The knife came at her in slow motion.  She could see it in all detail down to the micro-fine polishing scratches.  In its mirrored surface she saw Misty duck.

He heart thumped loudly.

Wendell shouted something.

"Combat multiplier," said Misty.

Or was it Misty from the past?

She was smiling.  Probably the past, then.

The thug behind her still held her hair tight with one hand.  And... her mouth with the other.  So what held her body then?  Jane wondered.  Nothing!

She doubled her waist in half, wrenching her head violently forward and to the right.  Pain surged through her scalp as hair was uprooted.  A small lock of it was sliced free by the blade whooshing past her ear.  The unseen thug was yanked forward, face-first into the oncoming knife of Enzo.

The pain of her hair stopped and she fell free to the floor as the man's hands flew to his eye socket and the knife now sticking out of it.

Enzo was actually stunned for a moment - which was not an easy thing to do!  By the time he was ready to fight, he took one step but was knocked back off the platform by the body of Misty's guard, whom she had used the diversion to send airborne with a Judo throw.

Misty turned to see if Wendell needed help.  He sat on the chest of his guard, waving an outstretched index finger.

"... and that's why terrorism is NAUGHTY!" Weird Wendell lectured the unconscious man.

She looked back at Jane who was smiling, and thankfully, not seriously hurt.

She looked back at Wendell again.

"You coming?" she asked.

Wendell snatched up the man's gun and followed.

Their preferred mode of escape would have been down the catwalk and into one of the subs, but they knew that the mercenary leader lay down there somewhere, and, if conscious, he'd be real mad too!

Their only other option was an oblong steel hatch that entered into the rest of the compound.

Wendell spun open the door's locking wheel and stepped inside.  Misty followed.  Jane brought up the rear, trying not to think of the stabbed man sitting in the corner, oblivious to reality as he still clutched his face in shock.  It was barbaric.  Even for somebody like that.

But what alternative was there?  What was done, was done.  And even if she had it to do all over again, she knew there'd be no choice but to do the same.

"Who was your friend back there?" Misty asked Wendell.

Jane listened attentively, not because she was particularly interested, but rather that she was grateful for any conversation to take her mind off the wretched creature they'd left behind.

"His name's Enzo.  Kurt and I met him back when we recovered the Golden Hind.

He nodded at Misty, "You remember.  It was back when the rest of you were playing Safari Jim down in the Congo."

"Sir Francis Drake's ship?  Isn't that in the Newington district of London?" asked Jane.

"Good one Jane!  I didn't know that factoid until Dr Puttery mentioned it afterwards." Wendell was genuinely impressed.  "It turns out that through some twist of fate, the revered landmark is actually the Hind's sister ship, 'The Wishing Star'.  The real boat was actually still afloat in the Philippines... and still being used for piracy to boot!"

The story was interrupted as they came to a blind corner.  Wendell held his hand up for silence.  He peeked cautiously around it, the barrel of the gun leading the way.  It was empty.  They continued walking and talking.

"Pirates, sure, but none of that Hollywood, 'arr, matey', business.  Mostly just a bunch of teenage thugs with rusty AK-47's, playing havoc with the local Indonesian trade routes.

"Our pal Enzo back there had bigger plans in mind for 'em though.  He worked for a major international drug lord named Fong and took over the pirates, bringing them over to the more lucrative business of drug smuggling.

"Long story, short:  Kurt and I put an end to that game and watched old Enzo sink to the bottom of the South China Sea, buried alive under a flaming avalanche of his own merchandise.  Easy peasy!"

He was just being modest.  The actual events were about as far from 'easy peasy' as you could get.  Wendell could still see it.  Even still smell it.  The horrible stench of burning diesel fuel and drugs mingled with whatever else aboard the flames could claim.  In his mind's eye, he saw himself cornered between the deck gun and the wall.  Trapped like some sort of animal.  He saw the wicked grin spread across Enzo's face as he raised his pistol for the kill.  Flames leaping in the background mingled with his cruel features to paint a perfect image of a daemon straight from Hell.  

Wendell went for a longshot, last-ditch stunt.  He felt himself fall to the floor hard on his back and kick both feet out with all his might.  The ship's steel gun swung around hard on its post, catching Enzo square in the face and knocking him into a fifteen foot stack of wooden crates.  He watched them toppled down on the feind, but didn't dare stick around to see more.  Wendell flung himself overboard and swam like a madman.  He barely made it out of range when the little surprise he'd left in the hold reached the end of its fuse and a fireball engulfed the ship.  The end of Enzo and his ugly operation!  (Or so he'd thought.)

"Apparently, the Captain didn't go down with the ship after all." observed Misty.

Wendell shrugged.  There was nothing to argue.  He'd seen it happen first hand and had no idea how it was possible to escape that.  But minutes ago the same murderous psyco had nearly carved up his new friend like a sliced ham, so somehow, beyond all reason, Misty was right.

His thoughts about Jane's recent escape sparked a new concern for her condition.

He turned to her.  "Are you sure you're okay?" 

She nodded lightly and tried to divert the conversation back to the previous, more comfortable one.

"Seems a shame to lose such an important historical treasure." she said.

"What?"  Wendell was confused until he realized that she was still referring to the story.  "Oh, no.  That wasn't the Golden Hind that sank.  It was some random piece of junk, junk.  The Hind is now safely stored away in one of Michel's drydocks 'till he can get her cleaned up and figure out how to break the news to the British."

Somehow, hearing him tell the strange and uncanny adventures of the Bible Study's past exploits calmed her nerves more than anything.  Perhaps it was something like how a good campfire story can make one forget the pitch darkness of the nighttime woods.

They walked down the hallway, hyper-observant to every nook and cranny in front of them.  They were so intent on what lay ahead they forgot to "check their six", as Ms McCready would say.  In other words, they forgot to watch for anybody sneaking up from behind.

So swift was the attack that not even Misty could fight back before her arms were helplessly pinned.  All three were dragged into a side compartment, the door locked, and the light turned on.

They were released once inside.  The one and only door was impossible to reach and the half dozen men with rifles meant that not even Misty's famous combat multipliers could get them out of here alive.  

The men had something else strange about them as well.  Although they all wore the black combat uniforms of the mercenaries, to Jane their skin seemed a little off.  Reminds me of a fake tan from a bottle, she decided.

"All right, Landies," said the leader, "explain your presence here and make it quick!"

Jane was far more relieved than the other two as the pieces of the puzzle fell into place.  It was the the strange terminology and accent that provided the clues.  It tallied with that of Captain Nemo, her son, and her late cousin.

"It's okay!" she reassured her friends. "They're Atlanticans!"

The man was evidently surprised that she'd seen through his disguise.  

Regardless, he saw no need to keep up the charade but continued with his interrogation.  "Yes we are.  Now let's discuss who you are."

Misty made the introductions, "This is Jane Jones, Wendell St Frank, and I'm Misty Carlisle.  We're from the land.  From California, if you know where that is."

The man only scowled.  He didn't care where that was.

"We're friends of your Queen." she added, still hoping to soften him up.

A hubbub of whispers passed between the group members.

"You've seen the Queen?  Where is she?  We've come to rescue her so she can lead our army against the Sea Dragons!"

The three friends were silent.  Perhaps, silent a moment too long.

"Where is she?" he asked again, his face pulling itself into a concerned frown.

Nobody knew how to answer, but Jane decided that the bad news was her responsibility.  She reached into her pocket and came out with a clenched fist.

"She's dead." she said simply.  She opened her hand to reveal the ring that the Queen of Atlantis had given her.

The Atlantican rescue party took a one-two punch of emotions, straight to the gut.  They argued and fought and cursed and wept among themselves.

As the tumult proceeded, one aged man stepped forward to inspect the ring.  It was clear that his eyesight wasn't the greatest.  He still carried a stolen Landie rifle like the rest though.  Jane desperately hoped he'd never try to fire it.  Who knows what he'd accidentally hit!

His ancient fingers shook with age - or perhaps something else - as he took the ring from her hand and inspected it over and over.

After a moment he looked up and spoke in a simple, low voice that somehow silenced the whole room.

"S-She gave this to you?" he asked.

"Yeah." Jane shrugged.

Every Atlantican eye in the room got big.  It was a significant thing for some reason.  Jane was generally good at putting puzzles together, but this one had her stumped.

"You heard her.  The Queen is dead." announced the leader.  "Grab your weapons, grab our new friends, and let's get out of this ill-conceived Landie hovel."

Though referred to by the term, the Atlanticans' three "friends" were marched toward the exit under armed escort.


~48~
Reporting In

Somewhere in the middle of the Atlantic, miles from anything but rolling, splashing sea, a small white orb suddenly bobbed to the surface.  It could've been any number of things, but due to a metal cylinder which slowly sprouted from its center it looked the most like some kind of giant robotic plant seed.  The 'stem' grew taller until it was nearly four feet high and then the tip of it began to open up.  It blossomed into a dish which then oriented itself, not towards the sun, but towards an unseen satellite soaring somewhere in orbit.

"Fong, this is Enzo," said the familiar villain.

"How's the harvesting operation coming along?"

"We've only just started.  But we have a problem.  There were some people who were about to stumble upon our undersea test location.  Americans.  Independent agents, to top it all off!"

"Well, go kill 'em then." said Fong.  Did he have to tell Enzo everything now?

"We did.  We blew their boat up.

"But it looks like they survived, and worse still, they somehow found Atlantis!  We captured 'em poking around the facility."

"Impossible!  The only ones who know about that place are us, the fish, and a city full of dead people!"

"Not so dead..." reminded Enzo.

"You mean, you still haven't put down that little rebellion!??" exclaimed the other.

"No.  They grow 'em slippery down here.  Now that they swiped those three kids I'm worried..."

"Wait.  You mean you captured them, and then went ahead lost 'em again?  And 'kids', you say?  Come on Enzo!  Get it together, man!  Draco handed us a tight schedule with no room for mistakes.  If this thing goes south, you know what's going to happen to both of us, right?"

He did know.  He knew all too well, in fact.

"I know how important this is." he protested.  "That's why I risked this broadcast.

"And you don't need to tell me things have gone too far!  That's why I'm asking you to send me China's submarine fleet."

"No, Enzo.  I'm not giving you my subs.  I need 'em for my part of the plan."

"Come on!  You've got plenty of surface ships.  That's all you really need.  Fork over the subs!"  Then Enzo played his trump card. "Either that... or explain to him why you didn't!"

The line went dead for a moment.  The threat hit home.  Fong was cornered and could give only one answer.

"Fine.  I'll send you the subs.  They should arrive there by tomorrow sometime.  But when they do, you make sure that you wipe out everybody.  All of them.  Capiche?"

Just what kind of "Fong" would use words like "capiche" was something none of the Sea Dragon henchmen in the room could understand.  Nor were any of them suicidal enough to ask.

"Capiche." agreed Enzo. "By Wednesday, there'll be nobody alive outside this dome.  

"I guarantee it."


~49~
General Janet Jones

Jane, Misty, and Wendell were herded on stage.  Actually, as far as a 'stage' went, it was more like a group of crates pushed together than anything Rodgers and Hammerstein would recognize.  Still, it served its purpose.  

The room itself was a dank old warehouse in a suburban area outside the city proper.  Water could be heard steadily drip, drip, dripping from innumerable pinholes.  From the smell of mold in the air, it had evidently been doing so for quite some time.

The room was fairly large but also packed wall to wall with the Atlantican refugees who managed to escape both the poisoning and the constant raids of the Sea Dragons.  Slowly, block by block, furlong by furlong, the invaders drove the rightful inhabitants back.  The invaders suffered relatively small loss of life in comparison.  Atlantis fought a losing war and the somber knowledge of it weighed heavy on every face.

Today, however, the refugees gathered to see the strange new Landies.  There was no need of formal invitation.  The news ran through their ranks like a lightning bolt and they came to see first-hand the source of their newfound hope.

The old man from among their captors back at the Sea Dragon stronghold was first to address the multitude.  At first it seemed that his aged voice had no chance of being heard over the excited chattering of the crowd.  But it turned out he didn't need to be.  All he did was hold up one gnarled hand and the room immediately dropped to abject silence as if under a magic spell.  

The only noise still to be heard was the never-ending dripping of the leaky room.  In the new stillness each drop rang out like the blow of a blacksmith's anvil.

"My friends, we all know why we have come.  We all know who these people are.  Three Landies into whose hands our beloved Queen - may her memory live forever - has entrusted us.

"We all know how much this means to our cause.  Not since our nation was founded in the days of twin sons of Nemo has it come under such peril!  My friends, you are all descended from that great crew.  And you have fought valiantly in defense of our homeland.  But we have been confounded at every turn!

"The picket lines near the Wentworth homestead have fallen.  The bold (if rash) troupe of Salazar's guerrillas is no more.  And yes, even our beloved Queen has been tortured and killed by the Sea Dragons."

At this, a murmur washed over the crowd once more.  And the man held up his hand once more.

"These things are all true." he reassured all the non-existent skeptics.  "But even now, in the bleakest days of our city, The Lord has raised up a new deliverer!  A beacon of hope and of victory!"

At this, Jane stopped following the speech as her mind began to wander.  She was so excited with the news that there may actually a way out of this predicament, that she failed to notice her own name being called.

She sure didn't miss it when the rest of the room began chanting it though.  "Jane-Jones, Jane-Jones, Jane-Jones..." went up the cry.

She turned to look at Wendell and Misty for some explanation.  Their gawking faces made asking them a moot point.

All eyes were on her.  Even the old man stood with a contented grin on his face, his arm still raised in her direction from the introduction.

It was like they all expected her to be some sort of Moses heading to Pharaoh's court, or General Patton leading the troops into battle.

This wasn't good.

The room was beginning to quiet down rapidly again.  The people were done telling one another everything that they wanted to say, and now wanted to hear what she had to say.

Great.  A speech.

The other two must have thought the same thing.

"Stall." whispered Wendell, giving her a slight shove in the direction of the old man.

"What!??" she exclaimed.

"Yeah, yeah!" agreed Misty.  "Go say something and we'll figure out how to get word to Michael for some help."

"I can't do this!"

"You don't have to 'do' it." said Wendell.  "Michael will.  All you have to do is make a speech."

"That's what I meant!"  She said it so loud that the others both clapped a hand over her mouth.

The room was now silent as a tomb now.  All eyes were on her.  Waiting.

Something occurred to Jane at that moment that caused her feet to move.  It was the memory of the Queen that drove her.  In her last moment, she must have seen something in Jane to offer her people hope.  And that, to Jane's astonishment, made this stranger the first person who had ever believed in her.  

Maybe she was right.  Maybe that encouragement was all that Jane needed, for in that moment she no longer felt like a helpless runaway, fallen into circumstances over her head, but now she really was the General they all expected her to be!

As she lifted her chin high, the room cheered!  Their deliverer had indeed come!

(Maybe?)

"People of Atlantis..." the words seemed to form themselves of their own accord, "My friends beneath the waves... you already know the evil that fallen upon you in these dark days."

"Look at that." whispered Wendell.

Misty smiled and nodded with all new confidence, "She can do this."

"Well, though I'm a stranger here, and I don't have a lot of leadership experience... and our enemies have us outnumbered and outgunned..."  All at once Jane let her own words sweep over her.  She hesitated.  

"Um...  Um...  Um...  Um..."

"Uh oh." said Wendell, in the understatement of the decade.

"She can't do this."

The two rushed forward and caught her, one on each arm.  It wasn't clear whether Jane was about to feint or run away, but she needed catching nonetheless.

"But none of that matters!" Misty completed her friend's last thought.

"These Sea Dragon punks won't know what hit 'em!" added Wendell.

Though they didn't understand some of Wendell's slang, the people heard his tone and cheered because of it.

The old man approached, raised Jane's limp arm high like a boxing referee, and shouted, "Our champion!"

The light glistened off the ring of the late queen.

The room went wild.

"We are so dead." the champion muttered.


~50~
War of Atlantis

The Atlantican army kicked towards the massive undersea dome in a strange formation that would have passed as rank and file on land.  Had they made it to the structure, it wasn't clear what kind of damage they could even inflict on it.  They never did, however.

A contingent of the Sea Dragons' minisub fleet was dispatched to meet the upstarts as well as their own full compliment of SCUBA soldiers.

As far as the divers went, the Atlanticans and Sea Dragons were a fair match.  The SCUBA divers were more nimble without the large metal helmets that the Atlanticans wore.  But these also gave the latter a defensive advantage in combat.

The category where the Atlanticans fell at a dangerous disadvantage was in the area of vehicles.  The Sea Dragon minisubs weren't exactly armored, but they were made of strong metal and, just as the divers from the Starfish found out, they could easily deflect hand-held weapons.

Because of it, the fight was a losing one from the start.  It was about the equivalent of foot soldiers going up against tanks on land.  The Atlanticans got in a few lucky hits and disabled several subs, but for the most part the dark red clouds of blood spreading all around were their own.

It was a sad thought indeed, but the three Bible Study members realized that the battle must be lost insultingly soon after it began.  At least, that is, without outside help - which was what they intended to bring to the table.

Yet at that moment, the fortunes of war weren't exactly being kind to them either.

"Do you think we can make it?" asked Wendell, eyeballing The Peanut from across the Atlantis alley where it remained parked from the day before.

"No." said Misty and Jane in unison.

"I'm gonna try it!"

They both pulled him back down as a harpoon tore overhead.

"Well, we can't just sit here!" he called irritably.

"Oh yes we can." chided Misty.

"Oh no we can't!" exclaimed Jane, tapping her on the shoulder and pointing.

There, looming behind them, was the ominous form of a Sea Dragon minisub.  Even as it dawned on them what was happening, the sub let loose a mini torpedo.

The three scattered.

The torpedo streaked past their now vacant foxhole and toward The Peanut.

Though it didn't strike the sub, it might as well have.  Instead, the bomb collided with the building where they had found the Queen.  Then it detonated, sending hundreds of pounds of rubble down over their submarine.

"We're so dead." said Jane for the hundredth time since joining the Atlantis insurgents.

"Without Michael and the others..." began Misty.

"They'll be here." snapped Wendell.

"How could they possibly..." Jane began, but was cut off by an enormous shadow passing overhead.

They all looked up to see where it was coming from.  What their eyes beheld was met with equal parts joy and fear.  There was no mistaking that sub.

With the Starfish now in the battle, maybe, just maybe they could give a reasonable account of themselves though.

The radio chatter over the Atlantican band was less optimistic.  None of the three could fathom why, but it was clear from their comments that even in such dire straits, help from this quarter was not appreciated.

The trio of Landies had no time to digest the meaning of it.  They merely chalked it up to local politics and went on fighting.


As the horribly one-sided battle raged on without, Enzo stood sipping his usual oolong tea.  Fong had gotten him hooked on the stuff.  The drink was an acquired taste, really.  (Once the drink acquired enough booze, Enzo thought it didn't taste half bad.)

He stood looking down on various technicians hunched over their respective controls.  The dome of the Atlantis control center soared above his head, giving full view of the sparkling green waters and the fish that darted about.  

Its breathtaking beauty couldn't have found a less grateful person to reveal itself to.  Enzo's pleasures came more along the lines of poison and other forms of wanton killing.  Looking out at the stunning underwater world didn't interest him in the slightest.

Perhaps it should have.

A huge smack with the resounding finish of a Chinese gong shuddered through the dome.

Enzo looked up to see the giant steel arms of the submarine Starfish clutching the massive transparent structure.

Without hesitation he reached forward and hit the "general alarm" button right under the nose of a paralyzed security chief.

All at once the sirens began to howl throughout the complex like a cat with a stepped-on tail.  Soldiers dropped whatever they were doing and scrambled to evacuate.

"Raise Fong's fleet." ordered Enzo with stark calmness, "Tell 'em we're under attack.  Tell 'em to get here, fast!"

They heard a pop noise as the Starfish's auger started in on the glass, sending the first crack shooting through the polycarbonate dome like a forked lightning bolt.  The material was amazingly strong as far as plastics went - literally 'bulletproof', in fact.  But it still wouldn't hold out long against a monstrous, case-hardened drill bit the size of a delivery van.

"You.  Keep trying." he barked at the radio operator.  It was a death sentence.  But the man knew disobedience would be a worse one.

"The rest of you, to the subs before that thing gets through."

He chucked his tea cup down hard, sending shattered porcelin flying.  Then he followed the staff to the sub pools, leaving the last man to his fate.

Remorse never occourred to him.


The Atlantican soldiers eagerly watched cracks spreading across the surface of the hated dome.  Without warning, the fractures suddenly popped it completely.

A gigantic bubble comprised of all the former command center's air billowed its way towards the surface.  What it looked like when it finally arrived and broke free to the atmosphere, nobody knew, but it must've been impressive.

Each of them let out a cheer over their Atlantican dive radios.

It proved to be a mixed blessing though.  The command post was now out of commission so there was less coordination of tactics on the part of there enemy.  For the moment it was nothing but every man for himself.  Unfortunately, it also meant that every minisub that the Sea Dragons owned was now afloat -- afloat and mad!

A swarm of them swept down upon the soldiers of Atlantis like a steel tidalwave.  Just as it was about to break over them, the Starfish evened the odds by cruising down the length of the enemy line, pushing, crushing, and generally mowing down everything dumb enough not to get out of the way.

Maybe with the Starfish on our side... thought Jane.

But it was a hope, short-lived.  The Sea Dragons' mayday had been heard.  No less than a dozen full sized nuclear subs began to gather ominously on the horizon like approaching sharks drawn in by the scent of blood.

With the help of the Starfish, the Atlanticans began to secure an upper hand of sorts by the time the metallic leviathans arrived.  The advantage was, of course, lost with the these new reinforcements, but the tipping point was not so critical as one might suppose.  The huge subs couldn't do much inside the battle itself without risk of clobbering more of their own than the enemy.  Likewise, the friendly fire of their torpedoes would have been counterproductive.  All they were really able to contribute to the battle was more SCUBA divers.

But then, they also served one other important purpose for the adversary.  They distracted the Starfish.  Certainly, Nemo's awesome sub could take out the Landie subs (as she had already demonstrated), but cutting through their tough double hulls took time.  And then there was no telling how many torpedo hits the Starfish, herself could take...

All that being said, what began as a hopeless effort, doomed to be over before lunch time, now stretched through the rest of the day.  Sometimes the battle trended one way, sometimes the other.  To the Bible Study trio, now in the thick of the fighting, it still seemed that they'd spent far more than their fair share of the day on the receiving side of the argument.  There still didn't seem to be much hope of achieving victory in the end.  But still, the battle raged on through the afternoon and into the early evening.

The fight slowly faded with the light as the twilight of onrushing night made combat nearly impossible.  They were far enough below the waves that the sunlight was dim even during the day, but at night the blackness would be complete.  Certainly the Sea Dragon subs, and even the troops could fight on with artificial lights, but not without making themselves an easy target.  

No.  Both sides knew that it was time to put aside their vicious game for the night.  


~51~
Bivouac

As from times long-predating even the original Atlantis, the troops all put aside their fighting for the night and hunkered down in whatever corner they could find for themselves.  Each, like the hundreds of generations before them, tried to close their eyes and snatch a few moments of sleep from the terrifyingly still night.  Like the soldiers of old they knew they must put aside the horrors of war somehow and rest while they could.  Yet at the same time adrenaline, shock, and the fear that this might be their last night on Earth fought back against their weariness.

Each thought about the very real possibility of himself entering into eternity tomorrow.  To some, it provided comfort.  To others, terror.  But all thought about it.

Like the ancients too, each side plotted surprise attacks during the night, and each side posted sentries to prevent them.  Whether their opponent's ambition would dare trump good judgment was a secret that the night's deathly silence refused reveal to either side.

The Altlanticans bivouacked together in one quarter of the city, while the Sea Dragons returned to their mother subs in the vicinity of their wrecked dome near the central palace.

Meanwhile, the leaders met and planned under low lamplight.  Jane, as their champion, was invited as a figurehead if nothing else.  After making her appearance, she excused herself at the first reasonable opportunity.

"Yo, Gunga Din, what's the word?" called the voice of Wendell.

Startled, Jane spun around to see him and Misty approaching from behind her.  Wendell's dive gear was now upgraded to Atlantican version, but other than that the two were unchanged since Jane had left them.

Jane waited for them to come up close before answering Wendell's question.  She didn't know how far the sound might travel in the stillness and didn't want to accidentally demoralize the troops.

"It's not good." she whispered.  "The Generals are still in the other room arguing.  They've got so many plans, I'm pretty sure it means they have no plan at all.  

"There's just no scenario where guys with spears can take out a fleet of modern nuclear subs and support craft."  She was clearly quite shaken.

"We've got the Starfish." offered Misty, trying to calm her down.  It didn't work.

"One sub against ten doesn't improve our odds a lot.  Especially when about half the Atlanticans would rather fight Nemo than the bad guys!"

"We could hide in the city.  Fight a running battle.  Shoot and take cover, and all that." suggested Wendell.

"And what?  Hide behind a two thousand year old building while the Dragons shoot torpedoes us!  We tried that, remember?  It cost us The Peanut and any chance of rescue."

Wendell went to speak, but she cut him off.  "And don't you dare say 'they'll be here'!  We're going to die tomorrow, and we're going to do it all alone, y'hear!??"

She noticed the hurt expression cross his face.

"Sorry I snapped." she said more softly.  "Listen, I'll tell the Generals about your strategy, ok?  We'll figure out some way to get through this.  It's just..."  She rubbed her face with a low groan.  "Can somebody remind me how I got roped into all this?"

Both of her friends shrugged.

"Come get some sleep." offered Misty, tugging her arm.  "We've fixed us all a nice spot right over by the door."

They then joined the rest of the soldiers in pretending to sleep.


~52~
Igor Rurick

A hundred desperate-looking pirates with snarling teeth surrounded them.  The rusty sepia of a south-seas sunset cast its eternal tint on the whole scene.  

A pontoon sea plane drifted behind him shot full of holes and unable to fly.  Somehow, he knew that it was his mother who'd shot it.  

Jack looked up at the man standing next to him in a classic fur-trimmed flight jacket.  His bold chin was defiant at the approaching horde.  The wind tossed his sandy hair.  He was invincible.

"Take heart Jackie." he said, and tussled young Jack's hair.

Just as the lead pirate sprang, the radio in the sea plane gave a jolting burst of static.  And yet, it wasn't the sea plane at all.

Fully-grown Jack awoke to the squelched grumble of the Humvee's radio.

At first he had trouble shifting gears from dreamland to reality.

He tried to open the door.  It didn't budge.

Oh yeah.  Smashed in the rollover.  He finagled himself out the passenger side.

He tried to step to the ground but his legs crumpled beneath him.  They were bruised from the wild ride and stiff from the awkward night's sleep.

Jack decided that if God wanted him on his knees that bad, then he might as well take the hint and pray.  

Any other man in his situation would have prayed that he could avoid the enemy army searching for him.  Or that he could unlock their secrets and spare millions of people the horrors of war.  Or even that he would survive the rugged wilds of the vast Siberian waste and live to tell the tale.

Jack prayed for none of those things.  It was the dream that really had him shaken up.  Jack had seen his father - his real father - only on occasion growing up.  His stepfather was nice enough and he'd come to respect him, but he had always felt a certain special affinity toward the reckless adventurer too.  It was hard to sort out.  In some ways he admired him like no other person on earth.  In other ways he hated him like no other.

He looked around at the rugged and exotic Siberian hillside with his bullet-riddled and stolen Humvee parked in the middle of it.  The adventurer motif was hard to deny.  Even his old leather flight jacket told the same tale.  Jack was a person who wore his pain close to him.

The radio crackled again.  He mentally kicked himself.  It was foolish to have kept the thing on all night.  All he needed was a dead battery way out here.

He only caught the tail end of what the voice was saying as he opened the door.

"... Igor Rurick:  ETA, 1100 hours."

Well, that sure doesn't sound good.  Jack took a wild gambit and grabbed the mic.  "Transmission lost.  Please repeat."

Would they really believe he was one of them?

They did!  "Convoy with arms and ammunition from Leningrad nearing base.  Also have police commandant Igor Rurick in custody.  ETA is 1100 hours."

Jack pulled out a map from under the seat and examined the roads coming and going from the area.  He checked his watch.  It would be tight if he wanted to intercept them.  If not, it would mean breaking in and out of the encampment again.  Not an experience he wanted to relive.

Of course, taking on an entire armed convoy wouldn't exactly be child's play either!

He clambered into the Humvee and back into the driver's seat.  It spat gravel as he shot off in the direction of the road.  

He drove the treacherous mountain roads only slightly faster than an escaped lunatic would have.

Jack couldn't help but think he was a little bit crazy.  It wasn't like they held his best friend captive.  He had only met Rurick that one time, and he was patronized and nearly shot in that encounter.  Somehow though, Jack had respect for the man and couldn't stand idly by and let the enemy have him.

As Jack thought about it more, something seemed dirty about the situation... that Rurick would suddenly be in their hands.

A half an hour later he pulled the Humvee onto the main road to the compound.  There was a huge cloud of dust rising up to his right.  He smacked the steering wheel in frustration.  It was too late.  He'd just missed them by ten, maybe fifteen, minutes.  They'd be rolling into the gate by the time he caught up.


That night Igor Rurick sat in one corner of the cell gently cradling his broken ribs.  He glared up at the guard pacing back and forth in front of the cell block.  

Rurick's eyes didn't hold the look of a man who had just been beat up, but instead were those of a tiger, daring his captors to open the cage and face the consequences.  They bore a certain fiery rage, fueled by the bitterness of betrayal.

The guard completed his circuit, spun on his heels, and marched the other direction.  Rurick was surprised to see a second guard suddenly behind him, marching in lock-step.

The first guard never saw the second bring his club down.

The new arrival searched the man and found his keys.

"Commandant Rurick, I'm here to get you out."  The man proved out the uncanny words by unlocking the door.  "Here.  Help me get the guard inside and you can swap clothes with him."

As they did so, Rurick finally identified his rescuer.  "Well, well.  If it isn't my cleaning friend from back at the office."

"Jack." said Jack.

Jack stripped the guard's clothes while Igor stripped his own.  Then each swapped clothes with the other.  The guard was laid in the corner, face to the wall, with something carefully placed under his head to make it look like 'Rurick' was asleep there.

Their ruse completed, the two made their way along the shadows of the buildings until they came to a metal storm drain.

"This leads to the sewer." said Jack.

"There are no better ways out?" asked Rurick.

"No, but believe me, there are a whole lot worse ones!"

The story behind that comment would have to wait.  Jack began lifting the grate but it was yanked from his finger tips when Rurick stepped on it.

"Not yet." he said.  "I need my files."

"I have your files, remember?  There's not much there but I'll gladly send you a copy.  Now come on, let's get out of here."

Rurick let out a long sigh.  "You still have a lot to learn about this game."

"You didn't give me all the files?" it dawned on him.

Rurick smiled.  "Of course not.  Just as I know the KGB didn't give me all the files.

"But all, and I mean ALL, of the files are here now.  A certain double-crossing pig in the Kremlin decided that we were getting too close to the truth and handed everything - including me - over to the rebels for disposal."

"So where do we look?"

"The papers were taken by a large beefy thug who smelt strongly of vodka.  It's just after 9 pm now.  So you tell me, mister secret agent, where would we find him?"

"At the roughest bar in town?" offered Jack.

"Precisely."


The man grunted for no reason.  He was every bit as large and smelly as Rurick had described.  He was missing several teeth and the ones that were there shouldn't have been.  His bloodshot eyes added to the countenance and betrayed a man who was far more interested in booze than sleep.

He stood watching two men arm wrestling.  They were seated at a barrel with a wood plank across the top.  On both sides, where each man's hand might normally contact the table upon losing, was placed an empty beer glass with a live scorpion inside.  The loser of the contest would suffer penalties far worse than his pride.

The two wrestlers had been locked in combat for quite some time.  Neither was willing to lose given the circumstances.  And so the beefy arms stayed frozen together, mighty and unmoving, like two girders of the same bridge.

The brute wasn't watching to see who was stronger.  He couldn't care less about the other mens' ego.  He was there to see somebody get hurt.  This stalemate was annoying him.

He slugged back the last of his vodka and shoved the glass in another man's arms.  He yanked one of the wrestlers loose from the combat and forced his hand into the glass.  The man shrieked in agony and ran away.  The cruel man laughed like it was the funniest joke ever told.

The other men in the room laughed because they didn't dare not to.

"Coward." said Jack.

The room fell instantly silent like somebody tripped over a speaker plug.

"What?  What did you say?" roared the kodiak bear.

"I said you're a coward.  You're a coward and you smell like a week old pile of pig manure."

Everybody took a step back.  Even a few people who came to the bar to see the fights walked out with weak stomachs.

"You're an American." he spat.  It was an insult.

"Any last words, American?"  He was walking towards Jack now, cracking his massive knuckles.

"Yes.  Give me those papers you took off the police commandant this afternoon."

"You gonna make me?"

"I was going to challenge you to a wrestling match with the scorpions for it.  But you're a coward, so just forget it."

Veins were beginning to pop out on his neck.  He walked back over to the barrel and put his arm up in readiness.

Rurick sat on the stool next to Jack through whole exchange.  As Jack went to join the contest, Rurick placed a hand on his shoulder and whispered, "I sure hope you know what you're doing, kid."

Jack gave him a sly, 'I've got it all under control', nod and made his way to the table.

"My daddy always said, 'never trust a coward'.  Show me the files so I know you won't welch on the bet."

The man pulled the corner of the envelope out of his jacket just enough that everyone could see it.  Jack also noted with delight that it was stuffed full this time.

He sat down and put his arm up.  Jack's normally large hand was tiny in the clutches of the massive meat hook.

Everyone looked on in interest.  Everyone except Rurick, who looked away.

Jack smiled.  His plan was working perfectly.  The man's half-drunk pride at being called a coward and a cheat put him on the defensive.  He never stopped to think whether Jack was a coward and a cheat.

Jack quickly slipped his hand away and smacked the board with both fists.  The end nearest the bruiser shot straight up and cracked him in the jaw.  Meanwhile both live scorpions were catapulted high into the air.

Tough guys were suddenly reduced to squealing little girls as they whirled around to see where the horrible stinging creatures might have gone.  The bruiser swung at Jack, but he was far too slow.  The American had already ducked low and snatched the folder from his jacket.

Rurick saw it all and simply bolted.  Jack didn't stick around long and was only ten paces behind him in the street.  At his heels was the enraged Russian tough guy.

The commandant arrived at the storm drain first and had barely enough time to yank up the grate and drop down inside.  Jack dove for it headlong and nearly made off clean except for the huge hand that clamped around one of his ankles at the last instant.

There Jack hung, upside-down.  He looked down, which was up, and saw Rurick standing by helplessly.  He could only shrug at the predicament.  Jack saw something dangling from the belt of his stolen uniform and grabbed for it.

The handcuffs snapped around the large Russian's wrist and the other end around a convenient underground water line.

Jack braced his free leg against the underside of the manhole opening and shoved hard.  Except for landing on his head in several inches of sewage it was a brilliant idea.

The two tore along the inside of the sewer line.  They needn't have hurried.  The large brute made an excellent cork against anybody attempting to follow.

They reached the end where the effluent waste drained into a pristine mountain stream.  (Because that's always healthy.)  The stolen Humvee was parked nearby.

The two decided that the best plan was to make for the Southeast and sneak over the weakly-defended Mongolian border.  They made conversation as they drove.

"And so I suppose you're going to ask me to defect now?" said Igor Rurick.

"You said yourself I'm not CIA.  I'm an independent agent, so there's nobody in this car you can defect to."

"Ah yes.  Mr Jack, the brave independent agent."

"Do I really deserve all the sarcasm?  I thought I was pretty professional tonight.  What could you possibly say that I did wrong this time?"

"...says the man with a scorpion in his hair."

Jack froze.  Color drained from his face.

But that's impossible.  After all, he'd seen where both had went.  The one on the right flew off towards the corner and the left one... come to think of it, it had flown nearly straight up, hadn't it?

A bead of sweat trickled down his forehead.

Now that he meanioned it, he could feel something up there.  It moved a little with every bump.  Nah!  It was just his imagination playing tricks on him.  

There!  It moved again!

Jack couldn't stand it any longer.  He grabbed the rearview mirror and yanked it around so he could see his head.  It was a...

Slimy cabbage leaf from the sewer.

The perpetually sour expression on Rurick's face disappeared as he burst out laughing.

Jack punched him in the arm.

Then he laughed too.


~53~
Outer Mongolia

Dr Puttery answered the incoming call.  "Jack!  It's been awhile.  How's the Siberian night life?"

"I wouldn't know.  I'm in Mongolia now.  Any more clues from the documents I sent you?"

"Not much.  I plotted all the military movements they were looking at and there was no set pattern of focus.  Unless their plan is to invade all of Russia with that small force of theirs, they're collecting much more data than they need."

"And the letter?"

"You were right.  It must be encoded.  The words are utter nonsense otherwise.  But I haven't yet found the key clue to unlocking it."

"Stand by Doc.  I'll upload copies of Igor Rurick's files.  Maybe they'll help."

"You gave those to me last time Jack.  There wasn't much in there that we didn't already know."

"Yes, but this time I was able to get all of them.  Plus some top secret KGB info thrown in as a bonus."

"Rurick was holding out on us?  I knew it.  That dirty rotten..."

"Excuse me, sir!" said Igor indignantly.

Dr Puttery hesitated.  "Is there somebody with you Jack?"

"Yes.  Commandant Rurick is on speaker as well."

"W-why?  What happened?"

"Long story, but suffice it to say he learned a little too much and was on his way to chopping block."

"So now what Jack?"

"The commandant and I are about to take a scenic tour of the Gobi desert and then fly back to the USA.  That is, if you can get a fake passport together in time."

" 'Fake passport', indeed!"  Dr Puttery was shocked and offended at the insinuation.  "I'll have you know that every one of the passports I have given you was professionally printed by the fine Federal Government of these United States!

"Please now.  No more insults to my integrity.  Send me a snapshot of the man, the spelling of his name, and which US state he wishes to be born in."

Jack chuckled and shook his head at the man.  He turned to ask Rurick a question, but he was no longer paying attention.  Instead he gazed out the window and stroked his nose.

"Rurick?" Jack prompted.

"We're not leaving." he replied.

"Oh yes we are!  You're a hot ticket around this part of the world and the faster we get you in the air, the better!"

"Their play is going to be for the capitol in three days time.  They're going to demolish it."

Even Dr Puttery was surprised by that one.  "How could you know that?"

"Their leader, this 'Anton Ravenclaw', calls himself a Czar.  That's more than just ego.  It's also a rallying cry for fools with dreams of restoring the pomp and grandeur of a bygone age.  The way he hangs on to power is by exploiting that image.

"Another test, agent Jack:  If you were the Czar coming back to town and you saw an impostor government in charge, what would you do?"

"I'd wipe out their leadership so things could go back to 'normal'.  (Whatever that is.)  But why blow up Moscow?  Isn't that where he wants to rule from?"

Rurick nodded at Jack's initial answer but began shaking his head at the questions.  

Dr Puttery came to Jack's rescue.  "Moscow was not the capitol of Imperial Russia.  Saint Petersberg was.  Or, as it was renamed under communist rule, Leningrad."

"And the three days?"  At least Rurick had confidence in Dr Puttery.

"June 25th is the historical coronation day of Peter the Great, himself.  What better time to set up shop as the next Czar?"

Jack forced himself back into the conversation.  "That's all well and good guys, but now we've got to somehow stop their invasion convoy with nothing but the clothes on our back!"

If he was hoping to befuddle the two brilliant men, he was sadly mistaken.  They were already way ahead of him.

"There's only one thing left to do." said Dr Puttery.

"Precisely what I was thinking." agreed Rurick.

"What?  What?" asked poor, confused Jack.

The other two spoke in unison, "Bomb Moscow first!"


~54~
Day Two

Jane's pink phone was programmed to wake her gently each morning at 6:00AM by playing "Smellin' Coffee", by the Christian artist Chris Rice.  It was a great way to wake up.

Today, however, she was blasted out of bed by the concussion of a two ton torpedo exploding against the side of the building in which she lay.  It was a rude awakening, but one that the Generals had warned everybody might be coming.

She was already to the door by the time the last mists of dreamland cleared from her mind.  She pulled on her facemask along the way and she and her two companions hit the pink airlock goo like three pebbles hurled into a wall of mud.  

As soon as they reached open water, the team tried to get a handle on the situation.  Misty pointed out with some satisfaction that the Starfish was already hard at work, wrestling with the source of their rude wake-up call.

Adrenaline now racing, they joined the other soldiers already swimming down the town's main street in the direction of the enemy encampment.

Another torpedo blast erupted in front of them causing the columns of a beautiful Grecian building to topple in their path.  They nearly got entangled with the falling rubble but managed to dart down a side alley instead.  There, they were brought up short by an abandoned Sea Dragon minisub that must have somehow gotten lodged between the buildings in the prior day's battle.

Jane seemed to have been right about Wendell's idea of hiding in the city.  They were much safer yesterday when they were intermingled with the enemy.

"The enemy!  That's it!" said Jane aloud.  Her microphone picked it up.

"What's it?" Misty's voice came back.

"The Atlanticans don't know Landie technology, but we do.

"How's about you two track down some of the disabled dragon minisubs, starting with this one, and see how many you can get working again?

"If we can get our guys equipped with some decent hardware, then maybe we might stand a fighting chance!"

"Where will you be?"  Concern was evident in Misty's voice.  She didn't like the idea of Jane going off on her own.

"I just thought of something else.  I need to go find the Generals." Jane said hastily, and swam off.

Misty looked at Wendell, who shrugged back, "She looked like she knew what she was doing."  

It wasn't much comfort, but there was nothing for it.  They decided to occupy themselves with her idea of salvaging the abandoned minisub.

Misty, being by far the smaller and more nimble of the two, swam into the sub's open hatch to have a look inside.  Meanwhile, Wendell swam around its exterior to assess the damage.

Just like the strategy they'd developed back aboard the Starfish, there was something jammed in the propeller.  The right front side was also severely gouged from an impact with something.  It probably wouldn't hold air anymore, but that was okay.  The pilot could wear dive gear -- if they could get it running, that is.

"Here.  You might want this." said Misty's voice over the intercom.

Wendell looked up to see a large canvas bag emerge from the hatch and topple over the side.  It landed in the silt at his feet, sending up a blossoming cloud.  Reaching down, he unzipped it slightly to peek inside.  Shiny hand tools gleamed back at him.

"Perfect!  Good Job Misty!"

Making his way back to the stern he got a closer look at the propeller.  It was jammed tight against a large piece of driftwood.  He braced himself against the propeller shroud and positioned his hands for a good grip.  

1... 2... 3! And it came free with a hefty yank.  So far, so good.

"The prop is free.  Give it a try, Misty.  We'll see what happens."

The engine chugged to life and the propeller seemed to turn but the craft didn't budge.  Wendell held out the driftwood and touched the prop with it.  It immediately stopped spinning.  That told him what he needed to know.  "Might as well come out Misty.  Looks like a key's sheared."

"Is that bad?" she asked.

"Nah.  We've just gotta take the propeller off and find something to jam in there as a replacement key.

"In fact, it's probably a good thing that it did shear off.  Doing that protected the whole engine from blowing apart!"

"How long?" asked Jane.  She wasn't physically nearby, so they had forgotten that she was listening on the radio.

"Gimmie maybe... twenty minutes.  It won't be a permanent fix, but it'll get us in the action."


No sooner had the twenty minutes elapsed, but three Atlanticans rounded the corner with another broken Sea Dragon sub.

"Oh, I see how it is." complained Wendell.  He gave the propeller nut one last grunt with his wrench.  "Show 'em how to drive the thing Misty, while I look over the next one."


While all this was going on, Jane was busy relaying her insider information to the Generals.  In some ways it felt like a bit of a betrayal giving Landie secrets to Atlanticans.  She was able to quell the emotion by reminding herself that it wasn't Atlantis against Landie, but Atlantis against Sea Dragon -- and that made them allies, not enemies.

Upon cornering a group of Generals, she began, "Last night one of my friends suggested that we take cover in the city."

"We tried that.  It didn't work." one replied with a gruff snort.

"Of course not!  That's my point!  You need to send your divers out to attack the big subs instead!"

They all offered incredulous sounds at hearing this.  She'd need to explain further.

"Think about it.  While we're here in the city we are easy targets for their torpedoes, but if we can hug those big subs they won't dare shoot at us for fear of hitting one another.  In fact, if we snuggle in real tight against the hull, their minisubs will have trouble maneuvering close enough to get at us too."

"But what about their divers?" somebody asked.

It was a good question, but Jane had already come up with the answer on the swim over.  "We let 'em come after us!  Atlantican dive gear has a major advantage over Landie gear."  

'Landie'?  Jane noticed that she was starting to refer to her own people with that term -- and doing so in the third person to boot!  It felt like she was forsaking her own kind and becoming Atlantican instead.  

She shook herself back to the topic at hand.  "Their divers can't generate their own air like ours can, so they can only stay out, oh, about an hour and a half at a time.

"What we do is:  We lure them away from their parent sub.  Then, after about an hour or so, we park one of our hijacked minisubs between them and their entrance hatch.  When they turn back to go for a refill of air, they'll find themselves trapped outside, sandwiched between two bodies of troops, and without enough oxygen to fight back."

The Generals all fell in love with the tactic and patted one another on the back in the strange slow-motion way that underwater movement required.

Then somebody else spoke up.  "Suppose they don't want to come out?"

"Ah,  that's a great question.  Which leads me to a second weakness of their technology.  The 'conning tower' on you peoples' subs - like the Starfish," the generals shuddered at the name, but she carried on, "is nothing more than a slightly raised access hatch.  On the Landie subs though..."  There it was again.  She lost her train of thought and had to begin again.  "On the Landie subs, the conning tower was designed for surface warfare.  It houses all the communications equipment and its periscope is the only visual gear they have.  If our men get some tools and hack away at the towers they only have two choices:  either send divers out to retaliate or to fight the rest of the battle blind and alone."

The Generals now had a complete picture in their heads and swam off to reposition their troops for this bold new plan.

Jane swam back to her friends to lend a hand in the minisub project.


Aboard his nuclear sub, Enzo peered out the periscope.  A troop of fifty or sixty of the Atlanticans swam directly towards him.  Actually, it was a slight illusion.  They all swam directly towards the periscope his eyes were looking through, which was twenty feet above his head.

To Enzo it seemed a futile gesture, like a hornet attacking a bull.  Or, perhaps, more like a body of village insurgents charging a tank with clubs and pitchforks.  It seemed to be a last-ditch effort by a people who knew that their time was at an end, and wanted to go down in a final, heroic blaze of glory.

He smirked devilishly at the thought.  It wouldn't be long now.

His grin didn't last long as another member of his fleet across the city saw what was happening and decided to launch a torpedo at the Atlanticans.  It smacked hard into Enzo's sub and sent him reeling.

As he stood, he barked a command at his radio operator.  "Tell that idiot to stand down!"

A couple seconds later the man looked up.  "I'm getting no response.  Something's wrong with the radio."

"Keep trying." ordered Enzo.  Then a thought struck him and he grabbed for the periscope again.  He spun it around until he saw a huge leg.  It stretched away from the lens to the figure of a man sawing feverishly away at a radio antenna.
