The man looked up at a familiar face standing atop a large crate.  He was so shocked that he forgot to be enraged.  For a second, anyway.

"Blast 'im!"

Jane recognized the visitor as well.  Weird Wendell!  She nearly screamed as the hail of automatic weapons fire rang out.  She may have even done so, if there wasn't a hand suddenly clamped over mouth, preventing it.

She recognized the smell of the cologne on the arm.  Michael Moast!  He deftly cut the ropes holding her and they both swiftly faded away into the shadows.  Jane glanced over her shoulder once more.

Wendell stood atop the crate shuddering and convulsing as wave after wave of gunfire poured into him.  Strangely, for what must've amounted to thousands of direct hits, he showed no trace of blood.

Dr Puttery crouched on the warehouse floor.  One hand was cupped tight over his ear, trying to understand the broadcast over the reverberating gunfire.  "It's Michael.  They're away!"

He'd spoken to a second Wendell that stood convulsing as if being shot to pieces just the same as the other one.  Only this Wendell was well-protected and the only thing he was being shot with was a specialty 3D video camera of Puttery's own design.

"That's enough Wendell."  Dr Puttery switched off his homemade holo-projector.  "Let us bid a hasty retreat, sir!"

The first Wendell, having been thoroughly and completely killed a dozen times over, gave the thugs a pouty face and waved "bye bye" before disintegrating.

All at once they realized that they'd been had.  They didn't know what happened, but they did finally notice that they were missing both their target and their captive.

The Bible Study knew that they had two options for this plan:  To sneak away, or to run away.  They were so out-manned that Michael's tactical mind knew that they wouldn't be able to hide for long if the enemy decided to fan out.  Doing things the second way, he could at least control where they were all going to be... and set up a couple of surprises for them.

He also knew from earlier experience that Jane was about as fast a runner as Wendell, which was saying something.

Michael and Jane sped down the artificial corridor created by a wall of crates, when the long-legged boy himself appeared from a side alley.  Dr Puttery brought up the rear, clearly at a disadvantage compared to the more physically-fit members.

Hampered by their slowest runner, the baddies were now closing fast.  Every now and then they even let off a burst or two of gunfire.  Michael wished they'd do it more often.  It did nothing but waste bullets.

"Hear that?" called Michael over his shoulder to Jane.  "We've got a truck idling near the main entrance.  If we get separated, go for the truck!"

Coming to a fork, several of the killers split off to try and flank the fleeing group.  The side passage they chose turned out to be a dead end... with four very trapped Bible Study members at the end of it!

The enemy troop spread out in a line with rifles raised.  Fortunately the one-eyed leader was not with them.  If he was, he'd have had them gunned down on the spot.  Instead, a runner was dispatched to fetch him which bought the group some time.

Melinda Moast held a DX-10 electronically-assisted, self-targeting rifle.  It was a clever bit of technology that had been commissioned and rejected by the Army twice.  What made it so interesting was that it had fast-acting ultrasonic motors inside that could, once locked on, track a target by itself.  It was a gun designed to make any dumb Private into a sharpshooter, dropping shots within the diameter of a nickel at 100 yards.

Ms McCready had a plain old big gun with a scope.  She could easily out-shoot the DX-10.

More important than their choice of firearms, however, was the fact that they presently lay atop some boxes looking down on the bad guys who had Michael and the others cornered.

The line advanced on the four friends.  "Down!" shouted Michael.

McCready and Melinda laid into the killers like ducks in a shooting gallery.

The man they had sent as a runner stopped and turned around upon hearing the gunfire.  A small figure silently slid down a rope behind him.

A moment later he lay limp on the ground and Misty was running off in the direction of the main corridor with his gun.

The rest of the group rejoined her moments later, this time with Jane in the rear and Melinda and McCready running across the top of the row of boxes that formed the left wall.

Jane could see glimpses of the waiting truck from between crates now.

All at once the one-eyed man appeared from a side passage, running in the direction he'd heard the gunshots.

It happened so fast that Jane didn't have time to dodge the butt of the gun that crashed into her head.  The blow sent her rebounding off a crate, and crashing to her knees.

Slowly, she started to stand.

He laughed cruelly, never expecting the mighty uppercut that came next.  

"Just a little boxing move I learned." Jane explained to the crumpled body.  She heard feet pounding ahead and decided to pound her own.

She emerged into a clearing where a delivery truck stood with the back door open.  Everybody but Melinda was in back, beckoning and shouting for her to hurry up.  

She leapt aboard and Ms McCready banged twice on the sheet metal between her and the cab as a signal.  Melinda put the pedal down and the truck sped off towards the ramp to the lower level.

As it rounded the corner, the truck immediately slid sideways as gunfire peppered the cab.  Melinda ghasped in spite of herself.  

Jane looked up through the open cab door to see her clutching a bullet wound in the shoulder.  Jane dove forward through the narrow opening and grabbed the wheel.  "Floor it!"

Jane yanked the wheel hard over and the truck reeled around, heading back in the opposite direction.

"Puttery?  Any other way outta here?" she shouted over her shoulder.

"Yes." he replied.  "There are exit ramps at both ends."

Jane shoved Melinda over slightly and gained a better hold on the wheel.

"Back off the gas as much as you dare.  I'm gonna try and take us between the boxes."

Jane did just what she'd said.  The aisle was far too narrow for a truck though, and she lost a little paint and both side mirrors along the way.  But she did it nevertheless.

As they neared the spot where Jane had cheap-shotted the one-eyed man, all at once bullets started plinking against the metal of the truck.  Everybody got low.  Several of the bad guys blocked the truck's way.  

"More throttle!" shouted Jane.  Now it was even harder to drive, especially while ducking.

The men all dove for cover.  All made it in time, although one was helped out of the way by the left headlight.  A thud was heard on the roof but whatever hit it didn't stop the truck.  She was much more concerned with the large forklift she saw still parked up ahead in the place she'd been tied up.

"More throttle!  More throttle!" she shouted at Melinda in desperation.

They collided with the forklift with a mighty bang.  Its partially-raised tines stabbed in through the front of the truck and nearly skewered Jane's leg.  The forks lifted sideways and up but at least ripped free.  In the process they tore a severe chunk out of the passenger side of the cab and even took the seat with them.  

The crates were now whipping past the truck dangerously fast, but Jane couldn't bring herself to slow down now.  No doubt they were in a race against downstairs crew rushing to meet them at the other ramp. 

The bad guys were too late for once though.  Jane beat them to the bottom of the ramp and made straight for one of the many garage doors on the ground level.  They were free!


Michael collected Melinda and carried her in back to take care of her wound while Dr Puttery took over the driving.  He knew the way to a private airstrip that the Moasts regularly used.  

Jane was grateful for the driver change.  It gave her a chance to have a nervous breakdown.

She went back and sat next to Misty near the (still open) back door.

"Can't we shut that thing?  I feel like everybody's watching us."

"Well normally we could, except somebody did a little body damage to this fine automobile."

Jane smiled, but Misty continued.  "But as long as you don't feel conspicuous about the passenger's side being completely torn off, I can stand here in front of the door like this."

She stood in front of the opening with her sweater pulled out to the sides like a human flying squirrel.

Jane started to laugh, but it turned into a scream as two boots planted themselves in Misty's back, sending her sprawling face first across the cargo compartment and knocking Jane down in the process.  Misty's pilfered rifle clattered to one side.

Jane made a lunge for it but her leg was caught by the one-eyed man.  He pulled her roughly up to her feet.  The man held a pistol in one hand but instead of shooting, decided to get some payback first.  The right hook slammed into her face and sent her flying into the side of the truck.  A jagged bullet hole tore a long scratch in her arm as she shrank to the ground.

The one-eyed man spun to cover the rest of the group with his pistol.

Dr Puttery, a self-acknowledged expert in the field of physics, jerked the truck's wheel hard left.  The truck spun, tilted up on two wheels, and continued on its original trajectory only now on its side, sparking and grinding along the asphalt.

The people in back were all tumbled like marbles in clothes dryer.

Eventually friction triumphed over velocity and the truck scraped to a stop.

Jane staggered to a crouch.  She picked up Misty's rifle and was raising it when a crashing blow hit her from out of nowhere and sent her flailing backwards again.

The one-eyed man lowered the muzzle of his pistol.

Jane fell back on one elbow, closed her eyes, and squeezed the trigger.

There was an explosion of gunfire.

Then all was silent.

Jane opened her eyes cautiously and inspected herself.  She was covered in blood!  She jumped to her feet with a start.

She mentally asked her body if it had any holes that she wasn't born with.  Except for a dull ache due to the hard landing, and more scratches and bruises than she could count, it registered all-systems-go.

Jane looked down.  The one-eyed man lay in a spreading pool of his own blood.  It was a ghastly thing to behold.  

A strange thought hit her.  I never even knew his name.

"Good shot, dearie!" said Ms McCready.  It was meant not just as a compliment but also to bolster her courage.

Jane's pale face turned to her.  "I think I'll stick to shooting tractors."

Michael turned to the both of them.  "So that's what happened to it!" 


~65~
Return Flight

After the one-eyed henchman was carted off to the hospital under the loving supervision of the FBI, Melinda and Michael opted for a private charter plane to take them all back to Santa Augusta, California.  It wasn't as a luxury, but a necessity.

Although the bodies of the battered Bible Study members certainly appreciated the soft crushed leather seats, Michael was more adamant that the middle of a major airport surrounded by strangers was not a safe place for any of them to be.

According to McCready's underground contacts there was a high-priced contract out on the heads of several of the team members.  It was a dubious sort of compliment to Jane that the price listed for her assassination was high enough to make all but the top crime lords of New York close up shop for the day and try to make some real money.

The charter jet served another purpose beyond that as well.  There still needed to be an official debriefing and the middle of a commercial airliner filled with everybody and their airsick two-year-old was not a good place to hold it.

During the long cross-country flight they discussed all of their various individual adventures.  Most of it was the same as the recounting done aboard the Cheapskate, but this time Melinda was dutifully recording all of it for future reference.

When they came to the part about their recent narrow escape, it was Jane who asked the most pressing question of all:  "How do you think they found us?"

"That's easy.  Dr Kyle told 'em!" said Misty.  A lingering tinge of bitterness still clung to her voice.

"No, that doesn't explain it." said Dr Puttery.  "They couldn't have chosen to approach Dr Kyle out of sheer random chance.  They somehow must have already known that we were headed for New York.  If they'd been waiting in Key West I'd assume that they saw the name of the Cheapskate and found out her home port.  But nobody outside the Bible Study knew that we detoured to New York City instead!"

"I'm afeared ya don't understand how big this really is." said Ms McCready.  "They got control o' most of the underground and a good chuck o' the above ground.  They got spies everywhere!"  Given all of the things that she'd personally seen and heard, the situation made her uneasy.  ...And it took a lot to make Ms McCready uneasy!  Presently she felt like everyone else was underestimating the magnitude of it.

"I don't like to think of those creeps watching our every move like that." agreed Misty.  She looked around the cabin in apprehension.  "It feels like they're watching even now!"

It was a thought that chilled them all.  She was right, of course.  Though the team prevailed this time, they had also made themselves some powerful new enemies.  They'd have to be on guard in the future!

"No need to worry about any of that."  Michael was trying to lighten the mood.  "They won't bother us anymore.  Not now that they've succeeded in destroying all of the evidence."

Jane smiled.  "Well, maybe not all the evidence."

"Or any of it, really." she added.

Everybody turned to look at her with puzzled expressions on their faces.

In reply she held up her pink phone.

"No, we said they wanted 'the evidence', not that they wanted to order Chinese take-out." said Wendell.

Jane laughed.  She hit a button with her thumb.  A picture of the Statue of Liberty appeared on the screen.

"Backups." she said simply.  

"Sheesh!  And you call yourselves 'Photography Enthusiasts'!"


~66~
Over Kansas

The turbines of the small jet kept up their steady whine as Jane looked out the window, watching the scenery fly by.  It still felt strange and new to her since her first time in an airplane was the trip to Bermuda only a few weeks ago.  Even now the world below seemed so small - so surreal.  How people could sleep through this spectacle was beyond her.

A thought crossed her mind, and she said it to the air before she realized what she was asking.  "Do you suppose we're over Kansas now?"

She flushed and looked around the cabin to see if anybody heard it.  Everybody else had their eyes shut.

Jane felt relief, but only for a moment.

Ms McCready sat in the seat facing hers.  She was apparently awake, though spoke with closed eyes. "Yea, somethin' like that I should imagine."

The plane hummed on, and Jane was about to turn back to the window when McCready spoke again.  She was no dummy.  She'd only been thinking and looking for the right words to say.  She supposed that she could have called Jane out on the spot, just as Melinda did to her back aboard the Cheapskate.  

But, no, McCready had far too much of that kind of confrontation in her past.  Besides, her own reputation was presently less than spotless anyway.  She decided instead that it was best to let the Lord do His own fighting.  She had only to recite some (personally painful) verses from their recent Bible Study in Deuteronomy.
"This commandment which I command you today is not too hard for you, nor too distant.

It is not up in heaven, that you should say, “Who will go up for us to heaven, and bring it to us, and proclaim it to us, that we may do it?”

Neither is it beyond the sea, that you should say, “Who will go over the sea for us, and bring it to us, and proclaim it to us, that we may do it?”

But the word is very near to you, already in your mouth and in your heart, that you may do it."

If anyone else was listening it might sound like a laughably random thing to say,  but Jane knew exactly what she meant by it.  The verses cut her to the heart.  

Jane was hoping to somehow find a solution to her problems, first, by running away to California, and now on her grand adventure.  It was as if she could somehow prove herself or overcome her past.  But then, the problem never really was "out there" somewhere, was it?  It was all close at hand -- packed along with her on all of her travels, tucked carefully away inside her own rebellious heart.

Yet she carried one more thing along with her the whole time as well.  Not only the problem but also the solution to it.  This was the morally right solution - the solution that she dreaded so much - and therefore, the solution that she suppressed.

Now her defenses were crumbling as the battering ram of The Spirit played against them.

As if to confirm her thoughts, Ms McCready said, "My phone's in the cup holder if you're ready to call 'em."

She had to hand it to the lady.  Ms McCready's well-chosen words hit her deep, dark, problem square on the head without even opening her eyes.

Jane withdrew the phone and stared at it a long time.  Then she looked out the window at the green and yellow patchwork quilt sliding by.  How small it all seemed.

Then she looked at the phone again and took a deep breath.


~67~
Back to Normal

Blevens made his inspection pass around the sales floor.  It was how he preferred to manage.  Just enough involvement to make sure people were doing their job.  Not enough to scare away customers with his personality.

He breezed past the jewelry counter and hesitated.

"Jones!  What's that piece of trash?" he barked.

Jane looked around, stunned.  What was he talking about?

Blevens walked over and grabbed her wrist, twisting the arm roughly so that she could see the back of her hand.  It was the ring from Atlantis.

"Now you tell me:  How does it look to customers if they come in to buy something nice but see you wearing this piece of cheap costume crap?"

"Sorry Mister Blevens." she said.  Jane took the ring off and slipped it into her front pocket.

In reality, in intrinsic value alone the ring could be sold for enough to buy the department store, the mall it sat in, and still have plenty of change to spare.  More than that, it was beyond price as a historical artifact of epic proportions and was also considered the crown jewel of an entire nation.

"Don't let it happen again!" he threatened.  The troll retreated back into his cave.

"You were wearing it again?" observed Misty softly.

Jane shrugged.  "I don't know.  It's just...  Well...  How do you do it?"

" 'Do it'?  What do you mean?"

"I mean, how do you return back to normal as if nothing happened?  It's like there's this big exciting adventure one moment and then it's back to punching the time clock the next.  How in the world do you switch it on and off like that?"

"Oh that."  A smile crossed Misty's lips and a faint chuckle as if she were remembering her own initial experiences.  "The real trick you've got to learn is to control your emotions and not let them control you."

Misty thought about her words for a moment.  "It sounds cliché, I know.  But it is true!

"I dunno.  It's one of those things that's hard to explain 'till you see it in action."

"Please try.  I've been climbing the walls ever since I got back!"

"Okay. Well, basically your emotions tell your heart how to feel, right?"

"Sure."

"Well it takes a lot of time and practice, but the trick is to insert your mind in between the two.  'Take every thought captive', is the idea.  Whenever your emotions say something, you've got to stop, check it out, and then decide what you're going to let pass and what you're going to reject."

"Is that possible?"

"Yeah, but it takes time and a whole lot of practice.  It's painfully annoying at the start, but it does get more automatic as you get used to it." 

Misty looked at her friend more closely.  Concern was evident on her face.  "If you want, I can have Ms McCready get you some tranquilizers instead though.  I remember how hard it was for me till I got the hang of it."

Jane thought back and realized that the last of her own anxiety meds lie amid the wreckage of the Sea Gypsy, sunk fifty miles off the coast of Africa.  Even though she'd fought mercenaries with guns and big knives, been in countless explosions, and even fought wars, she hadn't even missed them up until now.  Strange.

"No thanks." Jane told her friend. "I like your way better.  I'll try that."

They were forced to end it there due to the arrival of a customer at the counter.  They both exchanged the smiles of best friends.


~68~ 
Finale

A candle was lighted.  The figure that held it was still not recognizable due to the long burlap hood it wore.

Slowly, and with a measured cadence, the figure strode down the marble hallway.  What was beyond was impossible to see as the only the radius immediately around it was lit by the flickering flame.

As it reached the proper place, a ceremonious left turn was performed.  A few more steps took it to where six other figures of various sizes stood in line behind an empty marble pedestal.

A tall figure stepped forward and offered some solemn lines.

"What.  Is your name?" it asked.

"Janet Jones." the figure answered solemnly.

"What.  Do you bring?" it asked again.

"I..." she began, but was interrupted.

"Why do we have to do this?" came Wendell's voice came from the back.

"Sssh!" ordered Misty's.

"I..." she began again, but was interrupted.  Again.

"I mean, it's just creepy." whispered Wendell.

"No it's not.  It's a cool, mysterious ceremony." whispered Misty back.

"Quiet, you two!" said the tall figure (Michael Moast) over his shoulder.

They didn't listen.  Instead, other voices joined in.  Soon the disagreement grew in intensity until Jane had to shout to be heard over them.

"A pot from the mythical isle of Atlantis!" she shouted.

She took two steps forward, like in the rehearsed ceremony, only rushed.  She placed two halves of an orange pot on the column - the one that Michael had purchased at auction long ago and the half that she had found on this adventure.

"Done!  Ceremony's over!" Michael threw up his hands more in surrender than anything else.

The group argued, chatted, and recounted their adventures as they slowly drifted off into the darkness in the general direction of the meeting room and whatever delicious treat Ms McCready was working on.

Jane stayed behind where she stood, looking at the artifact in the candle's glow.

"It was quite a wonderful adventure." said a voice by her side.

"No." replied Jane.  "It was an adventure with quite a wonderful friend."

She found and squeezed Misty's shoulder as the two walked back toward the Bible Study room.

 
The Bible Study that night was strange in how un-strange it was.  They proceeded to study the next section of Deuteronomy they were working on, answered a few thought-generating questions from a booklet, and generally enjoyed eachothers' company.

And, yes, Ms McCready's treats were excellent as usual.  Wendell proclaimed it to be so, and nobody would dare challenge his authority when it came to judging snacks. 

About a third of the way through the meeting, the familiar sound of the Moasts' doorbell was heard, along with the frantic, bloodthirsty barking of the poodle Thor.

Michael got up and went to answer it as the others chatted amongst themselves.

"Hey!  Look who it is!" bellowed Michael a few minutes later.

A few people turned.

"It's Jack!" exclaimed somebody.

Jane was slow in cutting off her chatting as she surely wouldn't recognize the group's old friend.  When her conversation partner stopped talking though, she turned around to greet the newcomer.

There, standing six foot two, with broad shoulders and a perfect smile was, she must admit, the most handsome man she'd ever seen.  Everything about him from his perfect hair to the brown leather flight jacket seemed like some kind of romantic dream guy come to life.

Jane decided that if she was the heroine of a romance novel, she'd be engaged to some boring accountant or something, and he'd waltz into her life and upset everything, and eventually carry her away off into the sunset on a snow white stallion.

It was a ridiculous schoolgirl thought and he blushed because of it.

"And who's this lovely creature?" asked Jack.

Did he just call her a 'lovely creature'?  Jane couldn't be sure.  Her mind was a little muddled between fantasy and reality at the moment.

"Oh, yes." said Michel Moast, "This is the newest member of the team.  Janet Jones."

The notion of 'love at first sight' is a hotly debated one.  The concept of a crush at first sight is undebatable.

The two shook hands politely.  Jane's hand shook well enough on its own.  A guy like this must have an equally dashing name, she mused.

"Jane, this is Jack Savage." said Michael Moast.

Yeah.  That'd do it.

"You remember Atlantis?" he goaded Jack.

"Ugh!" replied the other.

"Exactly.  Well, Jane here actually found it!"

"Well, it wasn't just me..." Jane began.  She felt her face redden.  She was eager and ready to dump all the credit on someone else to take the spotlight off herself.  As if she wasn't already self-conscious enough about her social skills, being the center of attention made things a thousand times worse.

Michael wasn't done yet.  "She also stopped a series of political assassinations, met the fabled Captain Nemo, helped to lead an underwater army, got blown up a couple times, punched out a bad guy, prevented a potential chemical weapons attack by terrorists, and stopped an ancient artifact smuggling ring too."

Jack looked into Michael's eyes for some sign of pulling his leg.  There was none.  It was all true.

As it was, Jane felt like she was going to pass out from embarrassment.  Though she was uncomfortable with this much praise, her one consolation was that everyone who knew the secret of the ring also understood the importance of obeying the former Queen's last wishes and keeping it a secret.  In fact, not even Dr Puttery knew about it.  

She (perhaps, rudely) left the conversation hanging and wandered off in the direction of the kitchen to see if Ms McCready had any more punch.

After a time, the rest of the group began to gravitate back to the comfortable couches and settled in again for the remainder of the Bible Study.  When the gathering reached critical mass, Michael resumed the formal discussion. 

Upon hearing it starting up again, Ms McCready drifted back toward the couches and Jane had no choice but to follow.  She'd have rather run away.  Terrorists were one thing.  Boys were another.

At that moment, Janet Jones, beet red and heart aflutter, reacted in the same way any single girl like her would react when meeting a handsome and eligible stranger like the stunning Jack Savage.

She tripped over her own feet and spilled punch on him.

THE END


Epilogue

What of the girl from Atlantis of old?

After a long sojourn in North Africa, the once proud merchant's daughter found herself drifting from strange land to strange land.  None of the places she went was home.  And so she lived as an unwilling exile to the end of her days in near complete poverty.  Her children, lived in poverty not quite so dire, yet from place to place they still wandered.  Always a stranger and never a citizen; that was their lot.

Twenty more long, grueling generations the cycle of poverty kept up until all at once the chain was broken.  One Admunsen T. Grey, last surviving heir, broke free from his lifelong struggle with want when he suddenly struck it rich in a last all-or-nothing trip the Klondike gold fields.

From then on, the family tradition of poverty was broken, but not the chain of bitterness toward the many nations of the world.  In many ways, great wealth only compounded their great misery, for the people of the world all posessed something that, even despite their new financial state, the Greys could never have:  A homeland!

The final chapter in the family's sad history was finally begun one night a hundred years later, in Los Angeles, California during a heavy downpour.

After a horribly bad premier screening, of a horribly bad film, a horribly bad young actress named Vivian Grey found herself outside the theater exit, shouting horrible things at the rest of the cast.  The words themselves, unprintable.

Unknown to her, in the shadows of a nearby alley, a mysterious figure lurked.  He heard all the anger.  He heard all the ego.  He heard all the menace.  He heard it all... and he smiled!

The shouting woman gave a tuxedo'd director a hard shove and went to storm off.  Another man tried to stop her, but wound up holding only a sequened shrug, which she'd shed as easily a reptile loses her tail.

Vivian stormed down the vacant sidewalk, too angry to feel cold.   The streetlights grew farther and farther apart, their yellow-orange light glinting off the streaks of rain like a thousand shooting stars.

Shooting stars -- those things that shine so bright, and burn out in an instant. she thought, bitterly.

Behind her, a car slowed to a crawl along the curb, rolling only fast enough to keep pace with her walking.  

Vivian stopped at the sound.  Turning, she saw a black limousine.

"Daimler!  You worthless hack of a Director!" she shouted.  Running to the back door, she kicked it, and punched it, and cussed at it with all new kinds of profanity.

The window cracked on its seal and began to roll down as she continued to rant over the whine of the motor and the clatter of the rain.  "What gives you the right to follow--!??" but then she stopped.

The man, whose eyes alone were seen in the dim illumination of the car's interior, was clearly not her Director.

With a horrid, gravelly, voice, the man spoke.  "Vivian Grey," he croaked, "I am in need of an actress."

"Yeah?" she said, cocking a hip.

"I got a recent, um, 'vacancy' in my organization.  I'm in need of somebody with the right look.  Somebody who can do... let's just say, 'whatever is necessary'.  I'm in need of, in short, an Empress."  He drew the last word out, and let it hang on the air like a piece of bait on a hook.

Vivian cocked an eyebrow.  With all her pent-up vanity, she liked the sound of that. "Okay, old man.  It looks like I'm suddenly in need of work anyway, so I'll bite.  But before I do, just who exactly am I talkin' to?"

"Oh, me?" he said in mock surprise.  Then he laughed (which sounded like more of a coughing wheeze).  "You just call me... Draco."
